## 2. Master authority and scheduler contract

### 2.1 Master edit — one product promise, in both places

Replace the complete urgent-recovery clause at master §A2 line 51 and its verbatim Entry 8 repetition at line 436. Do not edit only one occurrence. The replacement sentence is:

> When the latest safe date has passed, surface the urgent-recovery card on the next successful scheduler evaluation under the frozen launch-capacity envelope, and never create a backdated reminder. Phase 2 measures a 15-minute delivery objective under that envelope; this is not a contractual wall-clock bound. A contractual bound requires Phase 2b — Deadline-Scheduled Wake.

Add `A12-13 Phase 2 urgent-delivery timing` recording that exact product decision and date. P1-I is **not superseded**: its server-owned threshold projection, independent scan, wake, intent, replay, routing, and race mechanism remains mandatory. Only the impossible cron wall-clock promise changes.

### 2.2 B1/D1 — schedule, lease, health, capacity, and monitoring

The production function configuration is exact:

```text
region:         us-central1
schedule:       */5 * * * *
timeZone:       Etc/UTC
timeoutSeconds: 270
memory:         512MiB
maxInstances:   1
concurrency:    1
retryCount:     0
```

The handler accepts only the scheduler event delivered by this trigger and requires its `scheduleTime`. `runOrdinal = floor(parseRFC3339(event.scheduleTime).epochSeconds / 300)`. Arrival time and process-local time never select an ordinal. A missing or malformed `scheduleTime` stops before the lease or scheduler state is written. The schedule is disabled during initialization. One initialization transaction requires the lease absent and `schedulerHealth` absent, reads the trigger-state document at server `readTime = R`, and writes `activatedOrdinal = floor(R.epochSeconds / 300) + 1`, `lastStartedOrdinal = activatedOrdinal - 1`, and required `recentRuns:[]`, with `lastCompletedOrdinal` and `dueObservation` absent. Every arithmetic result must be a safe nonnegative integer. The transaction must commit and the schedule must be enabled before the exact UTC boundary represented by `activatedOrdinal`; if operational tooling misses that boundary, it does not rewrite the floor—the first delivered later ordinal intentionally records the intervening gap. A present/malformed health map, nonabsent lease, missing/nonempty initialization array, underflow/overflow, active schedule during initialization, or inability to prove enablement state blocks rollout.

The singleton lease path is literal `phase1System/dispositionTriggerLease`; the scheduler-state path is literal `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState`; the cross-fence path is literal `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/migrationLeases/legacyOversizeMigrationV1`. The scheduler acquisition transaction reads all three literal documents and uses the trigger-state snapshot's server `readTime` as `leaseNow`; set `startedAt = runNow = leaseNow`. A present cross-fence document—valid or malformed—refuses before heartbeat, state, scheduler-lease, cursor, refusal, alert, or candidate mutation. `ownerToken` is a pregenerated lowercase UUID and `expiresAt == leaseNow + 270 seconds`. The same `leaseNow` compares prior scheduler-lease expiry; process wall time is used only for monotonic admission elapsed time. A nonexpired scheduler lease refuses another owner; an expired scheduler lease is atomically replaced in that same state-plus-scheduler-lease-plus-cross-fence acquisition transaction. A live owner deletes only its matching scheduler lease in `finally`; process death relies on expiry. `scheduledAt` is the exact Firestore Timestamp parsed from `event.scheduleTime`; `observationNow = observedAt = runNow`, and both threshold queries use that frozen cutoff. `completedAt` is the server `readTime` from the completion transaction's trigger-state read. No process `Date` supplies a persisted or candidate-comparison time. Rules deny every client operation at both lease paths; the acquisition read-count/budget constructor and its independent oracle include all three documents. Fixtures cover both literal paths, simultaneous state/scheduler-lease/cross-fence reads, a present valid or malformed migration cross-fence with zero scheduler mutation, and owner/other/anonymous get/list/create/update/delete denial.

The lease tuple is a fencing token. Every scheduler-owned Firestore mutation after acquisition—including phase-0 source/retry/quarantine/high-water, wake/intent/task, refusal create/update/delete, alert, cursor create/update/delete, durable metric state, heartbeat, and completion—first reads the literal lease document in that same transaction and requires exact equality with its captured `{schemaVersion:1,runOrdinal,ownerToken,startedAt,expiresAt}` plus `expiresAt >` that lease read's server `readTime`; its read version participates in the commit. Mismatch or an already-expired read writes nothing and makes that run incomplete. Read-only queries only nominate candidates; their eventual write transaction performs this fence. A transaction whose A lease read serializes before B's expiry takeover may finish idempotently before B's lease replacement and B then observes its result. If B's replacement serializes first, A's lease read/version conflicts, retry sees tuple mismatch, and A writes nothing. No claim is made that a pre-expiry read imposes a commit-time bound, and no mutation may begin from a lease read at or after expiry. Completion itself is fenced; failure to commit it leaves the heartbeat running and the run unsuccessful.

Trigger state gains:

```text
schedulerHealth:{
  schemaVersion:1,
  activatedOrdinal,
  lastStartedOrdinal,
  lastCompletedOrdinal?,
  recentRuns:[
    {runOrdinal,scheduledAt,startedAt,state:"running"} |
    {runOrdinal,scheduledAt,startedAt,state:"completed",completedAt,
     thresholdWakeLatency:({count:0} |
       {count:<positive>,p50Seconds,p95Seconds,maxSeconds})}
  ],
  dueObservation?
}
```

`recentRuns` is unique, strictly ordinal-ascending, and retains the newest four accepted ordinals. Deployment writes the initialized fields before enabling the schedule. At acquisition let `ordinalFloor = max(lastStartedOrdinal, lastCompletedOrdinal ?? activatedOrdinal - 1)` and require `runOrdinal > ordinalFloor`. Duplicate/out-of-order delivery writes no lease, health, cursor, refusal, or alert. Accepted acquisition appends that ordinal's `running` heartbeat and advances `lastStartedOrdinal`. Only after all admitted candidates have settled and all page checkpoints have committed does one transaction change that heartbeat to `completed` and advance `lastCompletedOrdinal`. Only after that fenced completion transaction commits, emit structured code `DISPOSITION_SCHEDULER_COMPLETED` with `runOrdinal` and no other dynamic field; log-based metric `phase2/disposition_scheduler_completed_count` increments from that event. Duplicate/out-of-order delivery, lease refusal, a running-only heartbeat, and every incomplete evaluation emit no completion event/metric. Any query, candidate, refusal-bookkeeping, checkpoint, metric-state, or completion-write failure leaves the heartbeat running and leaves `lastCompletedOrdinal` unchanged.

Running heartbeats forbid `completedAt` and `thresholdWakeLatency`; completed heartbeats require both. For every threshold path whose wake transaction committed successfully in that run, the completed-evaluation delivery-latency sample is `max(0,floor((completedAt-threshold_at)/1000))` whole seconds, using the completion transaction's already-frozen server `completedAt`. That same completion transaction computes the run's sample set from the run-local successfully committed threshold paths and their stored `threshold_at` values; if completion does not commit, neither samples nor metric state are stored. Sort ascending; `p50` and `p95` are nearest-rank values at ranks `ceil(.50*n)` and `ceil(.95*n)`. Exact `{count:0}` forbids all three quantiles; a positive safe count requires all three. These are completed-evaluation delivery telemetry, not a per-write commit timestamp and not proof that no row remained unwoken.

At threshold-lane start, run two uncursored global observations over this exact candidate superset:

```text
collectionGroup("tasks")
  .where("thresholdProjection.state", "==", "armed")
  .where("thresholdProjection.threshold_at", "<=", observationNow)
```

The first obtains aggregate count. The second uses the same predicates plus `orderBy(thresholdProjection.threshold_at ASC, documentId ASC)` and `limit(1)`. They retain separate read times and are not represented as one atomic snapshot. Store exact:

```text
dueObservation:{
  schemaVersion:1,observedAt,countReadTime,oldestReadTime,
  armedDueCandidateCount,
  oldestCandidatePath?,oldestThresholdAt?,oldestDueAgeSeconds
}
```

The aggregate count is read first and the oldest query second. Count zero requires the three oldest-candidate members absent and age `0`; positive count requires all three and `oldestDueAgeSeconds = max(0,floor((observedAt-oldestThresholdAt)/1000))`. A zero/present or positive/absent pair is exact `OBSERVATION_RACE`: write no due observation/alert/cursor/completion, admit no threshold candidate, and leave the heartbeat running; no impossible map is synthesized and no unbounded retry occurs. The names say *candidate*: malformed or stale projections can create a conservative false positive, but every valid overdue threshold row is in the superset. Either query failure has the same abort behavior.

Server-only Phase-2b condition slots live at `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/phase2bAlerts/{alertId}`. Their count is bounded to eleven: four global slots and one fairness slot for each of the seven exact refusal-lane tokens. The exact union is:

```text
{schemaVersion:1,condition:"TWO_MISSED_COMPLETIONS",
 firstObservedAt,lastObservedAt,firstObservedOrdinal,lastObservedOrdinal,
 occurrenceCount,effectiveLastCompletedOrdinal,requiredCompletedOrdinal}

{schemaVersion:1,condition:"OLDEST_DUE_OVER_900_SECONDS",
 firstObservedAt,lastObservedAt,firstObservedOrdinal,lastObservedOrdinal,
 occurrenceCount,candidatePath,thresholdAt,ageSeconds,
 armedDueCandidateCount}

{schemaVersion:1,condition:"THRESHOLD_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED",
 firstObservedAt,lastObservedAt,firstObservedOrdinal,lastObservedOrdinal,
 occurrenceCount,armedDueCandidateCount,admissionCapacity}

{schemaVersion:1,condition:"THRESHOLD_SCAN_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED",
 firstObservedAt,lastObservedAt,firstObservedOrdinal,lastObservedOrdinal,
 occurrenceCount,thresholdCutoff,candidateCountLowerBound,admissionCapacity}

{schemaVersion:1,condition:"FAIRNESS_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED",lane,
 firstObservedAt,lastObservedAt,firstObservedOrdinal,lastObservedOrdinal,
 occurrenceCount,eligibleRefusalCount,refusalCapacity}
```

The global IDs are literal `p2b1_two_missed_completions`, `p2b1_oldest_due_over_900_seconds`, `p2b1_threshold_capacity_exceeded`, and `p2b1_threshold_scan_capacity_exceeded`; a fairness ID is `"p2b1_fairness_capacity_exceeded_" + lane`, where `lane` is one of the seven already-frozen tokens. On first observation, first/last time and ordinal are equal and `occurrenceCount = 1`. A later accepted ordinal preserves both first members, replaces every condition-payload member with the latest observation, advances both last members, and safely increments the count. A byte-identical same-ordinal retry is a no-op; a same-ordinal disagreement, wrong condition/lane at a literal slot, nonmonotonic ordinal, unsafe count, or twelfth slot is a systemic invariant that writes nothing and leaves the evaluation incomplete. Once that condition's complete observation phase succeeds and proves it absent, the same lease-fenced evaluation deletes its slot; an incomplete run never clears an unobserved condition. Recurrence after deletion starts again at one. Each create/update emits one fixed-code structured condition event after commit; a log-based metric and Cloud Monitoring policy page named owner S3 on every active observation. The pre-ship checklist records all four global and seven lane-slot tests, metric/policy resource IDs, and a successful notification. `OLDEST_DUE_OVER_900_SECONDS` is observed whenever age is greater than 900, independently of wake-latency samples.

Before accepting ordinal `o`, let `effectiveLast = lastCompletedOrdinal ?? activatedOrdinal - 1` and `missingBeforeThisRun = o - effectiveLast - 1`. At two or more, observe/update the missed-completion slot before candidate work with `effectiveLastCompletedOrdinal = effectiveLast` and `requiredCompletedOrdinal = o - 2`; zero or one deletes a prior slot only after that gap observation succeeds. After the threshold observations and eligible-refusal aggregate are read, define `catchUp = (lastCompletedOrdinal is absent) || (missingBeforeThisRun >= 1) || (eligibleThresholdRefusalCount > 0) || (oldestCandidatePath is present && oldestDueAgeSeconds > 300)`. Define `thresholdAdmissionCapacity = catchUp ? 400 : 200`; “contiguous” means exactly `catchUp == false`. `THRESHOLD_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED` is active iff `armedDueCandidateCount > thresholdAdmissionCapacity`; the run is not described as meeting the objective in that case. Phase 0 remains 100 selected events. Each of the six ordinary lanes admits 50 total fresh-plus-refusal candidates. The threshold lane admits at most `thresholdAdmissionCapacity`. No implementation may have eight 50-row pages resident at once.

The named frozen launch-capacity envelope is exact: at most 200 threshold-superset candidates are due at any contiguous evaluation cutoff; at most 200 additional candidates become due in any half-open 300-second ordinal; after one incomplete/missing evaluation, the next cutoff contains at most 400; and each user has at most 500 armed projections. All counted projections satisfy the frozen producer schema/index contract. The objective applies only to a completed evaluation whose cutoff observation is within its applicable 200/400 ceiling and in which each valid due candidate settles to wake or definitive no-op; a refusal, timeout, failed query/write, or capacity alert makes that evaluation unsuccessful for the objective. The envelope is an operational launch assumption measured by H57, not a claim about arbitrary population.

The persisted phase-0 equation is `select_n = 100`, query `limit(101)`, and checkpoint the 100th examined cursor iff row 101 exists. Each ordinary lane instead uses `select_n = 50`, query `limit(51)`, and checkpoints the 50th iff row 51 exists. Otherwise each deletes only its own cursor. Threshold also pages 50/51 but has **no persisted cursor**. It fixes `thresholdCutoff = observationNow`, scans pages sequentially with a run-local cursor, then restarts uncursored passes. Fixed-point success requires one exhaustive pass that observes no eligible distinct path/fingerprint outside the run-local settled/refusal-backed set; “admitted zero” is not equivalent. If admission capacity has been consumed, scanning continues without mutation until either that exhaustive absence is proved or the first additional distinct eligible candidate is observed. The latter observes `THRESHOLD_SCAN_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED` with `candidateCountLowerBound = thresholdAdmissionCapacity + 1`, writes no candidate/cursor/completion after that finding, and makes the run unsuccessful; it never reuses the aggregate count as if both reads shared a snapshot. Only a true exhaustive fixed-point pass deletes a prior scan-capacity slot and permits threshold completion. Deadline/timeout before it leaves the run incomplete; the next run starts uncursored. Pages are fetched and settled sequentially with candidate concurrency at most 10. Phase order is phase 0 → threshold → the six ordinary lanes. Admission deadlines may stop starting new waves, but an admitted transaction is awaited; no prose assumes transaction cancellation or a settle-time bound.

The trigger-state cursor namespace permits exactly seven optional keys: `event_envelope_prepass` plus one for each of the six ordinary lanes. Each present value has the frozen exact cursor-map shape; absence of any or all keys means start-of-range. A `threshold_attention` cursor and every other key are forbidden. Because Phase 2 is unbuilt, deployment/startup encountering either is a specification contradiction and stops; it never deletes or ignores the member. In the replacement §5.2 text, replace the old count “eight” with “seven” and delete the threshold-cursor bullet. Fixtures cover all-absent, each singleton, all seven present, and rejection of an old threshold cursor or any unknown key.

The only total-absence claim comes from a deployed Cloud Monitoring dead-man policy on `phase2/disposition_scheduler_completed_count`: no metric increment for ten consecutive minutes pages named owner S3. Generic platform invocation success is not an input, so a duplicate/out-of-order/lease-refused delivery cannot mask a missing completion. The pre-ship checklist records both log-based metric and alert-policy resource IDs plus a successful test notification. Firestore heartbeat data is not claimed to detect the simultaneous absence of the function and its own observer. The timing arithmetic—570 seconds for an on-time run and 870 seconds after one killed run—is capacity rationale only, not a promise.

Mandatory fixtures: initialization just before/on an exact five-minute boundary, delayed enable, first delivery at `activatedOrdinal`, `+1`, and `+2`, arithmetic overflow, activation/no run; first, contiguous, one-gap, two-gap, and three-gap ordinals; duplicate, out-of-order, and lease-refused deliveries with zero completion metric; death before/after heartbeat, after candidate settlement, before completion, and after completion commit/before metric emission; lease expiry and atomic replacement; takeover immediately before every mutation-class commit; A transaction starting from a live pre-expiry read while B serializes takeover before A commit; transaction retry after B takeover proving tuple mismatch/zero write; no mutation beginning from an expired read; threshold row formerly behind a persisted cursor; insertion/movement behind a run-local cursor caught by the next pass; aggregate count exactly 200/400 followed by a late cutoff-eligible distinct candidate proving the corresponding scan-capacity slot, no checkpoint/completion, and next-run replay; malformed projection inclusion; an eligible 20-minute-old row with no wake sample; independent observation read times; every launch-envelope boundary; exact 200/201 and 400/401 aggregate capacity; nearest-rank metrics including zero samples; sequential page residency; ordinary final-checkpoint-before-completion; fixed-point-before-threshold-completion; 10,000 persistent-condition ordinals retaining at most eleven slots; same-ordinal replay/disagreement; resolve/recur/count-overflow/takeover for every slot shape; and recorded/tested completion and condition metrics/policies including a no-op platform-success masking test.

### 2.3 D2/D3/D5 — page, projection, and admission invariants

- Phase 0 uses exact-100/101 fixtures: 100 ends with its cursor absent and 101 persists the 100th before the next page. Ordinary lanes use exact-50/51 with the analogous 50th cursor. Threshold exact-50/51 instead proves run-local paging, a second uncursored fixed-point pass, and no threshold cursor read/write in trigger state.
- Each lane's `select()` mask is exactly its filter fields, order fields, `task_generation_epoch`, and `task_instance_id`; phase 0 uses its separately frozen event identity. A candidate transaction reads the complete document by path and compares the query-captured generation/instance/update identity before mutation.
- Admission, never cancellation, controls runtime. Before each concurrency-10 wave, compare monotonic elapsed time to the frozen deadline from run start: phase 0 at 40 seconds; threshold at 130; the six ordinary lanes, in frozen order, at 142, 154, 166, 178, 190, and 202. A missed deadline starts no new wave and awaits/bookkeeps admitted work. If any selected phase-0 row was not admitted, or any selected ordinary row was neither admitted nor classified by one of the durable-refusal-backed branches below, that lane performs no cursor create/update/delete for the page; the next run re-queries it and idempotently settles already-processed rows. Threshold is already run-local and leaves the run incomplete unless its fixed-point pass finishes. No clause assumes cancellation or a settle bound. The function timeout remains the only hard runtime stop, so a killed run stays incomplete and its work replays idempotently. Fixtures stop after each possible admitted/classified prefix of waves and prove zero premature cursor movement and zero skipped row.

### 2.4 D4 — durable refusal queue, no eviction

The bounded in-document refusal map and every eviction rule are withdrawn. Trigger-lane refusal authority is one server-only document per `(uid,lane,candidatePath)`:

```text
users/{uid}/schedulerRefusals/{refusalId}

refusalId = "srf1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({
  domain:"scheduler_refusal.v1",lane,candidate_path:candidatePath
})))
```

The lane union is exactly `threshold_attention|date_snoozed_deferred|date_inprogress_user_action|date_matching_waiting|event_snoozed_deferred|event_inprogress_user_action|event_matching_waiting`. Phase 0 retains its own retry/quarantine mechanism and never creates these records.

The exact record is:

```text
{
  schemaVersion:1,lane,candidatePath,candidateFingerprint,
  firstRefusedOrdinal,lastAttemptOrdinal,nextEligibleOrdinal,
  refusalCount,saturated,reasonCode,firstRefusedAt,lastRefusedAt
}
```

`reasonCode` is exactly `VALIDATION_REFUSAL|AUTHORITY_REFUSAL|IDENTITY_RACE|TRANSACTION_RETRY_EXHAUSTED`. `candidateFingerprint` is SHA-256 of `TaskCanonicalV1({lane,candidate_path,document_update_time,selected_identity})`; `selected_identity` is the lane's complete D3 field-mask result with explicit missing tags, so malformed candidates remain fingerprintable. On create, or on a changed fingerprint or reason, write `firstRefusedOrdinal = lastAttemptOrdinal = currentOrdinal`, `firstRefusedAt = lastRefusedAt = runNow`, `refusalCount = 1`, `saturated = false`, and `nextEligibleOrdinal = currentOrdinal + 1`. On a same-fingerprint/same-reason refusal, preserve `firstRefusedOrdinal` and `firstRefusedAt`, set `lastAttemptOrdinal = currentOrdinal` and `lastRefusedAt = runNow`, set `refusalCount = min(8,old.refusalCount + 1)`, `saturated = (refusalCount == 8)`, and `nextEligibleOrdinal = currentOrdinal + min(2^(refusalCount-1),16)`. All ordinal additions must remain safe. A valid row requires `1 <= refusalCount <= 8`, saturated iff count is eight, `firstRefusedOrdinal <= lastAttemptOrdinal < nextEligibleOrdinal`, and `firstRefusedAt <= lastRefusedAt`; derived-ID/path/lane disagreement or any invariant violation fails closed with no candidate mutation or cursor advance. Saturation never permits eviction.

An ordinary cursor may pass a candidate only after: (1) successful mutation/definitive no-op with matching-refusal deletion; (2) exact refusal create/update after rereading candidate identity; (3) candidate absence with obsolete-refusal deletion; or (4) a lease-fenced reread proves an extant deterministic refusal has exact ID/content and the same candidate fingerprint, whether it is ineligible or eligible beyond this run's selected retry prefix. Branch (4) mutates neither task nor refusal; the surviving refusal is the independent retry authority after the fresh-query cursor passes. A stale-fingerprint, malformed, wrong-ID, or wrong-content refusal is never branch (4). Refusal classification/bookkeeping/checkpoint failure aborts the lane before checkpoint. Thus a kill before durable classification leaves the cursor behind the candidate; a kill after branches (1)–(3) leaves their committed result, and a kill after branch (4) but before checkpoint leaves the still-queryable refusal plus a replayed fresh row.

Eligible retry query:

```text
collectionGroup("schedulerRefusals")
  .where("lane","==",lane)
  .where("nextEligibleOrdinal","<=",currentOrdinal)
  .orderBy("nextEligibleOrdinal","asc")
  .orderBy("firstRefusedOrdinal","asc")
  .orderBy("lastAttemptOrdinal","asc")
  .orderBy(documentId(),"asc")
```

Admission is a refusal-first deduplicated union keyed by exact `(lane,candidatePath,candidateFingerprint)`. Before a fresh-query candidate can consume a slot, its transaction reads the deterministic refusal path. A same-fingerprint refusal excludes that candidate from fresh admission at every ordinal: if eligible and selected it consumes exactly one retry slot; if ineligible or eligible beyond this run's cap it is skipped under its durable backoff and consumes no slot. A stale-fingerprint refusal is deleted only after a full candidate reread; the candidate may then consume one fresh slot in that same transaction. Refusal-ID/content disagreement fails closed. A run-local admitted set prevents one path/fingerprint from appearing in both sources or twice across threshold fixed-point passes.

Each ordinary lane reserves 10 of its 50 admissions for eligible refusals; the threshold lane reserves half its applicable ceiling. First obtain only the aggregate eligible count. Then fetch the ordered query in run-local 50/51 pages, never materialize the aggregate population, and stop once that lane's total admission ceiling has been collected; there is no persisted refusal cursor. Above 200 creates `FAIRNESS_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED`, makes the evaluation unsuccessful for the launch objective, but still processes only the bounded ordered prefix. Determine demand from that prefix and ordered fresh candidates after the exclusion above; unused reserved slots transfer to the other source, total never exceeds the lane ceiling, and source-internal order is unchanged. Retry rereads refusal and candidate: missing/changed deletes obsolete authority, success/definitive no-op deletes it atomically with the result, and another listed refusal updates it. Account deletion removes the user-root collection. Rules deny every client read/write. §7.4 gains exactly the composite used above; H57 reports total, eligible, saturated, oldest, and alerts by lane without task payloads.

Fixtures include 64/65/201/1,000 records with zero eviction and bounded 50/51 reads; continuous new arrivals while a fixed older refusal is retried; count `7→8→8`; 16-ordinal saturated retry; fingerprint/reason reset; same-fingerprint ineligible and eligible refusals visible in fresh query but never admitted fresh; 51 early ineligible matching refusals followed by fresh rows, proving branch (4) advances across durable authority and later fresh rows are admitted; an eligible refusal beyond the selected retry prefix remaining reachable by its independent query after cursor advance; crash immediately before/after the checkpoint for branch (4); stale refusal cleanup then one fresh admission; retry/fresh duplicate across threshold passes; both source transfer directions; all four reason codes; failure before bookkeeping; crash after bookkeeping; success/missing/change cleanup; hash collision; exact 200/201 alert behavior; rules/index parity; reset-created orphan eventually deleted when its missing task is retried; and recursive account deletion.

### 2.5 D6–D9/D11 — encoder, size, precedence, and regeneration

`OriginalEventBytesV1` keeps finite doubles as sixteen lowercase IEEE-754 hex digits, including negative zero, and adds only `nan`, `inf`, and `-inf` for nonfinite doubles. The sixteen digits are the bytes produced by `DataView.setFloat64(0,value,false)` read from offset 0 through 7 and hex-encoded in that order (network/big-endian); production and oracle fixtures are `1.0→3ff0000000000000`, `-0→8000000000000000`, and least positive subnormal `→0000000000000001`. Predicates run in this exact order: `null|boolean|string|number` primitive union; `v instanceof Date`; `v instanceof Timestamp`, `GeoPoint`, `DocumentReference`, or `VectorValue` using the pinned `@google-cloud/firestore` constructor identities; `Buffer.isBuffer(v)`; `Array.isArray(v)`; plain map iff `Object.getPrototypeOf(v) === Object.prototype || Object.getPrototypeOf(v) === null`; `NON_PLAIN_OBJECT` for every remaining nonnull object; otherwise `UNSUPPORTED_RUNTIME_TYPE`. No server `Bytes` constructor or generic Uint8Array branch exists. Malformed recognized instances and cycles are infrastructure invariants, not quarantine tokens. `PROTOTYPE_MISMATCH` does not exist.

`SOURCE_UNENCODABLE` is first-occurrence quarantine with digest:

```text
SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({
  domain:"unencodable_source.v1",source_path,reason_token,update_time
}))
```

Name that hash `unencodableSourceDigest` and set `quarantineId = "qevu1_" + first40(unencodableSourceDigest)`. The reason-token union is exactly `NON_PLAIN_OBJECT|UNSUPPORTED_RUNTIME_TYPE`. Cycles and malformed SDK transport objects remain infrastructure failures. Exact record at the normal quarantine collection is:

```text
{schemaVersion:1,sourcePath,sourceUpdateTime,unencodableSourceDigest,
 reason:{code:"SOURCE_UNENCODABLE",token,message},failureCount:1,
 firstFailedAt:runNow,lastFailedAt:runNow,quarantinedAt:runNow}
```

The token/message map is byte-frozen: `NON_PLAIN_OBJECT` → `Source contains a non-plain object.`; `UNSUPPORTED_RUNTIME_TYPE` → `Source contains an unsupported runtime type.` No other message is legal. `originalBytesDigest` is forbidden; record cap is 16,384 canonical bytes. Exact existence is replay; disagreement fails closed. In the same transaction the source retains producer fields, deletes any retry map, and writes standard terminal/quarantined fields at the same `runNow`. MIG-EVENT's gate enforces the retry-or-terminal admission envelope below; therefore first-occurrence terminalization is mechanically fittable rather than retried pending forever. The normal digest and migration-archive branches remain disjoint.

D8 copies, rather than dynamically referring to, the Standard-edition storage formulas retrieved 2026-09-01: Bytes stored-value size is byte length; GeoPoint is 16; DocumentReference is referenced document-name size (each collection/document component UTF-8 bytes + 1, plus 16); Vector is `8 * dimensionCount`. Ordinary ascending/descending/array/composite entry arithmetic uses that value contribution capped at 1,500 bytes. The frozen §7.4 registry has no vector, text-search, or geospatial-special index mode. The geospatial-special formula—collection-name size + document-ID size + 128 bytes per indexed point + 48—is recorded as inapplicable, not conflated with GeoPoint storage. A later special mode requires a registry/hash/budget amendment. Production and the independent oracle separately implement the copied equations.

For pinned public-v1 raw `Document` sizing, a map enters Vector validation iff its `__type__` field is exactly `{stringValue:"__vector__"}`. That discriminator requires the map to have exactly two fields—`__type__:{stringValue:"__vector__"}` and `value:{arrayValue:<A>}`—and any missing, malformed, or surplus member is systemic `ARCHIVE_CODEC_INVARIANT` that blocks rollout/runtime mutation. `A` is exactly `{values:[{doubleValue:<finite binary64>},...]}` for one through 2,048 dimensions; zero dimension permits the generated-binding-equivalent `{}` or `{values:[]}` only. Every element has only `doubleValue`. It contributes `8 * count` stored bytes and the ordinary 1,500-byte index truncation above. Every other map—including `value` alone, `__type__` with another legal Firestore Value/type, or both members without that exact discriminator—uses ordinary map arithmetic. Raw-vector fixtures cover both zero spellings, 1/2,048 dimensions, 2,049, exact-discriminator missing/malformed/surplus shapes, ordinary `{value:...}`, ordinary `{__type__:"other",value:...}`, and near-budget terminal/index parity between independent production/oracle implementations.

`RawPhase0SizingV1` closes the runtime unencodable branch. After `SOURCE_UNENCODABLE` is identified, fetch that exact source name through pinned public-v1 `getDocument` and retain its complete raw `Document`/Value tree plus updateTime. The subsequent Admin transaction rereads the same source and must observe the identical path and updateTime before any write; drift aborts and restarts with a fresh raw read. From raw values, `fitsPhase0TransitionRaw` preserves every producer field byte-for-byte, removes only the scheduler retry member, overlays only the exact terminal scheduler fields, and applies the same document/index/transaction equations. The transaction terminalizes with field-path update/delete operations and never decodes or rewrites producer fields; it creates the qevu record separately. Raw absence, path/updateTime mismatch after bounded retry, unknown Value oneof, malformed reserved Vector, or raw sizing/codec disagreement takes the systemic no-source-write `POST_CUTOFF_SOURCE_SIZE_INVARIANT` branch. Thus qevu fit is decided from a representable raw document even when the high-level runtime object cannot enter `OriginalEventBytesV1`.

Freeze the ordinary qev1 code/message table:

```text
REFERENCE_INVALID          -> Event source path is invalid.
ENVELOPE_INVALID           -> Event envelope must be a map.
EVENT_ID_INVALID           -> event_id must equal the document ID.
EVENT_NAME_INVALID         -> event_name must be nonblank.
CANONICAL_KEY_INVALID      -> canonical_key must be nonblank.
SOURCE_VERSION_INVALID     -> source_version must be a nonnegative safe integer.
OBSERVED_AT_INVALID        -> observed_at must be a valid timestamp.
SOURCE_EVIDENCE_ID_INVALID -> source_evidence_id must be nonblank.
EFFECT_INVALID             -> effect must be fire or retract.
PAYLOAD_INVALID            -> payload must be valid Firestore event data.
PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE          -> Event payload cannot fit the Phase 2 high-water record.
PROCESSED_INVALID          -> Event must be pending and unprocessed.
```

No truncation choice remains because every listed message is already below 1,000 UTF-8 bytes. Define `fitsPhase0Transition(preSource,preHighWater?,postSource,postHighWater?,quarantine?)` over the exact changed-document set. Using D8, final normalized §7.4, and `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1`, it requires every present prospective document ≤1,048,576 bytes, every entry ≤7,680, per-document entry count ≤40,000 and entry-byte sum ≤8,388,608, safe arithmetic, and the complete create/update/delete charge—including current/prospective symmetric index-entry deltas—≤8,388,608. Optional absent documents contribute their exact create/delete semantics; an exact preexisting byte-equal quarantine is zero write. From a candidate complete decoded source `S`, construct every exact prospective retry source `R(code,count)` by replacing only `phase0ValidationFailure` with `{schemaVersion:1,originalBytesDigest:<64 lowercase hex>,reasonCode:code,failureCount:count,firstFailedAt:<Firestore Timestamp>,lastFailedAt:<same-or-later Firestore Timestamp>}` for each ordinary qev1 code and `count` 1 or 2. Construct each qev1 prospective terminal source `Q(message)` by preserving unrelated producer fields, removing `phase0ValidationFailure`, and replacing the scheduler fields with `{processingState:"terminal",processed:true,processedAt:<Firestore Timestamp>,outcome:"quarantined",processingError:message}`. The two qevu terminal prospects use only `fitsPhase0TransitionRaw` over the sidecar above, never decoded `S`. Timestamp storage size is fixed, so concrete values do not affect the bound.

The retry-or-terminal admission envelope is exact: base raw/decoded-equivalent `FirestoreDocumentStorageBytes(sourcePath,S) <= 1,047,552` **and** every retry transaction `S→R(code,count)`, every qev1 terminal transaction, and both raw qevu terminal transactions with their maximum exact quarantine create satisfy the applicable fit predicate. Thus the 1,024 bytes are a conservative retry-or-terminal reserve proved by complete prospective transaction equations, never an assumed delta. `SOURCE_TOO_LARGE` is exact iff the base comparison or any prospective fit check fails; base equality is admitted only when all checks pass. It is an admission/audit classification for MIG-EVENT and any future server producer, not an ordinary qev1 retry reason after the migration gate. MIG-EVENT computes the entire envelope from its raw `Document`, including the exact vector rule, so runtime high-level decoder novelty cannot bypass it. Every future producer must apply the same oracle before its create and return its own already-authorized caller surface without writing when the classification fires; Phase 2 adds no producer because the publisher registry is empty. A phase-0 runtime encounter after §3.8 has passed is systemic `POST_CUTOFF_SOURCE_SIZE_INVARIANT`: write no retry map, quarantine, high-water, source, cursor, or completion; leave the heartbeat running; emit one structured error containing only that code, source path, base/oracle prospective maxima, and run ordinal; increment log-based metric `phase2/post_cutoff_source_size_invariant_count`; and page S3 on any positive value. The pre-ship checklist records the metric/policy resource IDs and a successful test notification. Raw source bytes are forbidden from both log and alert.

`PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` is evaluated only after every preceding validator through `PAYLOAD_INVALID` passes. Determine high-water classification from those validated fields before the final processed-state check; stale, duplicate, and version-conflict skip this semantic size predicate, while only `advance` evaluates it. Then run `PROCESSED_INVALID` if no earlier reason won. For advance, construct the exact prospective §5.2 event-state map, exact terminal source with retry-map deletion and `outcome:"advance"`, literal source/high-water paths, and current high-water bytes when present. The predicate is true iff `fitsPhase0Transition(currentSource,currentHighWater?,advanceTerminalSource,prospectiveHighWater,absent)` is false; equality at every document/index/complete 8,388,608-byte limit is admitted. Production and the independent oracle share no implementation. It follows the ordinary identical-failure path: attempts one and two retain the source pending and write the exact retry map; attempt three creates/requires the ordinary exact schema-v1 `qev1` digest record with the table's code/message, terminalizes the source with that same message, deletes the retry map, and leaves high-water unchanged. The admission equation—not the base threshold alone—proves every retry and terminal source write fits.

Validation precedence is exact:

```text
REFERENCE_INVALID
→ SOURCE_UNENCODABLE
→ SOURCE_TOO_LARGE
→ ENVELOPE_INVALID
→ EVENT_ID_INVALID
→ EVENT_NAME_INVALID
→ CANONICAL_KEY_INVALID
→ SOURCE_VERSION_INVALID
→ OBSERVED_AT_INVALID
→ SOURCE_EVIDENCE_ID_INVALID
→ EFFECT_INVALID
→ PAYLOAD_INVALID
→ PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE
→ PROCESSED_INVALID
```

Size cannot precede an unencodable value. Stale, duplicate, and version-conflict are valid terminal outcomes, not validation reason codes; they terminalize the event and leave high-water unchanged. Boundary fixtures use valid-oversize versus stale rather than an impossible stale-and-invalid source.
The displayed order selects deterministic semantic classification. Independently, `fitsPhase0Transition` is mandatory for every decoded retry/qev1 terminal, valid advance, and stale/duplicate/version-conflict terminal transaction; `fitsPhase0TransitionRaw` is mandatory for qevu. The last three retain their explicit semantic size-skip but must fit their concrete source-only terminal transition. A post-cutoff violation takes the systemic no-write invariant branch even when an earlier semantic classification such as `SOURCE_UNENCODABLE` was already identified.

## 3. MIG-EVENT-V1 / D10 — total legacy oversize-event migration

`OriginalEventBytesV1` remains runtime quarantine identity and is **not** the migration archive format. The migration must preserve a complete legal stored Firestore document, including int64 precision, nonfinite values, unknown outer fields, and scheduler-owned retry fields.

### 3.1 Scope and fence

`functions/scripts/migrateOversizeEvents.js`, owned by S3, audits and applies through the pinned public-v1 `FirestoreClient.runQuery` streaming API with parent `projects/peezy-1ecrdl/databases/(default)/documents` and exact `StructuredQuery`: `from:[{collectionId:"events",allDescendants:true}]`; `where:{fieldFilter:{field:{fieldPath:"processingState"},op:"EQUAL",value:{stringValue:"pending"}}}`; `orderBy:[{field:{fieldPath:"__name__"},direction:"ASCENDING"}]`; `limit:{value:100}`; and, after a full 100-document page, `startAt:{before:false,values:[{referenceValue:<last complete document name>}]}` for the next exclusive cursor. The wrapper is required because the pinned generated public-v1 binding declares `limit` as `google.protobuf.Int32Value`; absent, numeric/unwrapped, nonsafe, nonpositive, or out-of-int32-range values are fatal before query. It processes each raw document sequentially, counts only document responses toward the 100-document page, and never materializes a page of payloads. Response classification uses protobuf field presence, never generated-object default values. A document response requires exactly one complete raw `Document` and one valid `readTime`; `done` is absent or literal `true`, and true makes that response both final document and terminal marker. A progress response has no document, one valid `readTime`, and absent `done`; it may occur anywhere and does not count toward the page. A done response has no document, literal `done:true`, and `readTime` absent or valid when present; it is terminal. Each of those three shapes forbids `transaction`, nonzero `skippedResults`, `explainMetrics`, and every unknown own property surfaced by the pinned generated binding. At most one true terminal marker may occur and nothing may follow it; normal stream EOF also terminates. An empty/no-document query is valid at normal EOF only after at least one valid readTime progress response. Present `done:false`, an empty member, missing/invalid document readTime, invalid present done-response readTime, positive or negative nonzero `skippedResults`, any forbidden/surfaced-unknown/mixed shape, stream error, duplicate/nonascending document name, or non-reference cursor is fatal. The pinned protobuf decoder discards unknown wire tags before delivery; invisible transport-extension tags are outside classification and are not claimed detectable. The audit/apply run report pins and records the exact `@google-cloud/firestore` version and generated descriptor digest.

Each returned raw name must match exactly `projects/peezy-1ecrdl/databases/(default)/documents/users/{nonblankUid}/events/{eventId}` with one nonblank UID and event ID segment; every other matching collection-group path is `OUT_OF_SCOPE_SOURCE` and blocks rollout. The complete retry-or-terminal predicate is computed directly from that response's raw protobuf `Value` tree. Immediately before deriving archive identity or issuing any write, `getDocument` rereads that literal full name; only this reread's raw createTime, updateTime, and complete fields enter §3.4. A missing, no-longer-pending, or now-admitted reread produces no archive write and restarts classification on the next confirmation pass; a still-failing changed document uses only its new identity. Neither path uses `DocumentSnapshot.data()`, JSON, or a private SDK member.

Audit performs another full uncursored pass after its first complete zero-failing pass. Apply performs the same confirmation while holding the fenced lease. §3.8 may pass only when two consecutive complete passes report zero failing sources, zero out-of-scope sources, and the same total pending-source count. Any insertion or change observed by the confirmation pass restarts the two-pass requirement.

The module is import-side-effect-free. Its default CLI mode is audit/read-only and cannot acquire a lease or issue a write. Write mode exists only when the same invocation supplies the literal triple `--apply --project-id peezy-1ecrdl --confirm-project peezy-1ecrdl`, has no unknown argument, resolves the public-v1 client to project `peezy-1ecrdl` and database `(default)`, observes `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` absent, verifies the normalized rules/index hashes against the accepted rollout tuple, and then acquires the fenced lease. A missing or mismatched confirmation, different resolved project/database, emulator variable, hash mismatch, unknown argument, or lease refusal exits nonzero before the first write. Invocation drains are an external rollout authorization gate proved through the named platform evidence in the next paragraphs; the script neither infers nor claims to prove them from a caller-supplied file. The separately authorized production run records the exact command, resolved project/database, tuple hashes, captured platform drain evidence, owner token/generation, counts, and result in its run report. Output may contain only document paths/IDs, digests, counts, enum outcomes, and lease metadata—never raw source, archive, or chunk bytes. Fixtures prove import has no effect; default audit is read-only; each executable arming predicate fails closed; and only the complete literal arming set can reach a write seam.

Deployment/migration order is exact. First deploy and refreeze the archive rules plus `eventArchiveChunks.payload` exemption and wait until the index exemption is effective. Temporarily revoke invocation of `deleteAccount`; let `Tzero` be the proved termination/end of its final old execution; pin and record `@google-cloud/firestore` 7.11.6 plus `build/src/v1/firestore_client_config.json` SHA-256 `2ed7a9046d766121b7f308b22384b1a6caaf7c6eaed5d9ea553556af3c987e41`, `BatchWrite.timeout_millis == 60000`, and `Commit.timeout_millis == 60000`; then wait through `Tzero + 60,000 ms` before any migration-lease/archive write. Conservatively, when the last deletion invocation began immediately before revocation, this is 120 seconds after revocation. If the platform cannot prove `Tzero`, undeploy that revision, prove zero instances, then wait the full RPC window; a package/config mismatch blocks rollout. After the settle window, run two complete fresh user-root/archive absence/consistency passes.

Before the migration lease can be acquired, disable/pause scheduler delivery, revoke invocation of `evaluateDispositionTriggers`, undeploy every old revision/service capable of running its pre-cross-fence handler, and prove zero old instances and zero active executions. Let `TschedulerZero` be the proved end of the final old execution and wait through `TschedulerZero + 60,000 ms` for the pinned Firestore Commit/BatchWrite RPC window. If final termination cannot be proved, after proved delivery revocation and zero old instances wait the full 270-second handler timeout plus 60,000 ms. A merely disabled schedule is insufficient because a queued delivery may already exist. Only after that drain may the script acquire the server-only `legacyOversizeMigrationV1` cross-fence and write an archive. While retaining it, migrate/reconcile and pass every §3.8 data/rules condition, then deploy the new cross-fenced `evaluateDispositionTriggers` handler with its schedule disabled. Release only the matching migration lease, prove its document absent, perform the B1 scheduler-state initialization/validation, restore scheduler invocation, and enable the schedule at the frozen boundary. A queued old event that reaches the new handler is subject to the new ordinal and migration-cross-fence transaction; no old handler remains reachable. Keep `deleteAccount` revoked until the joined §13 operational partial order permits its single final restoration. A deletion or scheduler attempt during maintenance receives its platform-unavailable/refused result and may retry afterward. This operational mutual exclusion prevents both a final recursive-delete BatchWrite from removing a parent/archive and a delayed scheduler write from racing migration. `functions/index.js` separately changes for §11. Failure to prove either drain blocks migration. Fixtures issue the last deletion BatchWrite and the last scheduler Commit at their respective timeout-minus-epsilon boundaries, settle each 60 seconds later, and prove no archive write begins before both settle/confirmation gates.

The migration fence is literal document `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/migrationLeases/legacyOversizeMigrationV1` with exact map:

```text
{schemaVersion:1,ownerToken,fencingGeneration,startedAt,renewedAt,expiresAt}
```

`ownerToken` is a generated lowercase UUID; generation is a positive safe integer; times are Firestore timestamps; `startedAt <= renewedAt` and `expiresAt == renewedAt + 300 seconds`. Lease operations use public-v1 transactions. The migration-acquisition transaction reads both this document and literal scheduler lease `phase1System/dispositionTriggerLease`; its `leaseNow` is the shared server `readTime`, never process wall time. An absent scheduler lease is safe. A present malformed scheduler lease fails closed. A valid nonexpired scheduler lease refuses. A valid expired scheduler lease is eligible only when `leaseNow >= schedulerLease.expiresAt + 60,000 ms`, which drains a Commit/BatchWrite begun just before lease expiry. In that same transaction, acquire creates migration generation 1 when absent or increments the prior migration generation exactly once when its `expiresAt <= leaseNow`; a live other migration owner refuses, and overflow blocks. Thus a delayed new scheduler acquisition and migration acquisition serialize: scheduler-first leaves a live/draining scheduler lease and migration refuses, while migration-first leaves a present cross-fence and scheduler refuses. A matching migration owner may renew the same generation even after nominal expiry only if no takeover won first; because every scheduler acquisition refuses while this document exists, renew need not reacquire a second scheduler fence. Every renew preserves `startedAt`, sets `renewedAt=leaseNow`, and CASes the migration-lease document's exact update time.

The lease is a fencing token, not a read-then-write advisory lock. Every manifest create/transition, chunk create/delete, source/quarantine terminal commit, orphan mark, and manifest delete includes in the **same atomic commit/transaction** a matching `(ownerToken,fencingGeneration)` lease renewal under its update-time precondition. A stale owner therefore cannot mutate after a takeover. Exact-existing chunk/manifest comparison performs either a renew-only commit before continuing or no subsequent mutation. Release deletes only the matching tuple under the last update-time precondition; a takeover makes stale release fail harmlessly. Process death leaves an expiring lease; the next owner increments generation and resumes solely from durable archive/source state. Fixtures cover both scheduler/migration acquisition serialization orders, a queued delivery after schedule pause, a scheduler Commit begun at expiry-minus-epsilon and settling at plus 60 seconds, expired-plus-59,999 ms refusal versus plus-60,000 ms admission, and malformed scheduler or migration cross-fence fail-closed with zero archive/scheduler mutation.

### 3.2 `FirestoreDocumentArchiveV1`

Every multibyte numeric scalar and every length is big-endian: `u32`, `i64`, and the raw IEEE-754 `f64` bit pattern. `u8` is one byte. `LP(x) = u32(byteLength(x)) || x`.

```text
ASCII("PZFDA") || u8(1)
|| LP(UTF8(fullyQualifiedDocumentName))
|| Timestamp(createTime)
|| Timestamp(updateTime)
|| Map(fields)

Timestamp = i64(seconds) || u32(nanoseconds)
Map = u32(entryCount) || each LP(unsigned-UTF8-sorted key) || Value
```

The complete Firestore `Value` oneof is:

```text
00                         null
01                         false
02                         true
03 || i64                  integer
04 || f64                  finite double, including signed zero
05                         canonical NaN
06                         +infinity
07                         -infinity
08 || Timestamp            timestamp
09 || LP(UTF8)             string
0A || LP(raw bytes)        bytes
0B || LP(UTF8 full path)   reference
0C || f64 || f64           geopoint latitude/longitude
0D || u32(count) || Value* array
0E || Map                  map, including the raw map representation of Vector
```

All Firestore NaNs normalize to tag 05 because Firestore equality does not expose a NaN payload. No Unicode normalization occurs. Unknown protobuf oneof, malformed timestamp/path/UTF-8, impossible length, or decoder residue is systemic `ARCHIVE_CODEC_INVARIANT` and blocks the entire migration. The required invariant is `encode(decode(bytes)) == bytes`; restoration can reproduce all stored fields and expose original metadata but does not claim that Firestore will reassign historical create/update timestamps.

Let `S` be frozen Firestore document-storage bytes, `K` the total count of encoded map keys, `V` the total count of encoded value nodes, `F` the sum of `UTF8.byteLength(referenceValue)` over every raw reference node, and `N` archive bytes. Non-reference key/value payload bytes are already included in `S`; Firestore bills a reference by referenced document-name size while this codec writes the longer fully qualified reference String, so `F` is an independent term and is never inferred from `S`. `K`, `V`, `F`, `S`, and `N` are independently accumulated with checked arithmetic; no inequality between `K` or `V` and `S` is assumed. The implementation asserts only:

```text
N <= S + 4K + 13V + F + 8,192
```

Production computes `F` and `N` directly from the same raw traversal but accumulates each independently with checked integer arithmetic; the independent oracle separately traverses and recomputes both. No legal-document maximum is derived from `S`. A mandatory fixture uses at least 1,000 repeated shortest-current-database reference values and proves the former no-`F` inequality fails while this inequality, exact archive round-trip, and digest pass. Exact-bound and arithmetic-overflow fixtures are also required.

`archiveDigest` is lowercase SHA-256 of all encoded bytes. Identity is:

```text
archiveId = "qev2_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({
  domain:"event_archive.v1",source_path,
  source_update_time:{seconds,nanoseconds},archive_digest
})))
```

### 3.3 Paths, chunks, and manifest

```text
manifest: users/{uid}/eventArchive/{archiveId}
chunk:    users/{uid}/eventArchive/{archiveId}/eventArchiveChunks/{NN}
```

`NN` is two-digit decimal `00...48`. `CHUNK_PAYLOAD_MAX = 393,216`, `MAX_CHUNKS = 49`, and `MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES = 19,267,584`. After exact `N` is precomputed and before any manifest, chunk, source, or quarantine mutation, require `1 <= ceil(N / CHUNK_PAYLOAD_MAX) <= 49`; `chunkCount` is that exact quotient. `N > MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES`, zero, unsafe arithmetic, or an unrepresentable quotient is fixed systemic `ARCHIVE_CHUNK_CAP_EXCEEDED`: emit only that code plus the source-path digest and measured `N`, perform no archive/source/quarantine mutation, release only the matching lease if apply already acquired it, and block the pre-ship migration/rollout. The read-only audit evaluates the same gate for every enumerated source before apply is authorized. It is not a per-source skip or quarantine outcome. Proceeding requires a separately reviewed addressing/cap amendment or proof that every enumerated source fits; corpus accident is never used as a general legal-document maximum. Exact `N == MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES`, `N == MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES + 1`, and the reference-heavy fixture exercise this gate. Exact chunk:

```text
{schemaVersion:1,archiveId,index,offset,length,payload:Bytes,payloadDigest}
```

Index is numeric; path ID is its two-digit form. Offset/range are mathematical slice boundaries; nonfinal length is 393,216 and final length is 1...393,216. `payloadDigest` hashes only payload. Chunks contain no timestamps and use create-only semantics, one physical commit each. Existing requires complete byte equality. No normal code updates a chunk. §7.4 exempts only `eventArchiveChunks.payload` from single-field indexing; generic `chunks` is untouched.

Manifest identity is exact:

```text
{schemaVersion:1,archiveId,codec:"FirestoreDocumentArchiveV1",
 sourcePath,sourceCreateTime,sourceUpdateTime,
 chunkPayloadMax:393216,chunkCount,totalBytes,archiveDigest,
 state,createdAt,
 sealedAt?,terminalizedAt?,orphanedAt?,orphanReason?}
```

Presence union: `building` permits no tail times/reason; `sealed` requires only `sealedAt`; `terminalized` requires `sealedAt` and `terminalizedAt`; `orphaned` requires only `orphanedAt` and `orphanReason:"SOURCE_CHANGED|SOURCE_DELETED"`. Identity and `createdAt` never change.

Each fenced mutation obtains one `leaseNow` from its transactional lease read. Building create sets `createdAt = leaseNow`; sealing sets `sealedAt = leaseNow`; orphan transition sets `orphanedAt = leaseNow`; terminalization uses §3.4's `terminalizedAt = leaseNow`; chunks have no time member. Exact replay preserves every committed time. A transaction retry may obtain a new `leaseNow` only until one attempt commits; a lost response resumes from the committed document and never rewrites its time. Fixtures cover retry-before-commit and lost-response equality for every timed transition.

### 3.4 State machine and CAS

1. Raw-read source, capture exact create/update time, encode, derive identity/chunks, and compute all budgets before first write.
2. Transactionally renew the fence and create `building` if absent; exact existing state resumes; identity mismatch is `ARCHIVE_ID_COLLISION`.
3. Create/exact-compare chunks ascending. Every physical create is paired with the fence renewal. Reread all ascending and verify path/schema/range/per-chunk digest/reassembly/length/full digest.
4. Transactionally renew the fence and change exact `building→sealed`; exact sealed/terminalized is replay.
5. Terminalization uses one public-v1 `Commit`, not a high-level Admin transaction and not an automatically retried closure. Immediately before it, transactionally raw-read the lease to obtain `leaseNow`/lease update time, then raw-read source, sealed manifest, and `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/quarantinedEvents/{archiveId}`. Set `terminalizedAt = leaseNow`. An already terminalized manifest plus matching source stub/quarantine is replay. Otherwise source must still exist/pending with raw `updateTime == sourceUpdateTime`, manifest must be exact sealed, quarantine must be absent, and the lease tuple must still match. The commit contains exactly four writes: renew lease under its captured update time; replace source under `currentDocument.updateTime = sourceUpdateTime`; create quarantine under `currentDocument.exists = false`; replace sealed manifest with exact terminalized manifest under `currentDocument.updateTime = sealedManifestUpdateTime`. The same `terminalizedAt` is stored as manifest terminalized time, source processed time, and quarantine time. This full-document CAS covers `phase0ValidationFailure` and every unknown outer member. All four writes succeed or fail atomically and the client does not retry the same commit blindly. A conflict causes a fresh lease/source classification; an existing pre-terminalization quarantine is a collision, even if superficially equal, because no legal v6 partial state creates it.

Exact source stub:

```text
{processingState:"terminal",processed:true,processedAt:terminalizedAt,
 outcome:"quarantined",
 processingError:"Stored event exceeded the Phase 2 event-processing size limit.",
 archiveRef:{schemaVersion:1,archiveId,codec:"FirestoreDocumentArchiveV1",
             chunkCount,totalBytes,archiveDigest}}
```

Exact schema-v2 quarantine:

```text
{schemaVersion:2,sourcePath,archiveDigest,
 reason:{code:"SOURCE_TOO_LARGE",
         message:"Stored event exceeded the Phase 2 event-processing size limit."},
 migrationKind:"LEGACY_OVERSIZE_ARCHIVE",sourceUpdateTime,
 archiveRef:{schemaVersion:1,archiveId,codec:"FirestoreDocumentArchiveV1",
             chunkCount,totalBytes,archiveDigest},
 quarantinedAt:terminalizedAt}
```

### 3.5 Mechanical budgets

Every physical commit uses `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1` and the post-manifest normalized index registry.

```text
ChunkCommitBudget(i)  <= 524,288
ChunkDeleteBudget(i)  <= 524,288
ManifestCommitBudget  <= 65,536
LeaseFenceWriteBudget <= 4,096
TaskCanonicalV1(stub) <= 16,384
TaskCanonicalV1(quarantine) <= 16,384
FirestoreWriteBudgetV1(absent -> stub) <= 32,768
FirestoreWriteBudgetV1(absent -> quarantine) <= 32,768
```

Terminal commit charge is old-source removed index entries plus prospective stub, new quarantine, and terminalized manifest. Therefore:

```text
LegacyOversizeTerminalBudgetV1
 <= 8,388,608 + 32,768 + 32,768 + 65,536 + 4,096
 <= 8,523,776
```

Chunk/manifest commit caps include their paired lease renewal; the terminal sum spells it separately. That one cap is authorized only for this offline Admin migration and only for the exact lease/source/quarantine/manifest shapes. It is below Firestore's 10,485,760 request limit with 1,961,984 bytes headroom. Every production-connected transaction retains 8,388,608. `8,523,777`, a fifth write, shape/cap/index violation, or unsafe arithmetic aborts before write. The migration oracle operates on raw v1 Values and therefore admits the full stored domain, including int64 extrema and nonfinite doubles; it never routes those values through the production semantic canonicalizer.

### 3.6 Idempotence and orphan cleanup

The exhaustive branches are: absent manifest + matching source version creates building; building resumes chunks/seal; sealed + same pending update time attempts the atomic terminal commit; terminalized + exact stub/quarantine no-ops; changed pending update time creates a new archive and makes the old one an orphan candidate; absent source makes an unreferenced archive an orphan candidate; another terminal outcome/ref is owner conflict; source ref with missing/bad archive is `ARCHIVE_BROKEN`; sealed plus any preexisting quarantine is collision; any other ID/content mismatch is collision.

Under the fenced migration ownership, mark `orphaned` only in a transaction that renews the lease and rereads source/quarantine to prove neither references the ID and source no longer has the captured update time. Delete exact chunks descending with one lease-renewal+chunk-delete commit each and delete the manifest last with the same fence. In `orphaned`, missing suffix chunks are valid cleanup progress; mismatching extant chunks fail closed. Crash/takeover resumes cleanup. A referenced or terminalized archive is never cleaned. Retention is indefinite until account deletion or separately authorized policy.

Rules deny all client access to `/users/{uid}/eventArchive/{document=**}`, the migration-lease path, and all quarantine shapes. Existing recursive user deletion removes archives/chunks. Archive migration relies on §3.1's explicit invocation/RPC drain for mutual exclusion; `functions/index.js` changes independently for §11's permanent account-deletion fence. Fixtures prove nested recursive deletion after migration and attempted deletion at every archive/terminal boundary while invocation is disabled.

### 3.7 Mandatory migration fixtures

Every raw Value oneof; int64 extrema; byte-exact big-endian vectors for `1.0` (`3ff0000000000000`), `-0.0` (`8000000000000000`), the least positive subnormal (`0000000000000001`), and one geopoint pair; NaN forms; infinities; timestamp extrema; empty/max bytes/string; NUL/control Unicode; unusual valid field names; references; geopoints; nested arrays/maps/vector representation; encode/decode/re-encode; absent payload; 200-byte payload plus maximum unknown outer field; nonfinite unknown outer value; exact query normalized through pinned `StructuredQuery.fromObject` and passed to an injected `FirestoreClient.runQuery` spy, with the spy observing `limit.value == 100`, plus rejection of absent/unwrapped/out-of-range wrappers; runQuery enumeration at 0/1/100/101 documents counting documents only; ordinary document+readTime; final document+readTime+`done:true`; midstream and final readTime-only progress; no-document `done:true` with absent and valid readTime; normal EOF with/without documents; empty EOF requiring prior progress readTime; generated bindings whose absent fields expose defaults but no protobuf presence; surfaced unknown own-property rejection and a separately encoded unknown wire tag proving the pinned decoder discards it without changing known fields; present `done:false`; empty response; missing/invalid required readTime; duplicate, nonascending, mixed/unknown, nonzero skipped-results, explain-metrics, transaction, post-terminal, and stream-error responses; a same-leaf `events` document at wrong ancestry; source deletion/change between query and getDocument; insertion caught by the uncursored confirmation pass; stable two-pass pending count; chunk lengths at `P-1/P/P+1/2P` and maximum; every crash boundary; absent/live/expired lease; readTime clock; renewal; generation overflow; process death; two workers with takeover before every mutation and stale commit/release rejection; update-time-only retry-map change; unknown-field change; deletion; matching/mismatching terminal source; every collision/missing chunk; new-version orphan and cleanup crash/takeover; referenced archive protection; exact budget boundaries; generic `chunks` index unaffected; rules/account deletion; and H57 without raw data.

### 3.8 Pre-ship gate

Zero pending in-scope source failing the complete retry-or-terminal admission envelope or the exact archive-chunk gate; zero unresolved out-of-scope source; zero building/sealed/orphaned manifest; zero broken archive; every terminalized manifest paired with one exact source stub/quarantine and exact chunk reassembly/digest; every preexisting `quarantinedEvents` row complete-schema-valid with an exact parsable `sourcePath`; §11's UID-range query/index oracle passes over the complete quarantine population; exact normalized rules/index bytes and refrozen SHA. Before runtime deploy, while the matching migration lease is held, every preceding data/rules condition must pass. After deploy, release that matching lease and prove its document absent before scheduler re-enable. Failure blocks scheduler re-enable or restoration of `deleteAccount`.

## 4. D12/D13 — terminal-child reuse

Remove only the three create-time-zero revision comparisons. An existing policy child must match the already-frozen stable identity/provenance/policy, task instance, and generation-epoch projection. Its current `flowWriteRevision`, `notesWriteRevision`, `quotesWriteRevision`, and any live progress/tail are structurally validated under frozen §5.5 but are never compared with create-time zero and are preserved byte-for-byte. An existing legacy child retains the already-frozen legacy immutable identity/provenance and policy-absence match unchanged. The same rule governs `terminal_spawn_closure` reconfirmation. Fixtures vary each revision independently/together, preserve a live handoff/tail, drift every already-frozen immutable branch member, and prove no reuse/reconfirmation branch reads a revision for identity.
