## 5. B2/D14/D15 — one reset-envelope owner and gesture binding

`ResetOperationRegistry` inside `TaskPlanService.swift` is the sole actor and sole reader/writer of `PeezyTaskPlanReset-v2.json`. No view, coordinator, callback, migration helper, or façade directly accesses that file. Alias lookup is never a collection query: Phase 2 resolves normal resets from `(uid,expectedEpoch)` to the deterministic canonical path; D16/D17 remain withdrawn.

S1 also defines `ResetEpochAuthorityProviding.current(uid:expectedAuth:) async -> {uid,taskGenerationEpoch}`. Production reads the user-root authority through the existing authenticated Firestore seam and validates UID, safe epoch, and the complete expected auth tuple before returning. Bind obtains signed auth from `AuthAuthorityProviding`, awaits this epoch seam, rereads signed auth, and applies the envelope CAS below; neither protocol is allowed to synthesize epoch zero from read failure.

Payload is exact:

```text
{records:[ResetOperationRegistryRecordV2] <= 4,
 legacyMigrations:[LegacyResetMigrationV1] <= 4,
 gesture?:ResetGestureV1}
```

```text
ResetGestureV1 = {
  gestureId,uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision,
  phase:"reserved"|"bound",alias,expectedEpoch?,
  gestureGeneration,reservedAt,boundAt?
}
```

All local times in §§5–6 come from one injected `LocalDurableClock.now()` returning a canonical UTC RFC 3339 millisecond instant. Each atomic envelope replacement samples it exactly once immediately before encoding and defines `writeTime` as the later of that sample and every valid `reservedAt|boundAt|createdAt|updatedAt` instant on the gesture/row being advanced; comparison is by parsed instant and output is re-encoded in the canonical millisecond form. Reservation creation sets `reservedAt=writeTime` and forbids `boundAt`; reserved→bound preserves `reservedAt` and sets `boundAt=writeTime`; bound→prepared sets the new reset row's `createdAt=gesture.reservedAt` and `updatedAt=writeTime`. Attaching reserved-gesture `initiatingAuthority` to an existing blocked invalid-alias migration preserves the migration's `createdAt`, sets its `updatedAt=writeTime`, and preserves an already-stored gesture's `reservedAt`; only a gesture created in that same atomic write sets `reservedAt=writeTime`. Fresh migration and background/receipt-derived reset-row creation without a gesture set `createdAt=updatedAt=writeTime`; receipt-derived creation with a reserved gesture uses that gesture's `reservedAt` as `createdAt` and `writeTime` as `updatedAt`. Direct `phase2_active` adoption from an invalid-alias inspection likewise creates the ordinary `reset_dispatched` row with `createdAt=gesture.reservedAt` and `updatedAt=writeTime`. Transformation of an existing reset row preserves `createdAt`; every later reset/migration phase, redirect, or auth-rebind transition preserves `createdAt` and sets `updatedAt=writeTime`. Exact replay/no-write preserves every time byte. Clock failure, nonrepresentable sample, or noncanonical stored time writes nothing and enters corruption recovery. Exact-byte fixtures cover new attachment, crash-resume attachment, and direct phase2-active adoption.

`records` are unique by exact `(uid,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch)`, ordered by `createdAt`, then unsigned-UTF8 UID, then numeric expected epoch, and capped at four total. Recomputed `handleId` values must also be unique across the complete records array; a duplicate is structural corruption even when the row keys differ. `legacyMigrations` contains at most one row per UID, is ordered by `createdAt` then unsigned-UTF8 UID, and is capped at four total; duplicate UID, noncanonical order, or a row whose map UID differs from its indexed identity is structural corruption and no timestamp selects authority. This expressly replaces the frozen reset UID-only uniqueness sentence. Only the current signed-in UID participates in row selection or epoch conflict: on load, signed-auth change, and reserve, zero current-UID rows permits reserve, exactly one resumes that row, and two or more blocks. Signed-out state and rows for every foreign UID are inert under their ordinary retention/cap rules and never create an epoch conflict. The owner reclassifies on every auth change. With a conflict, reserve writes no gesture/row, dispatches nothing, and returns the exact recovery surface below; the owner must complete the listed phase recoveries until at most one current-UID row remains. Separately recovered epochs remain representable but are never chosen by timestamp or array order.

On load and every signed-auth change, before selection, materialization, reserve, bind, or drive, signed-out state or any `gesture.uid/authEpochUUID` mismatch atomically removes that gesture. If it byte-matches a reserved-gesture `initiatingAuthority`, that member is cleared in the same envelope write; the migration row and every materialized/reset-dispatched row remain intact. A mismatched bound gesture is also removed and is never materialized under another auth tuple. Same-UID/same-epoch higher `credentialRevision` may atomically rebase the gesture and its byte-matching reserved migration authority before further work; a lower revision is corruption and writes nothing. Crash/retry repeats this normalization idempotently. Fixtures cover reserved alone, reserved plus migration, and crash-loaded bound across A→signed-out, A→B, and A→new-A-epoch; every path performs zero bind/dispatch/materialization until a fresh Settings confirmation creates a new gesture.

Cross-member validity is exact. A same-UID migration may coexist with `gesture` only when the gesture is `reserved` and byte-matches its `reserved_gesture` initiating authority; a bound gesture beside any same-UID migration is structural corruption. Before migration APPLYING, a coexisting same-UID reset row must be the exact `reset_dispatched` authority named by the migration. APPLYING forbids `initiatingAuthority` and may coexist only with the exact receipt-materialized/transformed row selected by that migration outcome. A gesture and reset row for the same UID with no same-UID migration is structural corruption; no reserve, bind, drive, or materialization occurs. Cross-UID rows remain legal. Every other same-UID gesture/migration/authority-row combination enters B3 corruption with zero selection, materialization, or call.

`gestureId` is `rsg1_` plus lowercase UUID; alias is generated once as `ResetAliasV1`. `gestureGeneration` is a lowercase RFC 4122 UUID equal to outer `DurableFileEnvelopeV1.generationId` of the atomic write that first stores this gesture and is immutable for that gesture. Later whole-envelope writes receive fresh outer generation IDs but preserve this token; every continuation CASes the stored token plus gesture/auth identity. Reserved forbids epoch/bound time; bound requires both. No bind-failure counter exists; a transient failure leaves the byte-identical reserved gesture.

`Reservation` is exact `{gestureId,gestureGeneration}` from the gesture that actually won the durable envelope CAS. `ResetOperationHandle` is exact `{uid,handleId}` and never carries caller-supplied alias or epoch bytes. For a validated registry row, `handleId = "rho1_" + SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({uid,suggested_operation_id:row.suggestedOperationId,created_at:row.createdAt}))`; it is stable if RESET_ACTIVE adoption changes the row's expected epoch and differs for a later intentional reset. `ResetReserveOutcome` is the closed Swift union `binding(Reservation)|operation(ResetOperationHandle)|migrationPending(ResetMigrationPending)|legacyCompleted(LegacyResetCompleted)|legacyRetryRequired(LegacyResetRetryRequired)`, where the three migration payloads are the exact local-only shapes in §6.5. Registry-full, epoch-conflict, migration-full, blocked, auth, and corruption conditions throw only their already-frozen exact errors.

`reserve(gestureId:) async throws -> ResetReserveOutcome` first obtains signed-in auth before it consults any in-memory slot. `inflightReserve` is exactly one `(authIdentity:{uid,authEpochUUID},credentialBaseline,authoritativeGestureId,task:Task<ResetReserveOutcome,Error>)`; it is installed before that task's first suspension and cleared in `defer` on every outcome. UID plus auth epoch is the security/join identity; credential revision is a monotonic CAS baseline, not a distinct identity. A caller with the same identity and current revision greater than or equal to the baseline may join and receive the shared outcome, regardless of proposed gesture ID; a lower revision is corruption. A different-account, signed-out, or different-auth-epoch caller waits only for slot retirement, discards the prior task's result or error, rereads auth, and restarts; it never receives or projects the prior identity's outcome. Every joining caller rereads signed auth after the task returns and exposes the outcome only when UID/epoch still match and current revision is at least the baseline; the task's frozen same-epoch rebase therefore remains returnable. Identity drift discards/restarts. A losing proposed ID is neither persisted nor passed to bind. The existing Reset Assessment confirmation that invokes `RetakeAssessmentCoordinator.retake()` is the explicit consent attached to that fresh reserve invocation; background/startup work has no such consent. After current-auth validation, inspect same-UID migration rows **before** loaded-bound materialization or any reset-row drive, with three narrow confirmed-invocation transitions. If the sole migration is blocked `LEGACY_ALIAS_INVALID` without initiating authority, reserve first atomically adopts/writes the invocation's durable reserved gesture and attaches the complete exact `{kind:"reserved_gesture",gestureId,gestureGeneration,alias}` tuple byte-for-byte; no inspection or clear precedes that write. If that blocked row already carries initiating authority byte-identical to the stored reserved gesture, reserve resumes inspection/clear under that durable consent after crash; a present authority with no byte-identical gesture is structural corruption. If the sole migration is blocked `LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT`, confirmed reserve performs its frozen same-request blocked→PREPARED Retry and retains/attaches only exact existing authority. If it is blocked `LEGACY_ALIAS_COLLISION_EXHAUSTED`, confirmed reserve adopts or validates the exact durable gesture/initiating authority, draws one new migration alias, resets `aliasCandidateOrdinal` to 1, rewrites request bytes/hash, and atomically enters PREPARED; no background/startup path may do this. `OPERATION_REUSED` never transitions. After any narrow transition, migration precedence is absolute: no same-UID reset row is selected, bound, driven, reset, or finalized until migration reaches its frozen outcome. Any other blocked migration returns `RESET_MIGRATION_BLOCKED`, and more than one same-UID migration is corruption. Only when no same-UID migration exists may the actor materialize a loaded bound gesture; it then classifies reset rows, returning one as `operation({uid,handleId:<recomputed>})` or publishing the typed `reset_epoch_conflict` blocked state below for multiple. Only with neither kind may reserve adopt/create a gesture. When migration APPLYING finishes its key/application work and materializes/transforms the reset row, that same auth-scoped reserve singleflight may then return its derived operation handle; before that boundary it returns only a migration outcome. Otherwise reserve performs the coalesced current-auth read, refuses without mutation when the four-row cap is occupied, and atomically adopts an exact current reservation or writes one new `ResetGestureV1` **before any legacy-reconciliation or server await**. With no migration need it returns `binding(Reservation)` only after the winning reserved gesture is durable. Fixtures hold an A task while switching to B or a new A auth epoch and prove the later caller receives no prior outcome, authority, or error; a same-epoch credential refresh during the epoch await rebases once and returns the same outcome to both callers.

Every user-initiated migration retains its initiating authority through RECEIPT unless the mandatory auth-normalization rule above atomically removes a stale reserved gesture and its matching reserved authority. `upgraded|phase2_active` materializes surviving authority and maps to `operation(ResetOperationHandle)`; `not_dispatched` retires it and maps to `legacyRetryRequired`; `finalized_compat` retires it on compatibility APPLYING and maps to `legacyCompleted`; a transient durable phase maps to `migrationPending`. No migration outcome binds or dispatches a second reset in the same invocation. A background migration begun without a user gesture leaves `gesture` absent; an autonomous background caller may discard its internal result, but any concurrent or later user `reserve` joining that same migration still maps `not_dispatched→legacyRetryRequired`, `finalized_compat→legacyCompleted`, `upgraded|phase2_active→operation`, and transient→`migrationPending` through the nonoptional union. Every post-await write CASes gesture identity, captured `gestureGeneration`, UID, auth epoch, and current credential revision. UID/auth-epoch change must discard stale gesture authority under the rule above; a materialized record is never discarded.

The public bind operation is `bind(reservation: Reservation) async throws -> ResetOperationHandle`. It reads signed-in auth before consulting `inflightBind`, whose exact shape is `(reservation:Reservation,authIdentity:{uid,authEpochUUID},credentialBaseline,task:Task<ResetOperationHandle,Error>)`. Only an exact reservation-plus-UID/epoch match joins the shared task; current revision must be at least the baseline and a lower revision is corruption. Any UID/epoch/signed-out mismatch waits only for slot retirement, discards the prior outcome, rereads auth, and restarts. Every joiner rereads auth after task completion and returns the handle only when UID/epoch still match and revision is at least the baseline, so a frozen same-epoch rebase does not turn a successful bind into `RESET_GESTURE_STALE`. A same-ID/wrong-generation, different, unknown, or consumed reservation is `RESET_GESTURE_STALE`; no caller after an account or auth-epoch change can receive the prior account's handle. Binding reads one authenticated `{uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision,taskGenerationEpoch}` snapshot. Its first post-await atomic envelope write CASes the same reserved gesture, reservation generation, UID, and auth epoch, rebases only a newer same-epoch credential revision, and changes `reserved→bound` while storing exact `expectedEpoch` and `boundAt`. UID/auth-epoch change writes nothing. A second distinct atomic envelope write, with no intervening await, CASes that exact bound tuple, creates the UID/epoch exact `prepared` row using only its stored alias/epoch/auth, and removes gesture. A crash is possible between the two writes; startup or exact retry materializes a loaded bound gesture without rereading epoch. Only after that prepared row is durable does bind return its row-key handle. A late consumed-reservation call is stale and never recaptures root epoch or mints another alias. Fixtures hold A's completed-but-not-cleared bind task across A→B and A→new-A-epoch and prove the later caller receives no A handle; a refresh during the epoch await creates PREPARED once and returns the same handle to both same-epoch callers.

At bind entry and again at its post-await envelope CAS, any same-UID migration makes that reservation stale for binding: bind writes nothing and throws `RESET_GESTURE_STALE`; only reserve may resume/map the migration. At drive entry and after every reducer suspension, a same-UID migration has absolute precedence: drive joins/resumes it through the per-UID migration singleflight and performs no ordinary reset, finalize, cleanup, or callback until the migration reaches one exhaustive result. A transient phase returns `migrationPending`; `not_dispatched` returns `legacyRetryRequired`; `finalized_compat` returns `legacyCompleted`; `upgraded|phase2_active` requires the materialized row, recomputes its handle, and continues the ordinary reducer; blocked returns the typed blocked error. Only an outcome that promises a materialized row and then finds it absent, multiple, or mismatched throws `RESET_OPERATION_STALE` with zero ordinary action. Fixtures race a held Reservation and a preexisting handle against migration PREPARED, DISPATCHED, RECEIPT, APPLYING, and BLOCKED and prove this precedence and every result mapping at every suspension.

`inflightResetOperation` is an actor-owned dictionary keyed by exact `ResetOperationHandle`; each value is exact `(authIdentity:{uid,authEpochUUID},credentialBaseline,task:Task<ResetInvocationOutcome,Error>)`, installed before the first ordinary phase-reducer suspension and cleared in `defer`. Before lookup or install, `drive(handle:callbacks:)` requires freshly read signed-in auth with `uid == handle.uid`. Only an exact handle-plus-UID/epoch match joins, with current revision at least the baseline; a lower revision is corruption. A UID/epoch/signed-out mismatch waits only for slot retirement, discards the prior task's result, rereads auth, and restarts; no outcome or callback bundle crosses auth identities. When no same-UID migration exists, drive requires exactly one row for `handle.uid` and recomputes its handle ID byte-for-byte; then matching same-process callers await the one task already installed for it. Only that task runs the ordinary phase reducer and protected callback order. The winner revalidates signed auth after every suspension and immediately before every server call, protected callback, and return; different UID/epoch/signed-out drift preserves the durable phase, performs no later call/callback, and returns the frozen auth-pending/error branch, while a higher same-epoch revision is monotonically rebased under the ordinary CAS and may continue. RESET_ACTIVE adoption may change the row's expected epoch but preserves the suggested alias/created time and therefore the handle. An absent row, multiple same-UID rows, or handle mismatch is `RESET_OPERATION_STALE` with zero write/dispatch. `ResetInvocationOutcome` is the closed Swift union `completed(applicationId)|migrationPending(ResetMigrationPending)|legacyCompleted(LegacyResetCompleted)|legacyRetryRequired(LegacyResetRetryRequired)`. The winning task owns the first callback bundle; joiners receive only its outcome and never invoke their supplied callbacks. Relaunch loses in-memory singleflight but the durable phase still selects the sole idempotent recovery action below. Fixtures hold A's drive at every suspension while switching to B or a new auth epoch and prove the later caller receives no A outcome/callback and the stale winner makes no subsequent call/callback; a same-epoch revision refresh continues only after its rebase.

No active or receipt-bearing row is evicted. Completed rows are removed only after idempotent applying completes. Phase recovery is exact:

| Durable phase | Only recovery action |
|---|---|
| `prepared` | dispatch same reset request |
| `reset_dispatched` | retry same request |
| `reset_receipt/deleting` | resume server deletion |
| `reset_receipt/awaiting_local_reset` | run protected local cleanup |
| `finalize_dispatched` | retry finalize |
| `final_receipt` | enter applying |
| `applying` | resume idempotent state-derived apply |
| receipt disagreement/corrupt envelope | B3 reconciliation; never dispatch |

Local cleanup is generation-fenced, not merely UID-scoped or nominally idempotent. Define ephemeral, nonpersisted `ResetLocalCleanupAuthorityV1 = {schemaVersion:1,accountUid,operationId,requestFingerprint,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,taskGenerationEpoch,activeMoveEventId,deletedCount,deletedCounts:{tasks,notificationIntents,taskDeadlineEvidence,confirmationSnapshots}}`. Only `ResetOperationRegistry` may construct it, and only from a fully validated read-only `inspectCommittedOperation/RESET` result whose outcome is `pending` and whose exact progress receipt is `awaiting_local_reset`. Inspection must already have validated the total operation-record ↔ root-marker ↔ progress-receipt equality. Every authority member projected by `reset_progress` must equal that receipt byte-for-byte; `operationId` and `requestFingerprint` come only from the same validated registry-row/inspection identity. Marker-only state/cursor/lease/timestamp members are deliberately not invented in this authority. No view, coordinator, callback implementation, or caller may construct or retain authority across callbacks.

Immediately before **each** of the three destructive callbacks and again before finalize, the reducer revalidates current signed auth plus the exact registry row, calls the existing read-only RESET inspection, and handles the result exhaustively. Exact `pending/awaiting_local_reset` derives a fresh authority and invokes only the next callback. Exact `committed` takes a new atomic durable edge `reset_receipt/awaiting_local_reset → final_receipt`, stores the inspected final receipt, skips every remaining callback and finalize call, and enters replay application with zero ephemeral notification. `absent`, malformed/OPERATION_REUSED, identity drift, an active operation with no exact marker, or any non-awaiting pending receipt blocks with zero callback. Transport ambiguity preserves the phase with zero callback. After any callback error—including permission denial or transaction contention—the registry inspects again before exposing Retry: committed adopts final and skips the callback forever; byte-identical pending retains `reset_receipt/awaiting_local_reset` and permits Retry; every other result blocks. `recoverEpoch(...,action:run_local_cleanup)` uses this identical reducer and has no alternate closure.

Effective root epoch is absent → 0, present safe integer `0...9,007,199,254,740,991` → that value, malformed → no write. Every fresh `users/{uid}/user_assessments/{assessmentId}`, `userKnowledge/{uid}`, and `users/{uid}.dailyDose` value carries immutable safe integer `task_generation_epoch` equal to effective root epoch and may be created/updated only while `taskReset` is absent. Daily dose's exact map becomes `{schema_version:1,task_generation_epoch,date,taskIds}`. `AssessmentDataManager.saveAssessment()` reads root and creates the auto-ID assessment in one transaction. `UserKnowledgeService.merge()` transactionally reads root, requires reset absence, preserves an existing matching stamp or creates/stamps the current epoch, and rejects malformed/different-epoch authority.

One compatibility branch is frozen for shipped unstamped epoch-zero data. An existing unstamped assessment may be updated only while effective root epoch is exactly 0 and taskReset absent, and that update must preserve stamp absence; any root epoch >0 denies it. Creates always stamp the current epoch. An unstamped `userKnowledge` at root epoch 0 may be transactionally upgraded by `UserKnowledgeService.merge()` to stamp 0 while applying its merge; at any later epoch missing/malformed rejects. An unstamped legacy root dailyDose may be read only while effective root epoch 0, and its next successful freeze transaction replaces it with the exact stamped epoch-zero schema; at later epoch it is cleanup-only under the exact 0→1 bridge. Thus existing Settings/InApp assessment updates remain usable before the first reset without editing their semantic fields, while no unstamped value can cross that reset. Fixtures cover pre-first-reset update/freeze/stamp, reset cutover, and post-first-reset denial.

Every assessment delete is a transaction that reads root first and then the exact candidate; knowledge cleanup similarly reads root plus its document. `DailyDoseEngine.resetForRetake(authority:)` uses one root transaction. Each rereads and requires one complete exact root `taskReset` map with literal state `awaiting_local_reset`, `targetIndex:4`, `pageAfterPath` and lease absent, concrete Firestore `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, and `awaitingLocalResetAt`, and `createdAt <= awaitingLocalResetAt <= updatedAt`. Its account/operation/fingerprint, expected/result epochs, move-event ID, deleted total, and complete deleted-count projection must be byte-equal to the corresponding authority members. Marker timestamps are validated from the marker itself and are never compared to a differently shaped authority. Missing, surplus, wrong-state/index/cursor/lease/time/order/identity/count content writes nothing. A stored epoch `< authority.taskGenerationEpoch` may be deleted; `>=` is preserved. A missing stamp is legacy epoch zero only for exact `expectedTaskGenerationEpoch == 0 && taskGenerationEpoch == 1`; outside that bridge, or when malformed, it is preserved, emits fixed `RESET_LOCAL_GENERATION_INVALID`, and blocks finalization. Root daily-dose deletion may change only `dailyDose` and must preserve the exact marker. A marker change causes transaction retry and complete revalidation; absence or a different operation writes nothing. Firestore Rules independently enforce the same complete marker-shape/state/index/cursor/lease/time-order predicates for cleanup plus the generation relation, but do not claim caller operation identity; the client transaction supplies that stronger authority comparison. Fixtures cover every missing/surplus marker member, each marker timestamp and order boundary, and marker drift before every candidate commit.

`DailyDoseLocalStore`, inside `DailyDoseEngine.swift`, is the sole process-wide actor accessing canonical-JSON `Data` at `peezy.{uid}.dailyDose.v2`, cap 1,024 bytes, exact `{schemaVersion:1,taskGenerationEpoch,revision,completedCount,lastDate,firstLaunchDate}`. Epoch/revision are safe nonnegative; completedCount is `0...1,000,000`; dates are null or exact `YYYY-MM-DD`; unknown/surplus/malformed members block mutation and are never silently removed. All `PeezyHomeViewModel` dose reads/writes use this actor; each mutation CASes exact epoch/revision and increments revision. Cleanup at result epoch `r` replaces an older envelope with exact empty epoch-r state/revision+1; preserves equal bytes; preserves and reports drift for newer; creates an empty epoch-r floor when absent; and preserves/blocks malformed. Only for exact 0→1 does it write the v2 floor first and then remove all three legacy keys; a crash with both representations always gives v2 authority. Later reset never infers a legacy key epoch.

Ordinary reset notification is deliberately at-most-once, not exactly-once. `.retakeAssessment` may be posted only by the same in-memory reducer invocation that (a) received and durably stored the first exact `reset_final` receipt with `replayed:false` and (b) then commits that row's `final_receipt→applying` envelope transition. It posts only after APPLYING durability and at most once in that process. A receipt with `replayed:true`, a row loaded in `final_receipt`, or a row loaded in `applying` never posts; those paths perform only idempotent reload/routing/application and removal. A crash after final-receipt or APPLYING durability may therefore lose the ephemeral notification, but no crash, commit-response loss, replay, or relaunch may duplicate it. No notification occurs before final-receipt durability.

The local capacity error is exact `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"RESET_REGISTRY_FULL",capacity:4,occupants:[{uid,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,phase,recoveryAction}]}` in `createdAt`, unsigned-UTF8 UID, numeric expected-epoch order and changes no bytes. It is used only when all four slots are occupied and the current UID has zero rows. The distinct same-UID ambiguity error is exact `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"RESET_EPOCH_CONFLICT",uid,occupants:[{expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,phase,recoveryAction}]}` and lists every current-UID row in `createdAt`, numeric expected-epoch order. It likewise changes no bytes and exposes only the same phase-specific recovery actions. `phase` is the stored six-phase reset vocabulary. Recovery action is exact: `retry_reset` for prepared/reset_dispatched; `resume_server_deletion` for reset_receipt/deleting; `run_local_cleanup` for reset_receipt/awaiting_local_reset; `retry_finalize` for finalize_dispatched; and `apply_final` for final_receipt/applying.

The error is also an executable reset-store blocked state, not a terminal message. On load, signed-auth change, or reserve, more than one completely valid row for the current signed-in UID sets `StartupBarrier.reset = blocked(reset_epoch_conflict)` and forbids selection, binding, ordinary drive, or readiness. Signed-out state and foreign-UID multiplicity never enter this state. Its exact option is `{expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,phase,recoveryAction}` using the same sort/vocabulary above. `actionableExpectedTaskGenerationEpoch` is required and equals the numerically smallest occupant epoch; every later occupant is displayed but disabled until every smaller-epoch row is conclusively removed/applied and the store is reclassified. `recoveryStateDigest = lowercase SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({schemaVersion:1,store:"reset",baseState:"reset_epoch_conflict",uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision,envelopeGeneration,envelopeSHA256,actionableExpectedTaskGenerationEpoch,occupants:[<complete ordered options>]}))`; UID/auth remain private and the public snapshot carries no account. S1 declares `ResetEpochConflictRecovering`; S4 renders every option and enables only the exact actionable epoch; S7 wires it to `RetakeAssessmentCoordinator`'s existing protected callback bundle. The actor method is exactly `recoverEpoch(recoveryStateDigest:expectedTaskGenerationEpoch:expectedPhase:action:) async -> RecoveryResult`; the actor binds it to current signed auth. Before each call and after every await it revalidates current signed-auth triple, complete envelope generation/hash, exact ordered options, the minimum actionable epoch, selected row identity/phase/action, and digest. Drift returns the fresh blocked snapshot with zero further action; selecting any later epoch returns `RECOVERY_ACTION_UNAVAILABLE` with zero callback/server/write. Fixtures switch A→B and signed-in→signed-out with two retained A rows and prove both become inert without deletion or callback.

The selected action invokes only that row's ordinary idempotent reducer branch: `retry_reset`, `resume_server_deletion`, `retry_finalize`, and `apply_final` use their frozen server/receipt transitions; `run_local_cleanup` uses the coordinator's same assessment/knowledge/dose callback order and no alternate closure. The whole reset store remains blocked throughout that action. Completion/removal runs the complete reset-store classifier under §5's current-auth scope: more than one fully valid mismatch-free row for the current signed-in UID returns a new conflict snapshot exposing the next minimum epoch; zero or one becomes ready only when no higher-priority I/O, structural, quarantine, cleanup, or actionable receipt-mismatch predicate remains, otherwise it returns that higher-priority `BlockedSnapshot`. Post-await drift likewise returns this complete `ready|blocked(snapshot)` reclassification and never fabricates a conflict snapshot when another scene already made the store ready. Numeric epoch order is server-authoritative causal reset order, not timestamp/array inference; no other heuristic chooses or deletes a row, no action touches another occupant, and no callback can run outside the selected validated reducer. A stale, absent, or nonminimum option returns exact local `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"RECOVERY_ACTION_UNAVAILABLE",store:"reset"}` with zero action. One in-flight key includes the complete digest and selected option; an identical call coalesces and a different call returns `RECOVERY_BUSY`. Fixtures include inverse-createdAt rows, a later-epoch attempt proving unavailable/zero callback before the minimum row completes, simultaneous conflict plus receipt mismatch proving zero epoch callback until mismatch reconciliation, auth switching to a foreign conflict, and another scene resolving the store to ready during the await.

The two local stale errors are exact `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"RESET_GESTURE_STALE",gestureId,gestureGeneration}` and `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"RESET_OPERATION_STALE",uid,handleId}`. They are never Firebase details and change no durable byte.

Before any local-key, discovered, or background migration row create, four other-UID migration rows returns exact no-write `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION_REGISTRY_FULL",capacity:4,occupants:[{uid,phase,recoveryAction}]}` in creation-time/UID order. Occupant phase is the exact migration phase; recoveryAction is `retry` for prepared/dispatched, `apply` for receipt/applying, and `resolve_blocked` for blocked. No migration row is evicted. A user-initiated reserve retains its already-durable gesture and surfaces this capacity recovery; background reconciliation returns it directly.

Member caps: active reset row 10,240 canonical bytes; migration row 10,240; gesture 2,048; payload/outer metadata 4,096. The algebraic upper bound is `4*10,240 + 4*10,240 + 2,048 + 4,096 = 88,064`; prove that bound plus B3's 190-byte reserve is ≤ the 131,072 complete-envelope cap. Independently generate and round-trip the maximum reachable byte count under the closed schemas and prove its headroom to that algebraic bound. Exact/+1 applies only to schema-reachable member encodings; otherwise exercise every underlying field/count cap +1 without padding or surplus members. The outer cap is defense-in-depth and is not independently reachable for reset. Each row stores at most one 8,192-byte server receipt; writing final receipt drops obsolete progress bytes atomically. Member-cap +1 preserves the prior file.

Fixtures cover every `ResetReserveOutcome` and `ResetInvocationOutcome`; concurrent reserve/bind; two scenes proposing different IDs during reserve and during bind, both receiving and using the persisted winner `Reservation`; different/stale IDs not carrying that winner; same gesture ID with wrong generation; stale/absent operation handle; wrong/mutated gesture token and unrelated outer-generation advance; auth switch at every suspension; credential rebase; transient failure/retry; equal/backward clock samples, clock failure, retry-before-commit, lost response, and handle stability across every fresh/transition branch; kill immediately before/after the reserved→bound write and immediately before/after bound→prepared removal; loaded-bound materialization with zero epoch reread; two callers joining before bind completion and after PREPARED durability then awaiting exactly one drive task with one cleanup/finalize/notification callback sequence; crash/relaunch after in-memory singleflight loss; crash before/after migration dispatch/receipt/applying proving upgraded/phase2-active materialize once, not-dispatched retires and requires a new gesture, and finalized-compat never starts a second reset; coexistence of one initiating reset row with migration PREPARED/DISPATCHED/RECEIPT/APPLYING/BLOCKED proving migration precedence and zero ordinary reset/finalize call before mapping; recovered/background migration beside a bound gesture proving structural block with zero materialization/call; every allowed and forbidden cross-member combination; background migration with no gesture; no reset dispatch before prepared durability; an existing row at every six-phase branch; every reset occupant/recoveryAction branch; four reset rows plus fifth UID; two and four same-UID/different-epoch rows returning ordered `RESET_EPOCH_CONFLICT` with zero selection/write/dispatch; duplicate, permuted, UID-mismatched, and equal-time legacy-migration order fixtures; four migration rows plus fifth user/background/bound-gesture attempt; account switch; no eviction; cap boundaries; and winner cleanup before loser continuation causing one epoch rotation.

Generation-fence fixtures pause device A before each inspection/callback and after query but before every candidate/root transaction while B finalizes and writes assessment, knowledge, Firestore dose, and local dose at epoch `r`; A performs zero delete of those bytes. They repeat with B starting reset `r→r+1`, proving A rejects B's marker while B may delete epoch-r and neither deletes epoch-r+1. Assessment/knowledge/dose each cover older/equal/newer epoch, exact unstamped 0→1, unstamped later reset, malformed, absent, marker absent, wrong operation, and marker change during transaction. Callback error/permission denial followed by committed inspection adopts final with no retry; identical pending preserves Retry. Committed-inspection direct-edge crashes before/after durability and replay apply with zero notification. Daily-dose local fixtures cover two scenes, stale-writer epoch/revision CAS, process reload, empty-floor creation, exact bridge order/crash, malformed preservation, and equal/newer preservation. Rules reject fresh missing/stale stamps, stamp change/removal, cleanup outside awaiting, cleanup of current/newer bytes, and a root cleanup changing anything except dailyDose. Account deletion independently removes the v2 key plus all legacy keys.

## 6. MIG-RESET-V1 / D30 — total server-mediated legacy reset reconciliation

Clients never read `taskPlanOperations`. A raw legacy operation ID is never imported as `ResetAliasV1`, and a legacy final response is never fabricated into a Phase 2 `reset_final`.

### 6.1 Exact legacy authority

The per-UID local key is `phase1.pendingRetakeOperation.{uid}`. Migration reads that exact key once with `object(forKey:)`: `nil` is absent; only a Swift `String` enters string validation; every other nonnil property-list value is `non_string`. It never uses `string(forKey:)` for classification. A String is accepted only when it is already byte-for-byte its committed `cleanDocId` result: nonempty; at most 256 UTF-8 bytes; contains no `/`; is neither `.` nor `..`; does not both begin and end with `__`; and begins/ends with none of exact ECMAScript trim code points U+0009, U+000A, U+000B, U+000C, U+000D, U+0020, U+00A0, U+1680, U+2000–U+200A, U+2028, U+2029, U+202F, U+205F, U+3000, or U+FEFF. The implementation performs no local trim and Foundation `.whitespacesAndNewlines` is forbidden. Any other String is `invalid_string`; malformed values become durable blocked migration state and are never silently cleared. Fixtures include absent, non-string plist classes, every listed edge trim code point, internal whitespace, `/`, `.`, `..`, reserved double-underscore form, and UTF-8 256/257. The literal accepted legacy fingerprint is `reset1_862fe3fc8a08ce3eced6f25dfd2a8765b408e8386fa28f081f393d36f04e94a1`.

Accepted legacy operation maps are exactly:

```text
L-deleting = {kind:"reset",fingerprint,deletedCount,state:"deleting",at}

L-tasks-deleted = {kind:"reset",fingerprint,deletedCount,state:"tasks_deleted",at,
 tasks_deleted:{reset:true,deletedCount,replayed:false}}

L-finalized = {kind:"reset",fingerprint,deletedCount,state:"finalized",at,
 tasks_deleted:{reset:true,deletedCount,replayed:false},
 finalized:{reset:true,deletedCount,replayed:false},finalizedAt}
```

`fingerprint = "reset1_" + SHA-256(committed legacy canonical({kind:"reset",reason:"retake_assessment"}))`; counts are equal safe nonnegative integers; times are Firestore timestamps.

Accepted legacy markers are exactly:

```text
M-deleting = {operationId,state:"deleting",deletedCount,
 workerLease:null|{workerId,expiresAt},startedAt}

M-awaiting = {operationId,state:"awaiting_local_reset",deletedCount,
 workerLease:null,startedAt}
```

`workerLease` is either literal null or exact `{workerId,expiresAt}`; worker ID is a lowercase RFC 4122 UUID and expiry is a Firestore Timestamp. For either active accepted pair, `M.operationId == requested legacyOperationId`, `M.deletedCount == L.deletedCount`, and `L.at == M.startedAt` byte-for-byte; a present worker lease additionally requires `expiresAt > startedAt` (it may be expired at migration time). Every nested `deletedCount` equals the operation's top-level count. Finalized additionally requires `finalizedAt >= at`.

The transaction reads the requested operation, the root marker, and—when a marker is present—the marker's operation before classifying. With no marker, requested L absent → `not_dispatched`; requested L-finalized → `finalized_compat`; requested L-deleting/tasks-deleted/malformed → `LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT`. With an exact legacy marker, the matching requested L-deleting/M-deleting or L-tasks-deleted/M-awaiting pair → `upgraded`. If that exact active legacy marker belongs to a different ID B, requested A absent or finalized returns exact no-write `failed-precondition/{schemaVersion:1,reason:"LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION_REQUIRED",legacyOperationId:B}`; requested A active/malformed is corruption. No migration record for A is created and no B/root byte changes. The local redirect rule in §6.5 then makes the immutable request about the actual active authority B before any further server call.

With an exact active Phase-2 marker C and its byte-matching canonical operation, a requested record that is absent, finalized legacy, or that same Phase-2 canonical record → permanent `phase2_active` for A carrying C's exact progress projection, without changing C/root. A requested legacy-active/malformed record beside C is corruption. Any requested Phase-2 record/tombstone that is not the byte-matching authority selected by that exact active marker is `LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT` with `recordClass:"phase2"` and zero writes. Every malformed/orphan marker, mismatched active pair, wrong count/fingerprint/time/lease relation, or active operation without its marker is likewise `LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT`, zero writes. Thus both a requested alias that is itself current Phase-2 authority and an unrelated current Phase-2 authority use the same durable `phase2_active` branch.

### 6.2 Deterministic migration authority, random alias, and action

The server authority identity is deterministic, but the reset alias is not. Define:

```text
migrationId = "rlm1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({
  account_uid:uid,legacy_operation_id:legacyOperationId
})))
requestFingerprint = "rlmreq1_" + SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({
  account_uid:uid,legacy_operation_id:legacyOperationId
}))
migrationAlias = "rsa1_" + <fresh lowercase RFC-4122 UUIDv4>
```

Freeze parity fixture: UID `phase2-rule-owner` and legacy ID `20000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001` yield migration ID `rlm1_0a4d53a438873090f69e4255426310b0feb4cfd6` and request fingerprint `rlmreq1_0a4d53a438873090f69e4255426310b0feb4cfd63eabd6ce68c851ce827ad8e1`; its alias is any separately validated fresh `rsa1_` UUIDv4 candidate.

`rlm1_` remains reserved on every client-supplied operation/claim/submission/reset/legacy ID surface, but reservation is defense in depth rather than the migration-authority namespace. Ordinary actions reject it after their frozen normalization and before any authenticated document lookup, using their existing `invalid-argument/REQUEST_INVALID` field. Raw legacy reset/finalize rejects normalized `rlm1_` as `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"REQUEST_INVALID",field:"operationId"}`; reconciliation rejects client-supplied `legacyOperationId` beginning `rlm1_` with `field:"legacyOperationId"`. The migration authority lives only at the new server-only path in §6.3, which no shipped client or old callable could preoccupy. A historical `taskPlanOperations/{migrationId}` document is never read as migration authority and cannot block reconciliation. Fixtures attempt every reserved prefix on every client ID surface, install every otherwise-valid historical old-location decoy, and prove only exact internal derivation can address the server-only parity path.

`changeTaskPlan` adds authenticated exact request `{action:"reconcileLegacyTaskReset",legacyOperationId,migrationAlias}`. The server validates `migrationAlias` as exact `ResetAliasV1` with `rsa1_` plus lowercase RFC-4122 UUIDv4 syntax; it never recomputes the random value. It derives `migrationId` and alias-free `requestFingerprint`, then reads `users/{uid}/legacyResetMigrations/{migrationId}` first. A valid matching record is replay authority and returns its stored `migration_alias`, even when it differs from the request candidate; malformed, wrong-owner, wrong-ID, wrong-kind, or wrong-fingerprint content is `OPERATION_REUSED`. Only when that path is absent does the transaction classify the legacy/root authority and, immediately before any record creation, read `users/{uid}/taskPlanOperations/{migrationAlias}`. An occupied proposed alias returns the exact zero-write collision below. A valid migration record always takes precedence over candidate occupancy, so concurrent devices converge on the winner's stored alias.

Success union is exact:

```text
{schemaVersion:1,kind:"legacy_reset_reconciliation",
 outcome:"not_dispatched",migrationId,legacyOperationId,migrationAlias,
 accountUid,replayed}

{schemaVersion:1,kind:"legacy_reset_reconciliation",
 outcome:"upgraded",migrationId,legacyOperationId,migrationAlias,
 accountUid,sourceState:"deleting"|"tasks_deleted",replayed,
 progressReceipt:<exact Phase-2 reset_progress>}

{schemaVersion:1,kind:"legacy_reset_reconciliation",
 outcome:"phase2_active",migrationId,legacyOperationId,migrationAlias,
 accountUid,replayed,
 progressReceipt:<exact Phase-2 reset_progress>}

{schemaVersion:1,kind:"legacy_reset_reconciliation",
 outcome:"finalized_compat",migrationId,legacyOperationId,migrationAlias,
 accountUid,sourceState:"finalized",replayed,
 legacyFinalReceipt:{schemaVersion:1,kind:"legacy_reset_final",
   operationId:legacyOperationId,replayed:false,accountUid,
   deletedCount,state:"finalized"}}
```

For `upgraded`, `progressReceipt.replayed` is literal `false` because that reconciliation transaction creates the canonical Phase-2 reset. For `phase2_active`, `progressReceipt.replayed` is literal `true` because it projects a preexisting canonical reset without mutating it. These inner values are stored permanently and never change; replay of the migration changes only outer `replayed:false→true`. An opposite or missing inner value is a protocol error and writes no local receipt. First-call and replay fixtures freeze both outcomes and both replay layers.

Only outer replay changes on reconciliation replay. The D30 actions plus cutoff/discovery branches use this closed mapping; existing common reasons retain the frozen member names, while the migration-specific rows extend §4.6. Every unshown member is forbidden:

| Firebase code | Exact details |
|---|---|
| `unauthenticated` | `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"AUTH_REQUIRED"}` |
| `invalid-argument` | `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"REQUEST_INVALID",field:"request"|"action"|"legacyOperationId"|"migrationAlias"}` |
| `failed-precondition` | `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"OPERATION_REUSED",operationId}` |
| `failed-precondition` | `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT",context:"reconcile",legacyOperationId,recordClass,markerClass}` |
| `failed-precondition` | `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT",context:"inspect",recordClass,markerClass}` |
| `failed-precondition` | `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION_REQUIRED",legacyOperationId}` |
| `failed-precondition` | `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"LEGACY_RESET_ALIAS_OCCUPIED",legacyOperationId,migrationAlias}` |
| `failed-precondition` | `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"CLIENT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED",requiredProtocol:"phase2"}` |

`operationId` in OPERATION_REUSED is the deterministic migration ID. Both corruption cases use recordClass `absent|deleting|tasks_deleted|finalized|phase2|malformed` and markerClass `absent|deleting|awaiting_local_reset|phase2|malformed`; reconcile requires its requested legacy ID, while inspection intentionally exposes none. `recordClass:"phase2"` classifies only an occupied/malformed derived canonical reset path; candidate alias occupancy has its own exact error and never becomes corruption. `RESET_ACTIVE` is not a legal reconciliation result; receipt of that error for this action is a protocol contradiction because exact active Phase-2 authority must be committed as `phase2_active`. `field:"request"` covers a non-map, missing/unknown/surplus member set; otherwise validation reports the first value-invalid field in request member order. Every semantic error, including alias occupancy, writes nothing. Transport loss has no synthetic result.

### 6.3 Permanent migration record and active upgrade

Add permanent client-denied server-authority member:

```text
{schema_version:1,kind:"LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION",account_uid,
 migration_id,legacy_operation_id,migration_alias,request_fingerprint,
 outcome:"not_dispatched"|"upgraded"|"phase2_active"|"finalized_compat",
 source_state?,expected_task_generation_epoch?,task_generation_epoch?,
 canonical_operation_id?,active_move_event_id?,progress_receipt?,
 legacy_final_receipt?,created_at}
```

Not-dispatched forbids source/epoch/canonical/move/receipts. Upgraded requires source deleting/tasks_deleted, adjacent safe epochs, canonical/move IDs, and progress; forbids legacy final. Phase2-active requires adjacent safe epochs, canonical/move IDs, and exact progress; forbids source state and legacy final. Finalized-compat requires source finalized and exact compatibility receipt; forbids epoch/canonical/move/progress. Cap 16,384 canonical bytes; no TTL; excluded from ordinary reset deletion. Its permanent path is exactly `users/{uid}/legacyResetMigrations/{migrationId}`; stored `migration_id == migrationId ==` the deterministic derivation, `account_uid == uid`, and the path ID is never `legacyOperationId`, `migrationAlias`, or the canonical reset ID. Create-only/exact-existing semantics apply. `LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION` is removed from the `taskPlanOperations` operation union; any such old-location document is an unrelated decoy. Original legacy operation stays byte-identical. This server-only record is the permanent raw-alias fence: every delayed legacy reset/finalize reads it before root or legacy-operation state. It is retained through reset completion and deleted only by the recursive account-deletion sweep.

Every fresh reconciliation transaction uses the frozen retry-attempt `migrationAt = server_write_time`; retry obtains a new value, while exact replay preserves committed bytes. Transaction precedence is literal: (1) read the server-only migration path; (2) replay a complete matching record using its stored alias, regardless of the proposed candidate; (3) reject malformed/mismatched presence as OPERATION_REUSED; (4) only when absent, classify all legacy/root authority; (5) before any create, read the proposed alias path; (6) return exact zero-write LEGACY_RESET_ALIAS_OCCUPIED if it is present; (7) otherwise commit reconciliation and the authority record atomically. For an active pair, the same transaction reads effective root epoch `e`, requires safe `r=e+1`, derives the frozen `rso1_` canonical ID, and requires that derived canonical path absent. Unsafe/exhausted epoch or occupied/malformed derived canonical path returns zero-write reconcile-context `LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT` with `recordClass:"phase2"`; complete-content adoption is forbidden because no atomic migration record exists. The transaction then creates the **ordinary exact Phase 2 active reset record with no migration-only surplus member**, first alias `migrationAlias`, `target_index:0`, no cursor/lease/awaiting time, legacy committed count in `deleted_counts.tasks`, other counts zero, sum equality, `created_at=L.at`, and `updated_at=migrationAt`. Restart at target zero catches tasks inserted after the legacy empty check without recounting earlier deletes. It replaces the legacy marker with the exact canonical marker with `createdAt=L.at`, `updatedAt=migrationAt`, and no awaiting time; rotates root epoch `e→r`; and derives `activeMoveEventId` by the frozen deterministic formula `"me1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({uid,new_task_generation_epoch:r,reset_operation_id:canonicalId})))`. It creates the server-only migration record with `created_at=migrationAt` and returns the exact committed progress projection.

Not-dispatched, phase2-active, and finalized-compat records also use `created_at=migrationAt`. An unrelated exact legacy B produces only the no-write migration-required redirect above and therefore creates no permanent A record. When §6.1 proves active Phase-2 C, the transaction validates C/root/operation completely and writes only A's `phase2_active` migration record carrying C's exact progress, preserving every C/root byte. All committed effects are atomic. Fixtures cover safe-int maximum/+1 epoch, occupied alias, canonical active/tombstone/malformed paths, A-absent and A-finalized redirects beside exact legacy B with zero server write, phase2-active beside C, and commit-response loss.

Every old worker rereads marker ID/state/lease before delete/transition. The upgrade transaction changes that document, so an already-open transaction conflicts and any retry loses the marker CAS. Delayed raw calls see migration first. The imported reset then uses the unchanged four-target server reducer. A tasks-deleted import repeats protected local cleanup if required; each destructive client step uses §5's fresh inspection plus exact-marker/generation transaction and can delete only data older than the imported result epoch.

Finalized legacy is compatibility-terminal: do not rotate epoch, delete server tasks, rerun unfenced local destructive cleanup, or invent e/r; successor task/assessment/knowledge/dose work may now be legitimate. Server finalization alone is not proof of cleanup, but the supported migration case additionally has the retained per-UID key written by the shipped coordinator whose enforced order was cleanup before finalize. That pair is compatibility evidence only; it authorizes non-destructive reload/notification, never another delete. A direct/forged finalize without that local authority is not auto-repaired. The permanent record fences replay. A `reserve()` invocation that discovers finalized-compat is consumed by that compatibility completion and returns `LEGACY_RESET_COMPLETED`; it may not mint or dispatch a fresh Phase 2 reset in the same user invocation. Only a later intentional gesture performs the next real rotation.

### 6.4 Cross-device discovery and cutoff

Before a fresh Phase 2 reset creates a canonical record, it reads the root marker. An exact legacy marker returns the exact no-write `failed-precondition/LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION_REQUIRED` member above. The caller has already durably advanced its ordinary row to `reset_dispatched`; Swift retains that row, draws one fresh migration alias, and creates migration PREPARED bound by `initiatingAuthority.kind:"reset_dispatched"` before reconciliation. A local-key path instead reaches migration while its initiating gesture is still reserved and does the same durable draw/write ordering. Thus both entry paths have durable initiating authority and alias candidate before the migration await.

For either `upgraded` or `phase2_active`, validate the complete progress receipt, including safe `progressReceipt.expectedTaskGenerationEpoch == e`, `progressReceipt.taskGenerationEpoch == e + 1`, canonical operation-ID formula, account, move-event formula, state, and counts. `upgraded` has already rotated `e→e+1`; `phase2_active` has only observed an existing exact rotation. First durably persist migration RECEIPT while retaining its initiating authority. Then one atomic local `receipt→applying` envelope write materializes the ordinary `reset_receipt` row: reserved-gesture authority creates it with the gesture alias and removes the gesture; reset-dispatched authority transforms that exact no-receipt/no-canonical row in place using its retained alias/e; absent/background authority creates it under `migrationAlias`. All three store the validated canonical ID and exact progress receipt. After that complete envelope is durable, the same fully validated response/inspection atomically installs or updates two process-local provenance entries against its new generation/hash: LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION for the complete durable migration receipt, and RESET with `authority:{operationId:<canonicalOperationId>}`, `requestAuthority:{requestFingerprint:<the exact "reset1_" derivation at e>}`, `identityDigest = lowercase SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({kind:"reset",uid,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch:e}))`, and `receiptSHA256` over the exact nested progress-receipt map. Removing the migration row after APPLYING preserves the still-matching RESET entry and updates it to the replacement envelope generation/hash. Loaded APPLYING requires exact existing reset-row equality; disagreement enters B3 receipt reconciliation and writes nothing. After relaunch both entries are absent: classification exposes/reconciles the migration row first; a committed migration inspection that validates the nested reset materialization reinstalls both entries before application. If the migration was already removed, ordinary RESET inspection alone restores the reset provenance. Neither outcome calls `ResetEpochAuthorityProviding` or dispatches `resetAllTasks` before this row is durable. The unchanged reducer thereafter resumes with the retained alias/e against the existing canonical operation and can never address `(e+1)→(e+2)`. Fixtures kill immediately before/after dual installation, before/after migration removal, and with another retained APPLYING row, proving no unsafe application and no provenance ping-pong.

Deployment order: add exact immutable deployment environment `PHASE2_RESET_PROTOCOL_MODE`, whose only values are `compat` and `phase2_required`; missing or unknown value fails module initialization and its deployment health check, so no reset handler starts and no unlisted wire response is invented. Before deploying or routing any revision that contains the mutating reconciliation action, revoke external invocation of the currently deployed `changeTaskPlan` service from every principal and record the proved revocation-effective time. While invocation remains revoked, deploy one distinct compat revision containing the decoder/action/server-only migration authority/raw fence/rules/tests, shift 100% traffic to it, and verify that revision has no external invoker. Let `Tzero` be the proved termination time of every pre-compat/older execution. The deployed package must pin `@google-cloud/firestore` exactly `7.11.6`, and its loaded `firestore_client_config.json` must have SHA-256 `2ed7a9046d766121b7f308b22384b1a6caaf7c6eaed5d9ea553556af3c987e41` with `Commit.timeout_millis == 60000`; package/config mismatch blocks deployment. After `Tzero`, wait a full 60,000 ms Commit RPC window before the first migration-record creation/legacy-authority clear, invocation restoration, random-alias generation by an official client, or client release. If invocation revocation or `Tzero` cannot be proved, undeploy every old revision, prove zero old instances and no external invocation path, then begin the same 60,000 ms window. The old callable's 540-second function timeout alone is not a settle proof. Still revoked and only after the settle window, deploy a distinct `phase2_required` revision with the required value baked in and shift 100% traffic to it. Warm-instance mutation of one revision's environment is forbidden. Calls during maintenance receive platform unavailable; no client version or distribution state is treated as enforcement. Restore invocation only to the required revision and then release the client; neither compat nor any older revision regains an invoker. This server-admission sequence—not a client-local clock or `Tzero` claim—is the proof that a mutating reconciliation and its candidate alias occur only after the old reset revisions are drained. In required mode, fresh raw-legacy reset with no existing legacy op returns the exact no-write `failed-precondition/CLIENT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED` member above; an existing exact legacy op may finish until reconciled. Once any Phase 2/migration record exists, rollback may retain required mode and the compatibility bridge but may never restore fresh legacy creation. Deployment fixtures launch the final pre-compat Commit at callable-timeout minus epsilon and settle it at `Tzero + 60,000 ms`; prove no request—including a hand-crafted authenticated reconciliation request—can reach compat, required, or any older revision during the drain; and prove no migration, invocation restoration, required-mode routing, official-client alias generation, or client release is admitted earlier.

The raw fence is transactional and precedes every root-marker or legacy-operation mutation. The raw request retains its committed legacy validation behavior, including ECMAScript trimming; after `cleanDocId` succeeds, the handler uses the **returned normalized cleanDocId string**, never the raw request bytes, as `legacyOperationId` for `migrationId(uid,legacyOperationId)` and reads that literal permanent path in the same transaction as the contemplated raw reset/finalize write. Absence follows the mode/legacy-state rules above. A byte-exact `finalized_compat` migration plus its byte-exact finalized legacy operation returns the original old wire `{reset:true,deletedCount,replayed:true}` and writes nothing. A byte-exact `not_dispatched`, `upgraded`, or `phase2_active` migration returns exact no-write `failed-precondition/{schemaVersion:1,reason:"CLIENT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED",requiredProtocol:"phase2"}` for every delayed raw start, resume, or finalize. A present wrong-kind, wrong-shape, wrong-fingerprint, or deterministic-ID/content disagreement returns exact no-write `failed-precondition/OPERATION_REUSED` naming the deterministic migration ID. Compatibility fixtures cover a raw ID with leading/trailing ECMAScript whitespace fencing the normalized operation, raw reset and finalize at absence and each four valid outcomes, malformed/collision, response replay, and prove the fence read precedes every root/old-operation read-for-write and mutation.

### 6.5 Per-UID local migration

```text
LegacyResetMigrationV1 =
 {schemaVersion:1,uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision,
 legacyOperationId,migrationAlias,aliasCandidateOrdinal,requestFingerprint,
 legacyKeyGuard,initiatingAuthority?,
  phase:"prepared"|"dispatched"|"receipt"|"applying"|"blocked",
  requestCanonicalJSON,requestSHA256,receipt?,applicationId?,errorCode?,
  createdAt,updatedAt}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision,
  initiatingAuthority?,
  phase:"blocked",errorCode:"LEGACY_ALIAS_INVALID",
  legacyValueClass:"non_string"|"invalid_string",
  legacyValueUtf8Length?,legacyValueSHA256?,createdAt,updatedAt}
```

`initiatingAuthority` is absent for a background migration or after the exact same-UID/new-auth-epoch rebind rule has discarded an old reserved gesture; otherwise it is exact `{kind:"reserved_gesture",gestureId,gestureGeneration,alias}` or `{kind:"reset_dispatched",expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,suggestedOperationId}`. The reserved member must byte-match the sole reserved gesture. The dispatched member must byte-match the same UID's `reset_dispatched` registry row whose canonical ID and progress receipt are both absent; it is created only after that exact call returns `LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION_REQUIRED`. The binding is immutable through RECEIPT, except that same-UID auth rebind may atomically remove an old-epoch reserved gesture and clear only the reserved initiating member. APPLYING always forbids `initiatingAuthority`: every `receipt→applying` outcome atomically retires or materializes the referenced authority and removes the member in that same envelope write. The second schema member is the only legal invalid-alias representation and forbids `legacyOperationId`, `migrationAlias`, request members, receipt, application, and every other error code; it permits only absent or reserved-gesture initiating authority. A non-string forbids both value metadata members, while an invalid string requires its UTF-8 byte length and lowercase SHA-256 but never stores the value.

`aliasCandidateOrdinal` is exactly integer `1|2|3|4` and is required in every complete reconciliation-row phase, including BLOCKED; the invalid-alias union member forbids it. Each candidate draw consumes its ordinal even if the random generator repeats a prior UUID; no hidden resampling exists. `legacyKeyGuard` is immutable and exact: `{kind:"absent"}` for typed/background discovery with no key; `{kind:"valid_string",value}` where value is the exact accepted local String and equals the row's initial requested legacy ID; or `{kind:"invalid_value",valueClass:"non_string"|"invalid_string",utf8Length?,sha256?}` with the same presence rules and metadata as the invalid-alias row. Transition from invalid inspection to a server-returned valid ID carries the invalid guard unchanged and never stores the invalid value. APPLYING performs compare-and-remove: absent current key succeeds; a valid guard removes only an exact Swift String match; an invalid-string guard removes only an exact UTF-8 length/digest match. A non-string guard is never auto-removed and requires the fresh explicit inspect-none confirmation below. A different current value remains for a later independent migration. Migration completion never unconditionally deletes a changed key.

The first member requires `requestCanonicalJSON` to be the exact UTF-8 string of `TaskCanonicalV1({action:"reconcileLegacyTaskReset",legacyOperationId,migrationAlias})`; `requestSHA256` is its full lowercase SHA-256. The separately stored/validated `requestFingerprint` is the alias-free §6.2 derivation and is never the complete-request hash. RECEIPT requires one complete success from the four-member reconciliation union and forbids `errorCode`; `replayed:false` requires exact `receipt.migrationAlias == migrationAlias`, while `replayed:true` may return the different alias stored by a winning device. In every branch, background materialization uses `receipt.migrationAlias`; reserved-gesture and reset-dispatched materialization retain their already-bound initiating aliases. APPLYING retains the same receipt. `applicationId` is required exactly for `not_dispatched|finalized_compat`, forbidden for `upgraded|phase2_active`, and equals `"rla1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({uid,migration_id:receipt.migrationId,outcome:receipt.outcome})))`. PREPARED/DISPATCHED forbid receipt/application/error; BLOCKED requires exactly `LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT|OPERATION_REUSED|LEGACY_ALIAS_COLLISION_EXHAUSTED` and forbids receipt/application. Legal durable transitions are `prepared→dispatched→receipt→applying→removed`, self-retry in dispatched/applying, the exact redirect below, semantic malformed authority→blocked, the explicit `blocked/LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT→prepared` Retry edge, and the collision-only backward edge below. The corruption Retry preserves `uid`, current auth tuple, legacy ID, alias, ordinal, guard, initiating authority, request bytes/hash, and `createdAt` byte-for-byte; it removes `errorCode`, sets `updatedAt=writeTime`, and makes no call until PREPARED is durable. `OPERATION_REUSED` has no transition. Only a later explicitly confirmed `reserve()` may reset `LEGACY_ALIAS_COLLISION_EXHAUSTED` by drawing a new candidate, setting ordinal 1, recomputing request bytes/hash, and atomically writing PREPARED under the retained or newly attached exact consent; background/startup never resets that cap. One `inflightLegacyMigration` per UID is coalesced. For a user-initiated reserve with a local key, reserve first durably adopts/writes that invocation's `ResetGestureV1`, then draws ordinal 1 and creates migration bound to that reserved authority. Discovery from an ordinary reset similarly binds its already-durable `reset_dispatched` row before the first call. Background migration may create ordinal 1 with no initiating authority.

A completely validated zero-write `LEGACY_RESET_ALIAS_OCCUPIED` received for the immutable request in DISPATCHED is the sole automatic backward edge. At ordinal 1–3, draw exactly one fresh UUIDv4, increment the ordinal, rewrite `migrationAlias`, `requestCanonicalJSON`, and `requestSHA256`, and atomically CAS `dispatched→prepared`; dispatch waits for those complete bytes to become durable. At ordinal 4, atomically enter BLOCKED with `LEGACY_ALIAS_COLLISION_EXHAUSTED`. A crash before the CAS resumes the same DISPATCHED candidate; a crash after it resumes the new PREPARED candidate. Transport ambiguity remains DISPATCHED and retries the same candidate without consuming an ordinal. A collision detail whose legacy ID or alias does not exactly equal the durable request is a protocol failure and leaves DISPATCHED unchanged.

A completely validated `LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION_REQUIRED(B)` returned for a DISPATCHED request A, with valid `B != A`, permits one atomic redirect `dispatched(A)→prepared(B)`: draw one fresh alias for B, set `aliasCandidateOrdinal:1`, recompute `requestCanonicalJSON(B)` and `requestSHA256(B)`, preserve `uid`, current auth tuple, `legacyKeyGuard`, `initiatingAuthority`, and `createdAt`, set `updatedAt=writeTime`, and forbid receipt/application/error. No alias or ordinal from A is inherited. If B is invalid/equal A or the stored row/request/response/auth CAS differs, write nothing. Dispatch waits for complete PREPARED(B) durability. Crash before the atomic write resumes A; crash after it resumes B.

For a migration row, signed-out or a different current UID permits no new dispatch or APPLYING work. An already-returned response may advance only that existing original-UID row to RECEIPT after complete request/response/phase CAS validation; it never mutates current-account authority, and application waits until the row's UID is current. When that UID becomes current again under a different auth epoch, atomically rebind only the migration row to current `authEpochUUID/credentialRevision` while preserving immutable request, phase, receipt, and application bytes. In that same write, an old-epoch reserved gesture is discarded under B2 and its reserved `initiatingAuthority` member is cleared; a materialized/reset-dispatched row is retained under its own recovery rule. Then resume/retry the same deterministic migration. Within one auth epoch, current credential revision must be at least stored; a lower revision is corrupt, while a higher revision atomically rebases the migration row and any matching reserved gesture before dispatch or in the same envelope write that persists a post-await receipt. A newly returned response persists only against that rebased tuple. Fixtures force refresh before dispatch and during the reconciliation await, response arrival after account switch, revision rollback, and sign-out→same-UID/new-epoch at prepared, dispatched-ambiguous, receipt, and applying.

`LEGACY_ALIAS_INVALID` alone offers authenticated read-only exact request `{action:"inspectLegacyTaskReset"}`. Exact success union is `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"legacy_reset_inspection",outcome:"none",accountUid}`; or the same prefix with outcome `legacy_active` plus `legacyOperationId`; or outcome `phase2_active` plus `canonicalOperationId,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch`. Unknown/surplus members reject. Malformed/root disagreement uses the exact inspect-context `LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT` error above. Background/startup may inspect but never clear; only a migration carrying the durable reserved-gesture initiating authority attached by an existing confirmed Reset Assessment invocation may execute either clear outcome. `legacy_active(B)` draws one fresh alias and atomically replaces the invalid blocked row with complete PREPARED(B) bytes at `aliasCandidateOrdinal:1`, carrying its initiating authority and invalid `legacyKeyGuard`; B and the server remain untouched until normal reconciliation. A String guard may be compare-removed after B applies; a non-string guard remains for a later fresh explicit clear.

For `phase2_active(C)`, require safe `r=expectedTaskGenerationEpoch+1`, validate C's canonical formula/account/root-marker equality, and interpret the already-durable reserved initiating authority as consent for **Clear obsolete local legacy value and resume active reset**. The actor first revalidates the exact inspection authority, then compare-removes the exact invalid-string guard or freshly rereads and explicitly clears the current non-string value. If the guarded value drifted, the old confirmation never clears the changed bytes or creates server/reset authority: one atomic envelope write retires the stale migration, its initiating authority, and the exact gesture, leaves the current key untouched, and returns `legacyRetryRequired` with `applicationId = "rlic1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({uid,legacy_value_class,legacy_value_utf8_length?,legacy_value_sha256?,outcome:"legacy_value_changed"})))`, using only the stored old guard's optional metadata. A later Settings confirmation creates a new gesture and classifies the current value; no old consent survives. On a guard match or absence, the actor next CASes the exact blocked envelope and writes ordinary local adoption authority before any mutating reset call. Absence is the admitted crash-resume state after an earlier matching clear, not drift. It uses only the stored reserved alias and removes the gesture in the same write; initiating-authority absence is not clear-authorized and cannot enter this branch. That atomic envelope write creates the exact no-receipt/no-canonical `reset_dispatched` row for `(uid,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch)` with `createdAt=gesture.reservedAt` and `updatedAt=writeTime`, and removes the invalid migration row and its initiating authority completely. No pre-APPLYING migration row may coexist with the adopted row. It never stores C as a caller alias and never calls `ResetEpochAuthorityProviding`. A same-key unequal row or four-row capacity leaves the migration/gesture blocked for retry even when the already-confirmed key clear succeeded. Crash after key clear but before the envelope CAS re-inspects under the retained durable consent and accepts the now-absent guarded key; crash after CAS resumes the sole ordinary row. Relaunch reuses the persisted alias/epoch and retries ordinary `resetAllTasks`, which alone may append the alias and resolve C active or its epoch-keyed tombstone; it cannot rotate `(e+1)→(e+2)` and no second alias is minted. Fixtures cover background invalid → confirmed reserve → durable gesture/authority attachment; no migration-row coexistence; ordinary completion; kill before authority attachment, before key clear, after key clear, and after adoption durability; capacity/conflict after clear; invalid→different-invalid and invalid→valid drift retiring authority without clearing and requiring a new gesture; invalid→absent accepting the crash-resume path; C finalizing between inspection and confirmation; and response loss.

`none` permits clear only under the same durable reserved-gesture consent after the exact read proves no destructive reset active. Guard drift uses the exact `legacy_value_changed` retirement/result above: remove migration/authority/gesture atomically, leave the new key untouched, and require a new Settings confirmation. On a guard match/absence it compare-clears first, then one envelope CAS removes the blocked row, its initiating authority, and that exact gesture together; a crash after clear/before CAS resumes from the retained row/consent and accepts absence. It never binds or dispatches that invocation. Its deterministic local result is `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"LEGACY_RESET_RETRY_REQUIRED",uid,applicationId}` where `applicationId = "rlic1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({uid,legacy_value_class,legacy_value_utf8_length?,legacy_value_sha256?,outcome:"none"})))` with absent optional members omitted. Historical finalized records are not resume authority without their alias. Server unavailability, malformed inspection, ambiguous authority, or background inspection without consent stays blocked with no clear/write. Fixtures prove first corrupt Retry success, repeated corruption reblocking, `OPERATION_REUSED` never transitioning, background inspection never clearing, and crash after drift retirement leaving no row/gesture/clear before the next invocation creates a new gesture.

Local `LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT` offers only Retry of the same immutable reconciliation request; it never replaces requested A with an inspected B while A authority is unresolved. This edge applies only to the complete reconciliation-row schema. An inspect-context `LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT`, malformed inspection response, transport loss, or unavailable inspection has no legacy ID/alias authority: it leaves the complete invalid-alias row byte-identical, surfaces `RESET_MIGRATION_BLOCKED/LEGACY_ALIAS_INVALID`, and may retry only `inspectLegacyTaskReset`; it never enters blocked→PREPARED reconciliation Retry. `OPERATION_REUSED` remains nonrecoverable because the deterministic server-only authority record is malformed or mismatched. A changed server state may let exact reconciliation retry reach one of the four committed success outcomes; otherwise both remain blocked. `RESET_ACTIVE` is never mapped locally for reconciliation.

Order for a UID with no migration row: classify the key once; draw ordinal-1 alias; durably PREPARED; durably DISPATCHED before await; handle only an exact collision through the bounded backward edge; validate complete response/request hash/UID/IDs/formulas/counts and replay-dependent alias rule; durably RECEIPT; atomically enter APPLYING plus outcome mapping; compare-and-remove only the guarded UserDefaults value after APPLYING durability; remove migration only after comparison and all state-derived effects. On startup, an existing valid `LegacyResetMigrationV1` row always resumes from its immutable request/phase before the key is reread or classified; the key is only compare/remove authority during APPLYING. A crash immediately after invalid inspection creates PREPARED(B) therefore resumes B and cannot regress to the invalid row; its carried key guard decides cleanup.

`receipt→applying` is one atomic envelope write and always removes `initiatingAuthority` from the migration row. For `not_dispatched`, a present member retires exactly that no-commit authority—removes the matching reserved gesture or the exact no-receipt/no-canonical `reset_dispatched` row that received `LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION_REQUIRED`. An absent member is legal only for a background row and requires that no gesture or reset row references/binds this migration; it removes none. Both branches create no reset row, perform no epoch read/call, and store the deterministic application ID. They never preserve or rebind that invocation. For `upgraded|phase2_active`, the same write performs §6.4's exact receipt-derived materialization without consulting current epoch and removes the initiating member: upgraded adopts the rotation already committed by migration, while phase2-active adopts the existing canonical rotation and commits no server mutation. Existing-row disagreement enters B3 with zero write. For `finalized_compat`, a present member removes the exact initiating reserved gesture or exact no-receipt/no-canonical reset-dispatched row it names; an absent member requires the same background no-gesture/no-row proof and removes none. Both create no reset row, remove the initiating member, and store the application ID before non-destructive compatibility application. Any present-authority mismatch blocks with no write. Background application returns no user result; user application returns completed.

After APPLYING is durable, UserDefaults cleanup uses `legacyKeyGuard`, never blind deletion. Absence succeeds.
