Exact valid String or invalid-string length/digest match is removed; a different value remains. A non-string is never auto-removed by another migration and requires the fresh explicit inspection-none confirmation above. Crash before/after removal repeats the comparison. Only after comparison/application finishes may the migration row be removed.

A user-initiated not-dispatched application returns exact local `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"LEGACY_RESET_RETRY_REQUIRED",uid,applicationId}` and the coordinator surfaces a non-destructive retry-required result; only a later independently generated gesture may bind. Background not-dispatched returns no user result. Finalized-compat returns the existing completed result. Upgraded/phase2-active continue only through the materialized reset row.

For finalized-compat, `.retakeAssessment` is eligible only when the exact locally persisted compatibility receipt has outer `replayed:false`, and only the in-memory transition that commits RECEIPT→APPLYING may post it, at most once. A `replayed:true` response—including server commit/response loss, reconstruction after local-store loss, or a reappearing UserDefaults key after prior completion—performs non-destructive reload/routing but never posts. A row loaded already APPLYING never reposts; it reloads authoritative assessment/task state, performs only state-derived routing if needed, then removes the row. Ephemeral notification loss across a crash is permitted; duplicate notification/accounting is forbidden. Offline/auth-unavailable/ambiguous transport retains key/phase. A response after account switch may persist under its original UID under the exact rule above but cannot apply until that UID is current. Blocked refuses reserve with the exact local error/recovery already defined; no destructive discard exists.

Local-only surface shapes are exact and are never decoded as Firebase details:

```text
{schemaVersion:1,kind:"RESET_MIGRATION_PENDING",uid,
 phase:"prepared"|"dispatched"|"receipt"|"applying"}

{schemaVersion:1,kind:"LEGACY_RESET_COMPLETED",uid,applicationId}

{schemaVersion:1,kind:"LEGACY_RESET_RETRY_REQUIRED",uid,applicationId}

{schemaVersion:1,reason:"RESET_MIGRATION_BLOCKED",uid,
 errorCode:"LEGACY_ALIAS_INVALID"|"LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT"|
           "LEGACY_ALIAS_COLLISION_EXHAUSTED"|"OPERATION_REUSED"}
```

Reserve normally awaits the per-UID singleflight; if transient/auth/server authority prevents progress, it returns the pending member for the durable phase. Not-dispatched returns retry-required only after its idempotent application ID has finished; finalized-compat analogously returns completed. A blocked row returns only the blocked error/recovery above. Swift maps `LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION_REQUIRED` to initial preparation or the exact A→B redirect; exact `LEGACY_RESET_ALIAS_OCCUPIED` follows only the bounded DISPATCHED collision reducer; AUTH_REQUIRED retains bytes and becomes pending; only committed `upgraded|phase2_active` enters receipt-derived canonical materialization; `RESET_ACTIVE` is a protocol contradiction and retains the immutable phase; OPERATION_REUSED becomes nonrecoverably blocked; LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT blocks with same-request Retry; LEGACY_ALIAS_INVALID offers inspection; exhausted collision offers only a later confirmed-reserve reset. CLIENT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED is never retried through a raw legacy action and, when a retained legacy ID exists, enters reconciliation. REQUEST_INVALID after local canonical validation becomes LEGACY_RESET_CORRUPT. Unknown/malformed details retain durable state. Fixtures cover every server code/details member, surplus/missing/wrong-code rejection, and every local mapping.

### 6.6 Coordinator scope and fixtures

Authorize `Peezy 4.0/MainInterface/Models/RetakeAssessmentCoordinator.swift` to remove UserDefaults operation identity from the execution path and call registry reserve as the first asynchronous operation. Preserve the public `retake() async throws -> Void` shape because the protected settings caller ignores a return value. `retake()` switches exhaustively on both `ResetReserveOutcome` and any later `ResetInvocationOutcome`: `binding` → bind → drive; `operation` → drive; `completed|legacyCompleted` → return only after the already-frozen application; `migrationPending(payload)` → throw coordinator-local `Error.migrationPending(payload)` with fixed message `"Your reset is safely queued. Reopen Settings to continue when you're online."`; `legacyRetryRequired(payload)` → throw `Error.legacyRetryRequired(payload)` with fixed message `"No prior reset was committed. Start Reset Assessment again to begin a new reset."`. Both error cases retain their complete typed local-only payload, are non-destructive, and flow through the existing settings `catch` so spinner/error presentation is not a silent success. It never calls cleanup or TaskPlanService outside `drive`. The handle UID supplies all three cleanup calls; only the actor-owned materialized registry alias/epoch reaches TaskPlanService. Remove the obsolete operation-store dependency from coordinator production construction.

Before any product edit, read clean-worktree UTF-8/LF bytes. Complete `Peezy 4.0/Menu/PeezySettingsView.swift` must initially have SHA-256 `419055c9eb0e756daf70f68aeb37d5761888460a65f4c129243fe3f28a4c34d5`. The complete pre-edit `retakeAssessment()` function slice is exactly lines 662–679 and has SHA-256 `348ec7db63aeecb76e7012d4fac91e41b60df78562a32b9528a5e40553a5a2e2`; those complete bytes, count, and order must be identical after the patch. The file's sole authorized hunk is the unique complete function slice beginning with the LF-terminated line `    private func deleteAccount() {` and ending with that function's matching complete LF-terminated `    }` line. The pre-edit slice is exactly lines 681–712 and has SHA-256 `8f86efa8523960588cfe8a17d6351645cba50bb14f4258a77a4dc8ccf98a5bbf`; the next two complete lines must be exactly one blank LF and `    // MARK: - Helpers\n`, and the replacement must preserve both. Removing only the function slice from the pre-edit file yields SHA-256 `ef856d3bf2f89b79d4340e5b4afe8e3844b13bb6167854486d5919d83b37e4cc`, and removing the replacement function by the same parsed matching-brace boundary must yield that identical hash after the patch. The replacement preserves the existing UI progress/error behavior but delegates the operation to `Phase2ProductionRuntime.accountDeletionCoordinator`; it contains no direct Functions/Auth/store/presentation call and follows §11's durable-intent→server-data-authority→eight-owner-ack→preferences barrier→client-telemetry barrier→Auth finalization→durable terminal presentation→conditional-sign-out→all-scope-purge order. Settings never owns the post-sign-out UI. The process-wide presentation owner exhaustively consumes the exact completed/remote-unconfirmed union and projects both immutable Apple and Google dispositions after the gate clears. Zero/multiple start markers, a nonunique/unbalanced function boundary, different adjacent lines, any outside-slice byte change, any retake-slice change, or a direct `httpsCallable("deleteAccount")` remaining in the view is fatal.

In `RetakeAssessmentCoordinator.swift`, locate the unique source slice beginning with the 12-space-indented line `deleteAssessments: { userId in` and ending with the `resetDose` closure's exact 12-space-indented `},` line, inclusive of its final LF. Before the patch, the immediately following complete LF-terminated line must be exactly `            operationStore: store,`, and the slice preimage must have SHA-256 `a7cf81cab9bfa9c49e5dba2c12c618bbfe309818284156f9594b0be127515e93`; mismatch, zero/multiple marker matches, or different adjacency is fatal. The former same-hash-after-edit requirement is withdrawn because UID-only closures cannot carry exact cleanup authority. One exact replacement is authorized: all three closures accept `ResetLocalCleanupAuthorityV1`; assessment and knowledge perform the §5 per-document exact-marker transactions, and dose calls only `DailyDoseEngine.resetForRetake(authority:)`; the next complete line becomes `            postNotification: {`. The obsolete `operationStore` line is removed. No closure may accept only UID or retain an authority beyond its one invocation. The separately authorized registry/coordinator reserve-bind-drive rewrite remains in scope; Settings `retakeAssessment()` bytes remain unchanged. A test recorder requires the only complete same-process trace to be exactly `resetDispatch,inspect,deleteAssessments,inspect,deleteUserKnowledge,inspect,resetDose,inspect,finalize,notification`; any committed inspection short-circuits the remaining suffix and posts no notification. Fixtures mutate the preimage/replacement boundary, introduce any direct UID-only delete, and duplicate/remove/reorder every inspect or callback to prove the guards fail.

Fixtures cover every accepted/rejected legacy cross-product and member; server-only-authority-first replay; literal IDs/fingerprint; historical `taskPlanOperations/{migrationId}` decoy ignored; active upgrades from both states; old leased-worker conflict; insertion after tasks-deleted; all server/local crash boundaries; two devices proposing different aliases with exactly one authority record/epoch rotation and the loser adopting the winner's response alias; a valid existing record winning even when the request candidate path is occupied; candidates 1–4 occupied with crash before/after every DISPATCHED→PREPARED CAS, exact exhausted block, no background cap reset, and a later confirmed reserve resetting to ordinal 1; transport ambiguity retaining the same candidate; discovery without local key from a durable reset-dispatched row; not-dispatched removing either initiating-authority shape and requiring a fresh gesture; finalized arriving between migration-required and reconciliation and consuming that row; finalized-compat with absent background authority and present user authority; A-absent/A-finalized beside active B redirecting to B with a fresh ordinal-1 alias; crash immediately before/after A→B redirect and repeated redirect; phase2-active response loss and crash; valid-string key changed only by whitespace before compare/remove; non-string changed to another non-string and never auto-removed; raw fence reading the new path before root/legacy mutation; account deletion removing the new collection and preventing recreation; finalized no rotation/delete; malformed key blocked; exact corrupt Retry to PREPARED and forbidden Retry for OPERATION_REUSED; account switch; reserve during every migration phase; coordinator call order; and no path from UserDefaults to TaskPlanService. Every authority-consuming transition proves the referenced row/gesture and `initiatingAuthority` disappear atomically and that no APPLYING row retains it. Finalized-compat fixtures prove first `replayed:false` receipt posts once, commit/response loss followed by `replayed:true` posts zero, and key reappearance/server replay posts zero while all three perform the allowed reload/routing. Ordinary-reset fixtures kill before/after final-receipt durability, APPLYING durability, and posting; exercise `replayed:true` after server commit/response loss and loaded FINAL_RECEIPT/APPLYING; and prove zero pre-durability or duplicate notification. The mandatory upgraded-with-gesture fixture begins at root epoch `e`, commits migration to root `r=e+1`, then proves local materialization adopts receipt epoch `e`, performs no epoch-authority read or pre-durability reset dispatch, and every continuation addresses only the existing canonical `r` record—never `r→r+1`. It kills immediately before/after migration RECEIPT and immediately before/after `receipt→applying`, proving the gesture survives RECEIPT and loaded APPLYING accepts only exact reset-row equality. Production/oracle also prove the exact 5,872-byte index registry and direct-client denial for every operation on `legacyResetMigrations`.

---

## 7. B3/D22 — total blocked-store recovery

`BlockedSnapshot` below is the total startup-blocked union. `CORRUPT_BLOCKED` names only the structural quarantine/collision branches; storage, Keychain, foreign-installation, and server-receipt authority failures retain their own typed states. Every durable target uses this exact outer envelope (with its store-specific exact payload):

```text
DurableFileEnvelopeV1 = {
 schemaVersion:1,fileKind,generationId,payload,
 recoveryReceipt?:{
   schemaVersion:1,quarantineSHA256,recoveredCount,droppedCount
 },
 sha256
}
```

`sha256` covers the canonical envelope with only `sha256` omitted, including `recoveryReceipt` when present. `quarantineSHA256` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the complete quarantine file bytes. Recovery counts are safe nonnegative integers. Normal later writes preserve an existing recovery receipt byte-identically; its presence without a quarantine is valid audit state.

Every normal write and every recovery measures `baseEnvelopeBytesWithoutRecoveryReceipt`: the complete canonical envelope with `recoveryReceipt` omitted and `sha256` recomputed. It requires `baseEnvelopeBytesWithoutRecoveryReceipt + 190 <= storeCap`, whether or not the current/candidate envelope has a receipt. The 190-byte conservative reserve is the canonical member `,"recoveryReceipt":{"droppedCount":9007199254740991,"quarantineSHA256":"<64 lowercase hex>","recoveredCount":9007199254740991,"schemaVersion":1}` and deliberately exceeds each reachable store-specific count encoding. Thus a short retained receipt can never consume space needed by a later longer receipt. Recovery never drops/evicts a valid item merely to fit its receipt. Independent production/oracle tests exercise base+190 exact/+1 only for a store whose closed schema can construct that length; otherwise they round-trip the reachable constructive maximum, prove its algebraic headroom, and test each member/count cap +1 without padding or surplus fields. Receipt replacement covers shortest through longest reachable counts and preserves prior bytes on rejection.

Here `storeCap` means complete canonical outer-envelope bytes and is exact by `fileKind`: `TASK_ROUTE_INBOX_V1` 131,072; normal `HANDOFF_AUTHORITY_V1` 524,288; `TASK_PLAN_RESET_V2` 131,072; `WORKFLOW_REQUESTS_V2` 16,777,216. For a handoff write satisfying the closed expanded predicates below, `expandedStoreCap = 68,157,440` replaces the normal handoff `storeCap` in the base-plus-190 equation; it is the sole exception. All other handoff writes use 524,288. The route value is new; the other normal values retain/freeze their values. Except for the handoff ordinary/cancellation/combined ceilings expressly overridden below, every other store-specific member/count cap remains independently mandatory. Route fixtures round-trip its constructive eight-record maximum, prove base-plus-190 headroom, and reject every reachable member/count/cap boundary +1 without fabricated padding.

Handoff foreign recovery has one closed overflow exception so two individually valid source files cannot create an unresolvable merge. The item-recovery write that consumes a simultaneously present target and quarantine may enter this regime. A quarantine-only item recovery may also enter it only when the quarantine payload's complete candidates and member/count caps validate; duplicates/conflicts remain unresolved, and selected output is sorted under the frozen store order before the expanded byte check. An exact collision target that is bounded-enumerable with zero candidates and no singleton authority contributes no source population and uses this quarantine-only predicate even though it is physically replaced under the whole-state CAS. The output must contain no candidate not counted from the quarantine, writes a fresh generation and new recovery receipt naming that quarantine digest/count, and never trusts or preserves the malformed outer hash or old receipt. This is the sole re-entry path for an expanded target later quarantined by outer corruption. A genuinely two-population recovery commit may retain at most 32 ordinary session records and at most 96 reconciliation-cancel records, with ordinary-plus-cancel count at most 64 at that initial commit: replacement of one recovered ordinary candidate by one current-requester PREFLIGHT counts once, never twice. A quarantine-only (including zero-population collision-target) expanded recovery may retain at most 32 ordinary, 96 cancellation, and 128 combined because it is recreating a previously expanded valid candidate set, not merging two source populations. After readiness, exact auth/takeover reconciliation required by retained ordinary records may durably add current-requester cancellation authority while the frozen reducer retains required presentation/session bytes through RECEIPT; every later expanded envelope therefore permits at most 32 ordinary records, at most 96 cancellation records, and at most 128 combined. A reconciliation-cancel candidate never reconstructs as an ordinary `SERVER_SNAPSHOT`; only an ordinary candidate may take that projection, so the 32-ordinary bound is closed. No other record creation is admitted. Every expanded write requires `baseEnvelopeBytesWithoutRecoveryReceipt + 190 <= 68,157,440`. Every complete prospective ordinary/cancel record is independently bounded by the normal complete-envelope cap 524,288; `128 * 524,288 + 2 * 524,288 = 68,157,440`, where the two additional normal-envelope budgets cover installation/auth, arrays, outer members, generation/hash, and the maximum receipt reserve. This bound needs no absent-slot oracle and remains valid through every legal per-record transition. Every named record remains subject to its smaller frozen schema/member cap; this adds no wire member. Handoff target/quarantine physical reads are bounded at 68,157,441 bytes so a valid expanded file can be classified; a parsed envelope without the exact expanded predicates still faces the 524,288 normal cap. A candidate above any applicable count/byte limit writes nothing and remains blocked. Fixtures recover a full 128-row expanded quarantine beside an exact empty/no-singleton malformed collision target, and outer-corrupt 32-ordinary+64-cancel authority followed by auth rotation to 32+96 without loss.

An expanded handoff envelope is valid only while it retains a recovery receipt and either its current envelope or the exhaustive maximum prospective envelope across every legal remaining transition exceeds at least one normal count/byte ceiling. Define a cancellation lineage by the complete canonical server-session identity `{uid,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,sessionId}` proved for the ordinary and cancel records. The projection adds at most one current-requester reconciliation successor for each retained ordinary whose identity has no retained cancellation lineage; any PREPARED, DISPATCHED, RECEIPT, or APPLYING cancel for that identity already occupies the lineage and forbids another. A conclusive no-commit may replace that lineage one-for-one in the same atomic envelope write when its ordinary still requires reconciliation; it never removes one and adds two. Ambiguous authority blocks the transition. A RECEIPT/APPLYING lineage must settle under its frozen reducer before any successor can replace it. The actor may create a reconciliation PREFLIGHT only when the complete canonical server snapshot proves it is that sole frozen auth-rotation/takeover successor; creation obeys 32/96/128, and no general begin/preparation is admitted. Therefore a current 32-ordinary+96-cancel envelope is valid only when the cancellation set covers enough of those exact ordinary identities for the exhaustive projection to remain at or below 128; 32 ordinary plus 96 unrelated cancellations is invalid because its projection is 160. Every transition computes the lineage-aware closed projection, including maximum receipt/application bytes, before any mutating await and again before write. Applied/terminal rows are removed under frozen rules.

The first later atomic write whose projection proves every remaining transition stays within ordinary≤16, cancellation≤16, and base+190≤524,288 returns permanently to normal admission; no special marker remains. While the retained `recoveryReceipt` persists, every fresh handoff begin/preparation computes that same exhaustive normal projection before any server call or opener and is admitted only when every future transition remains ordinary≤16, cancellation≤16, and base+190≤524,288. Fresh work can never re-enter or borrow the expanded cap; failure returns the already-frozen local handoff-capacity diagnostic with zero server/opener/file mutation. Fixtures cover recovery output 0+64, 0+96, a valid lineage-covered 32+96, and invalid 32+96-all-unrelated; each count and 68,157,440 at exact/+1; every individual record at its reachable maximum; required auth rotation from initial 32+32; one existing lineage suppressing a duplicate successor; conclusive no-commit one-for-one replacement; RECEIPT/APPLYING settlement before successor; a small intermediate row whose later receipt would exceed normal admission; no general preparation; bounded cap+1 streaming read/classification; projected drain to 16/16/524,288; fresh begin from 15 ordinary+16 cancels rejected because its successor projection reaches 17 cancels; and 15+15 admitted only when its complete 16+16 future projection fits. Thus foreign recovery neither drops authority nor wedges solely because two valid inputs cannot fit one normal envelope.

| Persisted files | State | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| target absent; quarantine present | `quarantined` | Item-recover into one fresh envelope generation, or explicitly discard quarantine. |
| target malformed; quarantine present | `collision` | Recover enumerable items from quarantine while treating target as nonauthority, or discard quarantine only. |
| target valid; enumerable quarantine present; target recovery receipt exactly names its digest and its recovered+dropped counts equal that quarantine's raw pending-record count | `recovered_pending_cleanup` | Automatic full target revalidation, quarantine unlink, directory `fsync`, then ready. |
| target valid; quarantine present; target has no matching recovery receipt | `quarantine_conflict` | Explicitly merge valid quarantine rows into a new generation based on the live target, or discard quarantine only. |
| target valid; quarantine absent; the first current-auth-scoped receipt-bearing row in frozen store order lacks exact live validated-response provenance or disagrees with it | `receipt_mismatch` | Canonical-ID server reconciliation only; no recover/discard. |

Absent/absent is ordinary empty. Subject to the handoff Keychain ordering below, malformed target/absent immediately performs the frozen target→fixed-quarantine rename, directory `fsync`, and enters quarantined. A valid target receipt proves recovery only when the quarantine is enumerable, its digest matches, and `receipt.recoveredCount + receipt.droppedCount == rawPendingRecordCount` under that store's counted universe; only then may `recovered_pending_cleanup` run. A digest match with unenumerable quarantine, unsafe count, overflow, or count disagreement is `quarantine_conflict` and never auto-unlinks.

Handoff has one explicit action-precedence override after Keychain authority succeeds and any valid matching-receipt cleanup finishes: enter `foreign_installation` **before** generic structural actions only when every remaining present source is enumerable, every candidate needed for recovery passes complete schema/immutable-identity validation with no duplicate/conflict group, and at least one valid installation singleton differs from the Keychain installation. This includes quarantine-only and both-source layouts and therefore never exposes generic Discard. A malformed/unextractable installation, an unenumerable source, or any complete-schema/identity-invalid, duplicate, or conflicting candidate stays in the applicable generic structural state; Recover/Merge is absent but that state's exact whole-quarantine Discard remains available. For other stores the table applies directly. No other combination is legal; presence/shape and receipt facts are established before any action is offered. A quarantine-only foreign singleton beside one invalid row fixture must classify generic `quarantined` with only Discard, never `foreign_installation`.

Every Recover/Merge or foreign reconciliation that consumes a present quarantine creates a new generation/hash carrying a recovery receipt for that exact quarantine in this order: unique temp → complete write loop → file `fsync` → atomic rename over target → directory `fsync` → quarantine unlink → directory `fsync`. A target-only foreign reconciliation uses the same temp/write/file-fsync/rename/directory-fsync replacement sequence, performs no unlink, and creates no new or replacement recovery receipt; a receipt already validly carried by the target is preserved byte-identically under the normal envelope rule. It returns target source accounting only. Enumerable means successfully read bounded bytes parse as one JSON object under a duplicate-member-rejecting parser; the one payload member and every store-required candidate collection are unambiguous arrays; the optional reset gesture is unambiguous; and raw element counts plus their sum are safe integers. Successfully read non-JSON bytes, a duplicate member on any path needed to locate candidates, missing/wrong-type candidate collection, unsafe counting, or a readable over-cap file makes it unenumerable and permits Discard only. An open/read/file-protection failure instead enters `storage_io_unavailable` and never offers Discard. Each raw array element plus a present raw reset gesture is one candidate. Enumerability supplies only the counted universe; Recover/Merge additionally requires every candidate to satisfy the complete classifier below. In collision, malformed-target replacement is available only when that target is bounded-enumerable with raw candidate count exactly zero and contains none of the store's singleton authority members (`installation`, `auth`, route refresh authority, or reset `gesture` as applicable). Any candidate or singleton presence makes Recover unavailable; no phase/member heuristic can prove safety. The user may instead discard only the quarantine, after which the unchanged malformed target follows the normal rename protocol and becomes the next quarantine for a separate explicit recovery/discard decision. In quarantine-conflict, recovery starts from the fully validated live target and never removes/changes a live row or singleton except when the explicitly named reconciliation rule below replaces one exact row with canonical server bytes. Crash after recovery rename and before unlink is therefore provably `recovered_pending_cleanup`; a preexisting valid target beside an unrelated quarantine can never be mistaken for completed recovery. Collision Discard removes only quarantine and syncs. Quarantine-conflict Discard removes only quarantine. No action deletes both copies silently. Fixtures cover target-only foreign replacement with exact prior-receipt preservation/absence and no unlink.

A handoff Discard may never leave both durable files absent. If the selected action would remove the last file, after Keychain/Firebase authority and whole-state CAS succeed it first writes the normal canonical empty target with a fresh generation, no recovery receipt, and exact payload `{installation:{installationId:<current Keychain>},auth:<resulting signedAuth>,sessions:{records:[]},reconciliationCancels:[]}`. It uses the standard temp/write/file-fsync/rename/directory-fsync sequence, then rereads and matches the original quarantine digest, unlinks it, and directory-fsyncs again. Any freshly reconstructed auth epoch is generated once after initial CAS and retained through this sequence. Crash before unlink yields target+quarantine under the same installation and reclassifies without rekey; crash after unlink retains the empty target. Collision's first discard remains quarantine-only while malformed target exists; after that target is renamed into the sole quarantine, its final Discard uses this empty-target-first order. Discard still creates no recovery receipt.

Item recovery is schema-specific, never phase-ordinal merge. Keys are exact: route intent ID; ordinary handoff `(uid,installationId,authEpochUUID,taskInstanceId,sessionId)`; reconciliation cancel `(uid,taskInstanceId,sessionId,reasonCode,requesterNamespace.installationId,requesterNamespace.authEpochUUID)`; reset record `(uid,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch)`; reset legacy migration `uid` (at most one row per UID); workflow `(uid,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,workflowId,flowAttemptGeneration)`. A raw candidate is automatically recoverable only when its complete store schema and immutable identity validate. A complete byte-equal cross-source same-key candidate counts recovered without insertion. Any complete-schema/identity failure, any duplicate recovery-key group within one source, and any cross-source same-key unequal group is `unresolved` and makes Recover/Merge unavailable; generic recovery never classifies it dropped and never calls a server seam for an invalid candidate. A same-key unequal valid group remains unresolved unless a later store-specific clause names a frozen **read-only** proof that constructs one exact canonical replacement row without creating, dispatching, advancing, finalizing, or otherwise mutating an operation. No phase, kind, partial-member, or bounded-decoding heuristic is authority proof. After candidate/key resolution and before any action is offered, the complete prospective store must satisfy every store-level singleton, uniqueness, cross-member, phase/authority, count, and byte invariant, including §5's gesture/migration/reset-row matrix. Any aggregate failure is unresolved, removes Recover/Merge from `availableActions`, and writes no target/receipt. Fixtures include individually valid but aggregate-incompatible reset gesture/migration authority.

A complete schema-valid, immutable-identity-valid, unconflicted candidate is selected byte-for-byte for the new envelope with zero server call. Generic route/reset/workflow recovery never purges or drops a complete valid candidate based on current auth; it recovers those bytes and the ordinary owner applies its frozen auth rule only after target durability, readiness, and handoff-auth gating in a later envelope write. Handoff alone may classify a complete valid candidate under its signed-out/different-UID auth purge because its whole-state digest binds both Keychain and current Firebase authority and rechecks them after every await. Other `dropped` cases are only the handoff conclusive ordinary/cancel no-commit proof or explicit Continue-other-device. Workflow `retiredDiagnostics` are outside the counted candidate universe rather than dropped. No **mutating** server call may occur before recovered target durability and readiness. Expressly named read-only proofs may precede the recovery write only for the exact valid identity they accept. This manifest creates no generic reconciliation API and never synthesizes authority from a partial identity. Without a named proof, the source remains blocked and only its separately explicit whole-quarantine Discard action may remove it; `foreign_installation` continues to offer no Discard. No target or receipt write occurs while one candidate is unresolved.

The recovery durability barrier is absolute: write/fsync/rename the complete selected target and `fsync` its directory before quarantine cleanup, readiness, callback, opener, or any mutating operation replay. Every candidate counted recovered by byte-for-byte retention is written in its exact source bytes before readiness. A complete RECEIPT or APPLYING row is always in that category and is never classified dropped or inferred applied. PREPARED, DISPATCHED, and reconciliation candidates may be omitted or replaced only by the exact pre-durability read-only-proof or explicit-choice clauses below, and their source accounting must name that drop or canonical replacement. For every retained or replaced candidate, no mutating replay occurs until the complete selected target is durable and ready. A crash after target durability but before readiness/replay reloads the retained/replacement phase and cannot lose the request.

Complete byte equality is recovered without insertion; every invalid, duplicate-within-source, or unequal cross-source group remains unresolved under the exact classifier above. A complete valid route RECEIVED record, including its complete `refreshLease/refreshNamespace` pair, is recovered byte-for-byte; only after target durability/readiness may ordinary `AppRouteInbox` clear it under the frozen auth-revision/namespace or 60-second-expiry reducer. A malformed/partial refresh pair is unresolved, never transformed/dropped. An existing RECEIPT/APPLYING row is always recovered byte-for-byte and later rerun, never inferred applied from server terminal state. No candidate contributes twice. If resulting live-plus-recovered collections violate any applicable member/count/base+190 cap, recovery writes nothing and remains blocked; it never drops an otherwise valid item to fit. Resulting collections use their already-frozen store sort order before hashing; source iteration never chooses bytes. Workflow recovery omits quarantine `retiredDiagnostics` because they are explicitly outside the counted universe; a validated live target's diagnostics remain byte-identical. Complete-content equality is per candidate, not an all-merge abort.

Receipt provenance is deliberately process-local. `ReceiptProvenanceKey` is exact `{family,authority,requestAuthority,identityDigest}` and `ValidatedReceiptProvenanceV1` is exact `{family,authority,requestAuthority,identityDigest,receiptSHA256,uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision,envelopeGeneration,envelopeSHA256}`. `receiptSHA256` is exactly the lowercase SHA-256 of `UTF8(TaskCanonicalV1(<the complete exact durable receipt map carried by that family row>))`; no outer callable wrapper, replay rewrite, response authority expansion, or omitted optional member is hashed. For a legacy-migration response that carries a nested reset progress receipt, the migration entry hashes the complete durable migration receipt and the separate RESET entry hashes the complete exact nested progress-receipt map. Each owner actor holds `validatedReceiptProvenance: [ReceiptProvenanceKey:ValidatedReceiptProvenanceV1]`. An entry exists only when that same actor fully validated the exact server response against the exact request/identity/auth tuple and durably committed the named envelope. After every successful whole-envelope replacement, the actor drops each entry whose exact row/receipt/auth no longer matches, updates `envelopeGeneration/envelopeSHA256` in every survivor to the newly committed envelope, then inserts/replaces the response just validated. Disk load/relaunch and any signed-auth tuple change clear the complete map. No entry is encoded, inferred from a syntactically valid receipt, or reconstructed after load. A current-auth receipt-bearing row is provenance-safe iff its exact key finds one fully matching entry. Therefore every such row loaded from disk—including RECEIPT or APPLYING produced by crash, launch, recovery, or another process—classifies as `receipt_mismatch` before any application, callback, opener, notification, or accounting. Among multiple unsafe rows, only the first in the store's frozen order is exposed; successful reconciliation reclassifies and repeats without invalidating unrelated surviving entries. A same-process first response may retain its entry and follow its already-frozen first-response behavior. Any crash before local application loses the map and forces inspection, which is the durable representation of uncertainty. A mandatory two-row fixture keeps A in APPLYING while B is reconciled/applied and proves A's surviving entry remains safe and readiness converges without ping-pong.

`receipt_mismatch` is narrower than item recovery and uses one authenticated read-only action in existing `functions/taskPlan.js`: `inspectCommittedOperation`. Its request is the exact nested union `{action:"inspectCommittedOperation",family,authority,requestAuthority,identityDigest}`. Every hash is a lowercase full SHA-256, every ID/token follows its frozen grammar, and every missing, surplus, or cross-branch member rejects. Family projections are literal:

| `family` | exact request `authority` | exact `requestAuthority` | exact read set and accepted stored shape | legal outcomes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ROUTE_CLAIM` | `{operationId}` | `{requestSHA256}` | `users/{uid}/taskPlanOperations/{operationId}`; exact ordinary `INTENT_CLAIM/COMMITTED`, action `claimTaskIntent`, matching owner/ID/hash/identity, exact stored task-route response | `absent|committed` |
| `HANDOFF` | `{operationId}` | `{requestSHA256}` | same task-plan-operation path; exact ordinary `TASK_OPERATION/COMMITTED`, the candidate's retained handoff action, matching owner/ID/hash/identity, exact stored response | `absent|committed` |
| `HANDOFF_CANCEL` | `{operationId}` | `{requestSHA256}` | same path; exact ordinary `TASK_OPERATION/COMMITTED`, action `cancelHandoff`, matching owner/ID/hash/identity, exact stored response | `absent|committed` |
| `RESET` | `{operationId}` | `{requestFingerprint}` | one read-only transaction/same-read-time snapshot of `users/{uid}/taskPlanOperations/{operationId}` and root `users/{uid}`; exact `RESET_OPERATION` with matching owner/canonical ID/fingerprint/identity plus total active marker equality, or finalized tombstone plus marker absence; active deleting/awaiting is pending, finalized reconstructs its frozen final receipt | `absent|pending|committed` |
| `LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION` | `{migrationId}` | `{requestFingerprint}` | `users/{uid}/legacyResetMigrations/{migrationId}`; exact permanent migration kind/owner/derived ID/fingerprint/identity/outcome and its exact §6.2 response projection | `absent|committed` |
| `WORKFLOW` | `{submissionToken}` | `{requestFingerprint}` | derive `workflowSubmissionId = "ws2_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({owner:uid,submissionToken})))`; read only root `workflowSubmissions/{workflowSubmissionId}`; require exact owner/token/fingerprint/identity and stored workflow outcome | `absent|committed` |

The RESET request authority must equal the already-frozen `"reset1_" + SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({kind:"reset",reason:"retake_assessment",expected_task_generation_epoch:e}))`. LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION must equal the exact alias-free `rlmreq1_` derivation in §6.2 over only UID and legacy operation ID. The workflow fingerprint is recomputed from fully validated local `requestCanonicalJSON` under the frozen workflow fingerprint rule and is not the complete-request SHA-256. For every family, request `identityDigest` is exactly the same full lowercase SHA-256 used as that row's `mismatchIdentityDigest` over the exact private identity map below; no second identity projection exists. The handler authenticates UID, performs exactly that table row's read set, and performs no create/update/delete. From the authenticated UID and fully validated stored operation/response—or the reset/migration/workflow authority named in that row—it reconstructs that exact map, hashes `TaskCanonicalV1(map)`, and requires equality; the client digest is never accepted as a substitute for field validation. RESET pending reconstructs and validates the exact current progress projection from the active operation plus matching root marker; RESET committed reconstructs and verifies the final receipt/digest from its tombstone and marker absence. Migration committed reconstructs the exact §6.2 response from its terminal permanent record; neither family assumes a receipt map is stored verbatim.

The result is exactly `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"committed_operation_inspection",accountUid,family,authority,requestAuthority,identityDigest,outcome,receipt?}`. For WORKFLOW only, returned `authority` is `{submissionToken,workflowSubmissionId}`; every other branch echoes its exact request authority. That expanded workflow member is response-validation data only: the provenance key and every retained local request authority always use the request-form `authority:{submissionToken}` and never `{submissionToken,workflowSubmissionId}`. `receipt` is required only for `committed` and forbidden for absent/pending. Ordinary route/handoff/cancel and workflow return their exact stored response with only outer `replayed:true`; a RESET tombstone reconstructs exact `reset_final` with `replayed:true`; LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION reconstructs its exact §6.2 response with only the outer reconciliation `replayed:true` while preserving every already-frozen nested receipt replay flag. Active RESET is the sole pending case and returns no receipt. Receipt reconciliation always enters the loaded/replay local application path and never invokes first-response ephemeral notification, accounting, or callback behavior. A present malformed, wrong-owner/kind/action/path-ID/fingerprint/hash/identity/receipt record returns exact no-write `failed-precondition/OPERATION_REUSED`, naming `operationId` for every non-WORKFLOW family (including legacy migration) and `submissionToken` only for WORKFLOW. For inspect only, invalid top-level/type/member-set input is exactly `invalid-argument/{schemaVersion:1,reason:"REQUEST_INVALID",field:"request"}`; otherwise the first invalid value in exact order `action,family,authority,requestAuthority,identityDigest` names that literal field. A branch-incompatible authority or requestAuthority shape names that member; every unknown or surplus member names `request`. Unauthenticated inspection is exactly `unauthenticated/{schemaVersion:1,reason:"AUTH_REQUIRED"}`. Malformed or colliding stored authority uses only the already-stated `failed-precondition/OPERATION_REUSED` shape. No other inspection error detail is legal, and transport ambiguity is never synthesized. Fixtures cover every row of the table, every replay projection and zero first-response side effect, RESET as the sole pending member, literal root workflow path/derived ID/wrong owner, request-form versus expanded workflow authority, zero `taskPlanOperations` read for workflow, and missing/wrong/surplus/cross-family values for every request field in the exact precedence order.

The owner sends only that retained family projection, then rechecks auth plus the whole recovery-state digest after the await. `committed` writes the exact store-specific RECEIPT/APPLYING replacement and installs `ValidatedReceiptProvenanceV1` for that exact new envelope; for a migration replay it adopts the returned stored alias under §6.5 rather than requiring the local losing candidate. Application then uses only the loaded/replay path. `absent|pending` writes the exact same-request pre-replay phase: route `CLAIM_DISPATCHED`; ordinary handoff/cancel `DISPATCHED`; reset `reset_dispatched` without canonical/progress receipt; legacy migration `dispatched`; workflow `DISPATCHED`. It clears only receipt/application members forbidden by that phase, preserves immutable request/identity/creation bytes, and makes no mutating server call until the target is durable and ready; a concurrent later commit is recovered by ordinary exact replay. Error, malformed response, or disagreement leaves the target byte-identical and blocked. A crash after committed inspection but before or during application loses provenance, reloads as `receipt_mismatch`, and repeats the read-only inspection. This action is the sole receipt-mismatch server mechanism; no direct client read of `taskPlanOperations`, `legacyResetMigrations`, or `workflowSubmissions` is allowed. A receipt-bearing row is actionable only while current signed auth is signed in to that row's UID. Signed-out and different-UID rows—including retained handoff-cancel receipts—remain inert under their frozen retention/auth-purge/cap rules and never enter `receipt_mismatch`; matching sign-in reclassifies them. Every auth change triggers reclassification, and A→B fixtures for each multi-UID store prove old-A receipt rows cannot wedge B.

Reset `gesture` is one counted optional singleton. Any gesture that fails its complete schema/identity is unresolved and makes Recover unavailable. A live target gesture is never replaced: equality counts recovered, while an unequal live/quarantine gesture is unresolved regardless of current auth. With no live gesture, every complete valid reserved or bound gesture is recovered byte-for-byte regardless of current auth. Only after that envelope is durable, reset and handoff stores are ready, and current auth is revalidated may the ordinary B2 owner apply its frozen reserved-auth purge or materialize a bound gesture in a later atomic envelope write; if the four-row cap prevents materialization, retain it and expose B2 capacity recovery. Unequal rows with the same `(uid,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch)` are unresolved and make Recover unavailable; no reset callable or alias reconciliation runs while blocked. Complete valid rows for different epochs are both retained, subject to sort/count/byte caps, and only the ordinary owner may reconcile them after target durability and readiness. No recovery path mints an alias or epoch.

Signed auth is computed before any handoff-row decision. Preserve stored SIGNED_OUT only when Firebase is currently signed out and stored installation equals Keychain. Preserve stored SIGNED_IN only when Firebase is signed in to the same UID and stored installation equals Keychain. Otherwise reconstruct once: signed out → `{state:"SIGNED_OUT",installationId:<Keychain>,epochUUID:<fresh lowercase UUID>}`; signed in → `{state:"SIGNED_IN",uid:<current>,installationId:<Keychain>,epochUUID:<fresh lowercase UUID>,credentialRevision:0}`. No stored credential revision crosses a mismatch. No timestamp/ordinal precedence exists.

For same-installation handoff recovery, an ordinary session is retained/openable only when its `(uid,installationId,authEpochUUID)` equals the resulting SIGNED_IN tuple and its complete schema validates. Signed-out or different-UID rows follow the frozen auth-purge rule. A same-UID/same-installation row under an old auth epoch is never opened: a complete canonical active-session proof replaces that one source candidate in the selected recovery output with the exact current-requester `AUTH_EPOCH_CHANGED` PREFLIGHT row; the old ordinary is not also retained, and source accounting counts the replacement once. Conclusive no-commit retires an old cancellation only in the same envelope write that creates its replacement; no takeover confirmation is offered; and ambiguous/offline authority remains blocked. This preserves the two-source recovery limit: an initial 32 ordinary + 32 cancels commits at most 32 + 32 after count-once replacements. Only after durability/readiness may an ordinary row actually retained by recovery cause the owner to add a separate reconciliation row under the expanded 32+64 rule. Reconciliation-cancel rows validate the exact reason-paired stored/requester namespace invariants. A row already under the resulting requester namespace is preserved byte-for-byte across its valid phase. A foreign-requester row is never dropped offline: canonical authority must produce the generic committed-receipt/application or conclusive no-commit result above; an active different-installation cancellation still requiring takeover creates a new current-requester record only after the frozen explicit Restart-here confirmation. Fixtures prove both the pre-durability replacement/count-once case and the distinct post-ready add-and-retain case.

`HandoffSessionStore` is sole accessor to one installation Keychain item through `InstallationIdentityProviding`. Logical lookup identity is exactly `kSecClassGenericPassword`, service `com.peezy.phase2.installation`, account `installation-id-v1`, `kSecAttrSynchronizable:false`, and the default application access group by omitting `kSecAttrAccessGroup`. Accessibility is not a query discriminator. Load requests both data and attributes, then requires generic-password data equal to one lowercase RFC 4122 UUID in UTF-8 with no terminator/newline and returned accessibility exactly `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`; data or accessibility mismatch is `invalid`. Add/update always write that accessibility. Duplicate-add rereads with the logical lookup identity. It is installation-level and is not removed on sign-out or account deletion.

The seam results are exact: `load()` returns `absent|present(validUUID)|invalid|error`; `addIfAbsent(candidateValidUUID)` returns `inserted(candidateValidUUID)|existing(validUUID)|invalid|error`; and actor-owned `rekeyEmptyContainer(candidateValidUUID)` returns only `rekeyed(candidateValidUUID)|existingConcurrent(validUUID)|error(stage:"load"|"update"|"add")`. No result carries raw Keychain bytes or OSStatus. The rollout premise is frozen: Phase 2 is unbuilt, and this implementation commits a Keychain item before its first handoff-envelope write. Therefore an absent Keychain item beside any target/quarantine can only be restore/reinstall, external loss/corruption, or a fixture—not a legitimate pre-Keychain upgrade.

File presence is checked before choosing the Keychain operation. When both target and quarantine are absent, generate a fresh lowercase UUID and call `rekeyEmptyContainer` before creating the empty target, regardless of whether the logical Keychain item was valid, invalid, or absent. Inside the owner actor it rereads the logical identity, updates any valid/invalid existing item to candidate data plus ThisDeviceOnly accessibility and returns `rekeyed`, or adds an absent item and returns `rekeyed`. After duplicate-add it performs one bounded reread: valid returns only `existingConcurrent(validUUID)`; invalid or still absent returns `error(stage:"add")`; query failure returns `error(stage:"load")`. Any other lookup/update/add/reread failure returns its stage-specific error and maps to `KEYCHAIN_LOAD_FAILED|KEYCHAIN_UPDATE_FAILED|KEYCHAIN_ADD_FAILED` with no target creation. Crash after rekey and before target creation may rekey again because no durable handoff authority exists yet. This empty-container handshake is what enforces “once per app installation” even though a Keychain item can survive uninstall/reinstall.

When either file is present, a valid Keychain item is never overwritten. On `absent`, generate one fresh lowercase UUID and call add-if-absent before mutating either file; `inserted(candidate)` adopts it and `existing(winner)` adopts only the winner. Never derive a candidate from target/quarantine bytes. Preserve both files and classify every differing structurally decodable installation as `foreign_installation`. Crash after Keychain success resumes from that authority. `ThisDeviceOnly` prevents backup migration; it is not treated as proof that uninstall removed the item.

When either durable file is present, every Keychain load/add `error` enters handoff-only `installation_authority_unavailable`, whose sole action is Retry; it performs no file mutation, UUID generation after the error, envelope creation, recovery selection, or dispatch. In that same file-present branch, `invalid` enters separate handoff-only `installation_authority_invalid`; Retry is forbidden because deterministic malformed bytes cannot self-heal. Its sole explicit user action is **Repair installation identity**. The repair candidate is always one fresh lowercase UUID, never a target/quarantine ID. `InstallationIdentityProviding.repairInvalid(candidate)` rereads the logical Keychain identity inside the owner actor and returns exactly `existing(validUUID)|repaired(candidate)|added(candidate)|addExisting(validUUID)|stillInvalid|error(stage:"load"|"add")`. A valid reread returns `existing` without write. An invalid item is updated once with candidate data plus ThisDeviceOnly accessibility and returns `repaired`; update item-not-found enters the absent/add flow. An absent item is added and returns `added`; duplicate-add rereads and returns only `addExisting(validUUID)` for a valid winner. After any other update result, perform one bounded reread: valid returns `existing`, invalid returns `stillInvalid`, absent enters the normal add-if-absent branch, and reread failure returns `error(stage:"load")`. After an add failure, one bounded reread returns `addExisting` for a valid winner, `stillInvalid` for an invalid item, `error(stage:"add")` if still absent, or `error(stage:"load")` on reread failure. `stillInvalid` maps back to `installation_authority_invalid`; errors map to the existing load/add unavailable codes. A concurrent valid winner is adopted and all classification reruns. A valid item is never overwritten, and no target/quarantine byte changes before repair succeeds. No result exposes raw bytes or OSStatus. In the both-files-absent branch, an initially invalid item is handled only by `rekeyEmptyContainer` and never enters `installation_authority_invalid`; its stage-specific errors map to the unavailable codes already frozen above.

`foreign_installation` is handoff-only, requires the enumerability/foreign-singleton predicate above, and permits no dispatch or Discard. **Reconcile restored handoff data** enumerates target and quarantine independently and retains both until every candidate not conclusively removed by the frozen auth purge is settled by read-only canonical server inspection; only then may it commit one current-installation generation or return the exact decision state below. First apply the exact digest-bound ordinary signed-out/different-UID auth purge and count each removed valid candidate as dropped. Every remaining ordinary/cancel candidate awaits canonical server authority; no current-requester exception bypasses that proof. A candidate proved canonical under the current requester may then be retained byte-for-byte under the normal phase rule. For an active server session, branch on the canonical server namespace—never the source file's installation singleton. Only an **ordinary-session candidate** whose canonical server `installationId ==` current Keychain may reconstruct the exact ordinary `SERVER_SNAPSHOT` row solely from validated server authority; an exact resulting auth epoch resumes normally, while a same-installation old epoch enters the frozen `AUTH_EPOCH_CHANGED` reconciliation. A reconciliation-cancel candidate remains a cancel record under every proof/choice result and can only be retained, replaced by one current-requester cancel, or dropped under its separately frozen conclusive rule. Never copy a foreign candidate as current authority; mandatory RECEIPT/APPLYING candidates are the sole wording exception and are retained byte-for-byte only as non-openable local application obligations, never as current namespace authority. Conclusive no-commit for an old foreign cancel is not enough to drop it while its session remains active; it follows the applicable same-install auth-rotation or different-installation branch. Offline/ambiguous authority causes zero target/receipt write. After all proofs, if no permitted complete proof/choice result can meet every count/member/byte cap without dropping nondroppable RECEIPT/APPLYING authority and a quarantine is present, return the underlying generic structural state with only `discard_quarantine`; never discard or rewrite the valid target. With target only, reconciliation must either prove the complete current-target projection fits or preserve the bytes and return the same `foreign_installation` snapshot with zero write and no Discard. A signed-in B fixture containing a valid old-UID A foreign row proves the auth purge performs zero server call and accounts that row dropped. Another fixture combines a full expanded target with a foreign quarantine of nondroppable APPLYING rows and proves the escape exposes only quarantine discard rather than wedging or dropping target authority; a target-only reachable maximum proves the zero-write same-state result.

Before choice grouping, every RECEIPT/APPLYING ordinary or cancel candidate is mandatorily selected byte-for-byte for output and excluded from decision groups; it is never Continue/Restart eligible even when the server session is active. Group only the remaining proved-active choice-eligible candidates by exact canonical server-session identity `{uid,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,sessionId}`. All complete validated read-only proofs in one group must be byte-equal; disagreement is unresolved and permits no choice or write. Each eligible candidate belongs to exactly one group. The proof must also carry the byte-equal canonical server `activeHandoff.presentation_snapshot.action_label`, validated under its already-frozen trimmed/bounded safe-label grammar; absent, invalid, or proof-disagreeing label makes the group unresolved and offers no choice. For each group, privately sort the full lowercase SHA-256 digests of its complete typed candidate identities by unsigned UTF-8 and define `decisionDigest = lowercase SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({server_session_identity:<complete typed identity>,server_proof_sha256:<full lowercase digest of the complete validated read-only proof>,candidate_identity_digests:[<complete sorted list>]}))`. Define public group `{decisionDigest,actionLabel}` using only that canonical server label. Sort unique groups by `decisionDigest` unsigned UTF-8; name the immutable complete list `decisionGroups`, and set `resolutionDigest = lowercase SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({recovery_state_digest:recoveryStateDigest,decision_groups:decisionGroups}))`. One group requires exactly one explicit choice. Neither private identity nor proof bytes are exposed. A nonempty list produces ephemeral `foreign_resolution_required` carrying that same complete ordered `decisionGroups` on every rendering. S4 renders `actionLabel`; when two labels are byte-equal it appends deterministic 1-based ordinal in displayed group order without changing the label bytes or digests. Retained RECEIPT/APPLYING rows contribute no decision group.

Choice collection is purely local to S4 and performs no owner call, server call, or file mutation. S4 collects exactly one `continue|restart` choice for each displayed group, then invokes the owner once as `resolveForeign(recoveryStateDigest:resolutionDigest:choices:)`, where `choices` is the exact array `[{decisionDigest,choice:"continue"|"restart"}]` in displayed `decisionGroups` order. The call rejects a missing, duplicate, surplus, reordered, malformed, or unknown choice before any proof or write. `HandoffSessionStore` reruns all named read-only proofs, recomputes the complete groups/labels and both digests, performs the post-await whole-state CAS, and commits at most one envelope: Continue drops every **choice-eligible** source candidate covered by that proved-active group and counts each dropped candidate in its own source; Restart creates exactly one normal current-requester takeover PREFLIGHT record for the group, replaces every choice-eligible covered source candidate by that single authority row for output selection, and counts every covered candidate recovered in its own source. Mandatorily retained RECEIPT/APPLYING rows remain byte-identical beside either choice. Contradictory per-candidate choices for one server session are structurally impossible because no per-candidate digest is displayed. Any state/proof/label/digest drift returns complete `ready|blocked` reclassification with zero mutating call/file write. Process death or UI loss before the single call/commit forgets choices and re-prompts the complete list; no partial decision state exists in an owner, file, or snapshot. Actual cancel/takeover dispatch occurs later through the ordinary ready-store owner. Fixtures cover two distinct labels, duplicate labels with stable ordinals, server label drift, and a forged foreign-local label that never renders or influences authority.

Foreign no-commit dropping is closed and read-only. A complete PREPARED **ordinary handoff/begin** candidate may be dropped only by its already-frozen local no-dispatch proof. A PREPARED reconciliation-cancel is never dropped solely for no-dispatch: same-installation old-auth is atomically replaced by the exact current-requester `AUTH_EPOCH_CHANGED` PREFLIGHT row, and a different-installation active session follows Continue/Restart. A DISPATCHED ordinary or cancellation candidate may be dropped only when one read-only Firestore transaction/same-read-time snapshot proves both its permanent operation record absent/no-commit for the exact immutable request and its canonical session absent or already terminal with no local receipt/application owed. The complete validated snapshot plus read time is bound into the proof digest. If the seam cannot guarantee that one snapshot, DISPATCHED is never dropped before durability. A cancellation whose session remains active is never this branch and follows same-installation reconstruction or the different-installation decision flow. RECEIPT/APPLYING is retained and later applied after readiness; terminal server state never proves local application. Every permitted no-commit removal counts `dropped` in the final source accounting. A race fixture commits the operation between two nontransactional reads and proves that torn-read implementation is rejected and the candidate remains unresolved/retained.

Source accounting is the exact union `{present:false,enumerable:false}` or `{present:true,enumerable:true,candidateCount,recoveredCount,droppedCount}`, with `recoveredCount + droppedCount == candidateCount`. Recovered means a complete valid candidate retained byte-for-byte under current authority, transformed to the exact canonical RECEIPT/APPLYING row, reconstructed as exact `SERVER_SNAPSHOT`, or replaced by a durably created current-requester PREFLIGHT record. Dropped has only the complete-valid authority-safe meanings frozen above; invalid, duplicate, conflicting, RECEIPT, or APPLYING bytes never qualify, and server terminal state alone never implies local APPLYING finished. Continue-other-device counts only a choice-eligible proved-active candidate dropped after that explicit choice; Restart counts it recovered only when its replacement PREFLIGHT is in the same committed envelope. The exact result is `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"foreign_installation_reconciliation",target:<SourceAccounting>,quarantine:<SourceAccounting>}`. Target counts are returned but not persisted in `recoveryReceipt`; a present quarantine's new receipt names only its digest/counts and the normal write/unlink sequence removes it. A matching target receipt has already removed its quarantine before this state, so no candidate is double-counted. No source mutation occurs until all required server proofs, any explicit replacement record, and the new-envelope write can succeed. A foreign active cancel already in APPLYING is retained, produces no decision digest, and applies only after readiness.

For every actionable structural, recovered-cleanup, receipt-mismatch, foreign-installation, or foreign-resolution snapshot, the owner computes opaque `recoveryStateDigest = lowercase SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1(RecoveryObservedStateV1))`. The private exact map is `{schemaVersion:1,store,baseState,target,quarantine,availableActions,keychain?,firebase?,auth?,mismatchIdentityDigest?}`; `availableActions` is the exact ordered list exposed by that snapshot. `baseState` is the stable file/authority classification; ephemeral `foreign_resolution_required` uses base `foreign_installation`. `FileObservationV1` is exactly `{present:false}`; or `{present:true,classification:"valid"|"malformed",byteLength,bytesSHA256,generationId?,envelopeSHA256?}` for a successfully read within-cap file, with generation/envelope hash present iff valid; or `{present:true,classification:"over_cap",byteLength,fileIdentityDigest}`. The over-cap observation reads at most that file kind's applicable cap plus one byte and sets `fileIdentityDigest = lowercase SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({deviceDecimal,inodeDecimal,sizeDecimal,mtimeSecondsDecimal,mtimeNanosecondsDecimal,ctimeSecondsDecimal,ctimeNanosecondsDecimal}))`, where every value is the base-10 string from `fstat` on the opened no-follow descriptor. Immediately before any over-cap discard, reread `fstat` on that same path through a new no-follow descriptor and require the exact identity digest; drift aborts. Open/read/protection failure instead has no actionable observation or digest and enters storage-unavailable. Handoff alone requires `keychain:{state:"valid",installationId}` and `firebase:{state:"signed_out"}|{state:"signed_in",uid}` after successful authority lookup. Every `receipt_mismatch` additionally requires `auth:{state:"signed_in",uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision}` from `AuthAuthorityProviding`, captured before the server call and recomputed after every await; signed-out is forbidden because no authenticated action exists, and `auth` is forbidden in other base states. `mismatchIdentityDigest` is present iff that base state uses it. Only stable observed inputs enter this map: a newly generated replacement auth epoch is forbidden. When reconstruction needs one, generate it once after the initial CAS, retain it only in the in-flight context across awaits, and commit that retained value only after the final stable-input CAS.

Every action on a snapshot that carries `recoveryStateDigest` accepts and CASes that displayed digest. Before any call or mutation, recompute the complete observed map; drift performs complete reclassification and returns `ready` when the store is ready, otherwise `blocked(fresh BlockedSnapshot)`, with zero call/write. After every await, recompute again immediately before any target write or unlink; drift returns the same complete `ready|blocked` reclassification with no write/unlink and no further call. It never fabricates a blocked snapshot for a ready store. A quarantine unlink additionally requires its original `FileObservationV1`: within-cap bytes are completely reread and hashed, while over-cap bytes use the exact descriptor identity check above. Once a synchronous file replacement sequence begins, no actor reentrancy is allowed between its write/fsync/rename/directory-fsync/unlink checks. This whole-state CAS supplements, rather than replaces, every candidate/request/auth CAS below.

All S4 recovery methods return the closed Swift union `RecoveryResult = ready | blocked(BlockedSnapshot) | busy({schemaVersion:1,reason:"RECOVERY_BUSY",store}) | unavailable({schemaVersion:1,reason:"RECOVERY_ACTION_UNAVAILABLE",store})`; no other result or thrown semantic error exists. Drift performs the complete classifier and returns `ready` when ready or `blocked` with its fresh snapshot otherwise; completion with a ready store returns `ready`, and the two exact local errors map only to their named cases.

Unavailable Retry and invalid-authority Repair carry no state digest because the failed bytes/authority could not be observed. Define the private closed union `RecoveryAttemptKey = {kind:"recover"|"merge"|"discard"|"cleanup",recoveryStateDigest} | {kind:"receipt_reconcile",recoveryStateDigest,mismatchIdentityDigest} | {kind:"foreign_reconcile",recoveryStateDigest} | {kind:"resolve_foreign",recoveryStateDigest,resolutionDigest,choicesSHA256} | {kind:"recover_epoch",recoveryStateDigest,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,expectedPhase,action} | {kind:"unavailable",store,state,errorCode,action:"retry"|"repair"}`. Public-to-private mapping is exact: Recover→`recover`; Merge→`merge`; Discard quarantine→`discard`; Retry cleanup→`cleanup`; receipt Reconcile→`receipt_reconcile`; foreign Reconcile→`foreign_reconcile`; Resolve→`resolve_foreign`; unavailable Retry→`unavailable/retry`; Repair installation identity→`unavailable/repair`; epoch option→`recover_epoch`. `choicesSHA256` is lowercase SHA-256 of `TaskCanonicalV1` over the exact complete displayed-order choices array. Each store owner has exactly one shared `(RecoveryAttemptKey,Task)` slot across structural, receipt, initial foreign reconciliation, foreign resolution, unavailable, and reset-epoch recovery; exact equality, including every semantic argument, coalesces and any different key returns the exact `busy` result before any callback, server call, or write. Thus identical initial foreign Reconcile calls share one task, while any other key receives BUSY. A fresh Repair UUID is generated only inside the winning shared task after this key is installed. An unavailable attempt repeats only its failed authority/I/O step and then performs a full fresh classification before any later action. `DIRECTORY_FSYNC_FAILED` unconditionally opens and syncs the directory, then reclassifies. `FILE_OPEN_FAILED|FILE_READ_FAILED|FILE_PROTECTION_FAILED` retry only read/classification. `FILE_WRITE_FAILED|FILE_FSYNC_FAILED|FILE_RENAME_FAILED|FILE_UNLINK_FAILED` abandon any owner-private temporary file and fully reclassify target plus quarantine; they never blindly repeat the ambiguous target/quarantine mutation. Keychain Retry or Repair completes only its exact authority operation, then fully reclassifies. No unavailable attempt mutates target or quarantine, and any subsequent mutation requires a newly returned token-bearing action. Thus an old Q1 Retry after another scene reaches Q2 or ready can only return that complete current classification and cannot reapply Q1. Fixtures include stale Q1 after another scene reaches ready.

Blocked-state classification order is exact. File open/read/protection unavailability is first. For handoff with any file present, resolve Keychain load/add authority next; unavailable or invalid authority wins before every structural action and permits zero file mutation. After valid Keychain authority, perform valid matching-receipt cleanup, then apply `foreign_installation` precedence only under its complete enumerability/validity predicate; otherwise select `quarantined|collision|quarantine_conflict`. For the other stores, select those structural states directly after I/O. Then select the first current-auth receipt-bearing row in frozen store order that lacks exact live `ValidatedReceiptProvenanceV1` or disagrees with it and expose `receipt_mismatch`; repeat until none remains. Only a fully valid, quarantine-absent, provenance-safe reset target may enter current-UID `reset_epoch_conflict`; then ready. A quarantine is recovered/discarded/cleaned before either server-derived reset state. Thus simultaneous multiple reset epochs plus one actionable receipt mismatch exposes only receipt mismatch and executes zero epoch callback until reconciliation clears it. Fixtures cover a syntactically valid but canonically contradictory receipt followed by crash; loaded RECEIPT and APPLYING in every family; crash after inspection before apply; multiple ordered mismatches; same-process first-response provenance; provenance loss on auth/envelope drift; and malformed target with Keychain invalid/error and zero Recover/Discard/file write.

Exact blocked snapshot is a disjoint union:

```text
BlockedSnapshot =
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"route"|"handoff"|"reset"|"workflow",
  state:"quarantined",recoveryStateDigest,
  targetPresent:false,targetDecodable:false,
  quarantinePresent:true,quarantineEnumerable,pendingRecordCount?,
  availableActions:["recover","discard_quarantine"]|["discard_quarantine"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"route"|"handoff"|"reset"|"workflow",
  state:"collision",recoveryStateDigest,
  targetPresent:true,targetDecodable:false,
  quarantinePresent:true,quarantineEnumerable,pendingRecordCount?,
  availableActions:["recover","discard_quarantine"]|["discard_quarantine"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"route"|"handoff"|"reset"|"workflow",
  state:"recovered_pending_cleanup",recoveryStateDigest,
  targetPresent:true,targetDecodable:true,
  quarantinePresent:true,quarantineEnumerable:true,
  availableActions:["retry_cleanup"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"route"|"handoff"|"reset"|"workflow",
  state:"quarantine_conflict",recoveryStateDigest,
  targetPresent:true,targetDecodable:true,
  quarantinePresent:true,quarantineEnumerable,pendingRecordCount?,
  availableActions:["merge","discard_quarantine"]|["discard_quarantine"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"route"|"handoff"|"reset"|"workflow",
  state:"receipt_mismatch",recoveryStateDigest,
  targetPresent:true,targetDecodable:true,
  quarantinePresent:false,quarantineEnumerable:false,mismatchIdentityDigest,
  availableActions:["reconcile"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"reset",state:"reset_epoch_conflict",
  recoveryStateDigest,actionableExpectedTaskGenerationEpoch,
  occupants:[{expectedTaskGenerationEpoch,phase,recoveryAction}],
  availableActions:["recover_epoch"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"handoff",state:"foreign_installation",recoveryStateDigest,
  targetPresent,targetEnumerable,targetPendingRecordCount?,
  quarantinePresent,quarantineEnumerable,quarantinePendingRecordCount?,
  availableActions:["reconcile"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"handoff",state:"foreign_resolution_required",
  recoveryStateDigest,resolutionDigest,
  targetPresent,targetEnumerable,targetPendingRecordCount?,
  quarantinePresent,quarantineEnumerable,quarantinePendingRecordCount?,
  decisionGroups:[{decisionDigest:<lowercase SHA-256>,actionLabel:<safe label>}],
  availableActions:["resolve"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"route"|"handoff"|"reset"|"workflow",
  state:"storage_io_unavailable",
  errorCode:"FILE_OPEN_FAILED"|"FILE_READ_FAILED"|"FILE_WRITE_FAILED"|
            "FILE_FSYNC_FAILED"|"FILE_RENAME_FAILED"|
            "DIRECTORY_FSYNC_FAILED"|"FILE_UNLINK_FAILED"|
            "FILE_PROTECTION_FAILED",availableActions:["retry"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"handoff",
  state:"installation_authority_unavailable",
  errorCode:"KEYCHAIN_LOAD_FAILED"|"KEYCHAIN_UPDATE_FAILED"|"KEYCHAIN_ADD_FAILED",
  availableActions:["retry"]}
 |
 {schemaVersion:1,store:"handoff",
  state:"installation_authority_invalid",
  errorCode:"KEYCHAIN_VALUE_INVALID",availableActions:["repair_installation_identity"]}
```

Both unavailable branches are Retry-only and forbid every file-presence/decodability/count/identity/digest member. Entry performs no synthetic replace, drop, or further file mutation. Their Retry behavior is only the failure-specific `RecoveryAttemptKey` flow above; a process relaunch fully classifies whichever complete old/new directory state survived and never treats an unsatisfied in-process directory sync as acknowledged success. The invalid Keychain branch also forbids file/digest members and permits only the explicit Repair action above. In the five structural file-state members, `pendingRecordCount` is present iff the quarantine is enumerable and the state permits it; recovered-cleanup and receipt-mismatch forbid it, and only receipt-mismatch permits `mismatchIdentityDigest`. Each of those states requires `recoveryStateDigest`. The reset-conflict member forbids every file-presence/decodability/count/mismatch/foreign member, requires two to four ordered occupants for one current UID, and each option must match the complete row's stored phase and derived recovery action. `availableActions` is required, ordered exactly as shown, and has no other value/order. `recover` or `merge` is present only when the complete candidate classifier, duplicate/conflict resolution, malformed-target zero-candidate/no-singleton predicate when applicable, all count/member/byte caps, and every required named read-only proof admit that action; mere enumerability is insufficient. An action absent from the freshly recomputed list or any unknown/surplus/reordered action list returns exact local `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"RECOVERY_ACTION_UNAVAILABLE",store}` with zero server call or file write. A stale `recoveryStateDigest` retains the whole-state-CAS rule below and returns the refreshed snapshot, never this error.

`foreign_installation` permits only Reconcile, requires at least one source present and every present source enumerable, and enforces `targetPresent == targetEnumerable` and `quarantinePresent == quarantineEnumerable`; each pending count is present exactly when its source is present. `foreign_resolution_required` has the same presence/count invariants; `decisionGroups` is the complete nonempty immutable list, its digests are unique and unsigned-UTF8-sorted, every actionLabel is the exact validated canonical safe label, and its only action is the one-shot exact full-map Resolve flow above. Both foreign states require `recoveryStateDigest`; only resolution-required permits `resolutionDigest` and `decisionGroups`, and every other state forbids both. `availableActions` is part of `RecoveryObservedStateV1` and therefore bound by the displayed digest/CAS. No raw installation UUID, OSStatus, path, account, payload, candidate identity, proof, or localized error enters any snapshot.

For every enumerable source, pending count is its raw counted universe: route `records` elements; handoff session plus reconciliation-cancel elements; reset record plus legacy-migration elements plus one when a `gesture` member is present; workflow active-record elements. Installation/auth/refresh singletons and workflow retired diagnostics are excluded. Generic quarantine recovery requires `recoveredCount + droppedCount == pendingRecordCount`, with no double count. Recovered means a complete valid candidate inserted byte-for-byte, same-key equal, a retained gesture, or an exact row selected by an explicitly named store-specific read-only proof. Dropped means only a complete valid **handoff** candidate covered by its digest-bound auth purge, a PREPARED ordinary handoff's named no-dispatch proof, the stricter dispatched/session-absent proof, or an explicit Continue-other-device choice; generic route/reset/workflow auth state, invalid/duplicate/conflicting candidates, and PREPARED reconciliation-cancel authority are never dropped. Recovery never completes APPLYING or invokes a mutating operation before target durability/readiness. Any unresolved candidate makes Recover unavailable and creates no receipt. An unenumerable source has no count, cannot create a receipt, and permits only its state's non-Recover action. Discard creates no receipt.

`mismatchIdentityDigest` exists only for receipt mismatch. The owner actor privately constructs the exact typed identity map—`{kind:"route",intentId}`; `{kind:"handoff",action,uid,installationId,authEpochUUID,taskInstanceId,sessionId}`; `{kind:"handoff_cancel",uid,taskInstanceId,sessionId,reasonCode,requesterInstallationId,requesterAuthEpochUUID}`; `{kind:"reset",uid,expectedTaskGenerationEpoch}`; `{kind:"legacy_reset_migration",uid}`; or `{kind:"workflow",uid,taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,taskGenerationEpoch,workflowId,flowAttemptId,flowAttemptGeneration,submissionToken}`—selects the unsigned-UTF8-smallest exact `TaskCanonicalV1` encoding among actionable current-auth mismatches and exposes only its full lowercase SHA-256. It never exposes the canonical JSON or raw identity. `reconcile(recoveryStateDigest:mismatchIdentityDigest:)` first recomputes current auth, both digests, and scope inside the actor; drift performs the complete `ready|blocked(snapshot)` reclassification and performs no server call/write. A match performs only `inspectCommittedOperation`, then after that await recomputes auth, the complete observed state, and first mismatch immediately before its atomic target replacement. A canonical `committed` response installs the exact receipt-derived row; canonical `absent|pending` installs the exact pre-replay projection in §7.
