Any transport error, malformed/noncanonical response, auth drift, or digest drift writes nothing and returns complete current classification. Every valid replacement revalidates the entire envelope and remains blocked on the next actionable mismatch. Fixtures mutate every identity-map member, including ordinary handoff `action`, and cover server/client digest equality, cross-action rejection, digest ordering, opacity, identical-action coalescing, different-action BUSY, stale whole-state/identity pre-call no-call/no-write, target/quarantine/auth drift during await with zero write, A→B and signed-out drift, stale action after ready, and refreshed-snapshot return.

Fixtures construct every disk combination; kill every file boundary; prove both collision actions and two-step discard including handoff last-file empty-target-first; distinguish matching digest+count receipt cleanup from digest-only/count-mismatched/unenumerable valid-target quarantine-conflict; preserve every live row except an expressly valid post-durability owner transition; enumerate mixed valid/invalid/duplicate/unequal candidates with exact sums; distinguish successfully read malformed bytes from open/read/protection failure; read at most cap+1 bytes from a multi-gigabyte sparse over-cap file and prove a replaced inode is never unlinked; make collision Recover unavailable whenever malformed target has any candidate/singleton; sort output; enforce normal and expanded combined caps without opportunistic drop; take a valid expanded target, corrupt only its outer SHA-256, rename it to the sole quarantine, change to a foreign Keychain installation, and recover the exact candidates under a fresh expanded receipt with no Discard or permanent wedge; recover/reset reserved and bound gesture plus legacy rows; switch auth during generic recovery and prove every valid route/reset/workflow candidate is retained for post-readiness owner handling; omit quarantine workflow diagnostics while retaining live diagnostics; order multiple mismatches by canonical identity; exercise exact ordinary/cancel keys and handoff digest-bound signed-out/signed-in reconstruction; and retry a directory-sync failure before readiness. Whole-state fixtures create Q1, resolve/reclassify it through another scene to Q2, then invoke stale Q1 Retry and prove Q2 bytes remain; they coalesce exact Retry/Repair keys, return BUSY for a different key, and exercise every failure-specific no-blind-mutation branch. Authority fixtures cover complete valid unconflicted pre-await, dispatched, RECEIPT, APPLYING, and bound candidates copied byte-for-byte with zero server call; malformed/duplicate/unequal authority making Recover unavailable; no generic server seam; kill after target rename+directory-fsync but before quarantine cleanup/readiness/replay; mutating replay only after readiness; exact receipt-mismatch op-first replay/no-create proof; absent/new ambiguity with zero call/write; loaded APPLYING rerun; and never inferring local application from server terminal state.

Keychain fixtures cover accessibility excluded from query but validated in result; add-before-file; missing-key restore with old ID never adopted; target-only/quarantine-only/both-source foreign state; concurrent add winner; load/add errors; reinstall with a surviving valid Keychain item rekeyed before empty-target creation; surviving-key/both-files-absent update failure entering `KEYCHAIN_UPDATE_FAILED` and succeeding on Retry; crash after rekey/before target; fresh-only invalid repair; update item-not-found; duplicate-add/reread; every repair error/reread result; and zero file mutation before authority success. Foreign fixtures cover per-source counts, receipt subsumption, active/terminal/no-commit/ambiguous ordinary rows, PREPARED ordinary no-dispatch versus PREPARED cancel retention/replacement, cancellation replacement consent, exact returned/persisted accounting, and no Discard. Multi-decision fixtures render the same complete ordered group-digest list, collect locally in any UI interaction order, canonicalize the one full ordered choices call, reject missing/surplus/reordered choices, kill before the call/commit and re-prompt all, and drift one proof with zero write. Two scenes submitting opposite choices for the same snapshot have different `choicesSHA256`; one wins and the other receives `RECOVERY_BUSY`, while byte-identical choices coalesce. A mandatory same-server/two-local-namespace fixture proves one group, one choice, Continue dropping both covered candidates, Restart creating one PREFLIGHT while accounting both recovered, and mixed-choice impossibility; proof disagreement within that group is unresolved. One mandatory namespace fixture has file installation B, Keychain A, and canonical active server session A: it reconstructs exact SERVER_SNAPSHOT under A and offers no takeover cancel. Same-installation old-auth and different-server-installation branches are separate fixtures. Receipt mismatch never offers discard/recover.

---

## 8. B4/D23/D28 — readiness gates dispatch, never receipt

New S1 `Peezy 4.0/Tasks/Durable/DurableStoreReadiness.swift` declares `StartupBarrier`, store/state vocabulary, `RouteIngressReceiving`, `RouteIngressDiagnosticReporting`, `AuthAuthorityProviding`, `CurrentFirebaseUIDProviding`, `AccountDeletionProviderContextProviding`, `AccountDeletionCompletionPresenting`, `AppleCredentialStateChecking`, `GoogleIdentityControlling`, `FirestoreLocalCachePurging`, `NotificationIdentityPurging`, `ClientTelemetryPrivacyPurging`, `RoomCaptureArtifactPurging`, `LocalPrivacyPurgeCoordinating`, `ResetEpochAuthorityProviding`, `ResetEpochConflictRecovering`, `InstallationIdentityProviding`, `AccountDeletionRemoteProviding`, and the four exact durable-store purge seams `RouteAccountDeletionPurging`, `HandoffAccountDeletionPurging`, `ResetAccountDeletionPurging`, and `WorkflowAccountDeletionPurging`; it performs no file I/O. `CurrentFirebaseUIDProviding.currentFirebaseUID() -> String?` is a synchronous snapshot used only to compare deletion-gate UID for local navigation; it is never server/file/auth authority. `AccountDeletionProviderContextProviding.dispositions(expectedUID:) async` returns exact immutable `{appleRevocation:"not_required"|"manual_required",googleRevocation:"not_required"|"sdk_disconnect_required"|"manual_required",googleProviderUid?}`. Exact current Firebase provider data containing one valid `apple.com` entry makes Apple manual-required; a complete list without it is not-required; absent/ambiguous metadata is manual-required. Exactly one valid `google.com` entry whose provider UID byte-matches the stable provider snapshot returned inside `GoogleIdentityAuthority`'s exclusive slot makes SDK-disconnect-required and stores that UID; no Google provider is not-required; provider presence without a uniquely matching stable SDK identity is manual-required and forbids a provider UID. Ambiguity never blocks Peezy deletion.

`ClientTelemetryPurgeOutcomeV1` is the closed Sendable union `cleared|relaunchRequired|failed`; `ClientTelemetryPrivacyPurging.purgeAll() async -> ClientTelemetryPurgeOutcomeV1` is nonthrowing and exposes no SDK error/value. `ClientTelemetryPrivacyAuthority` singleflights every caller onto one process-lifetime check task. The check is issued once on MainActor and races a literal 10-second monotonic timeout; callback false before timeout returns cleared, callback true calls delete once and returns relaunchRequired, and timeout/cancellation/invariant returns failed. A late or duplicate callback loses the operation-generation CAS and performs zero delete/state change. After relaunchRequired, every same-process call returns relaunchRequired without another SDK check/delete; after cleared, later same-process calls return cleared under the static zero-record invariant. Only a fresh process resets the singleflight. `relaunchRequired|failed` both retain journal/gate and map to existing `LOCAL_PRIVACY_PURGE_FAILED`; only cleared passes the barrier.

`AppleCredentialStateOutcomeV1` is the closed union `authorized|revoked|notFound|transferred|unresolved`; `AppleCredentialStateChecking.credentialState(forProviderUID:) async -> AppleCredentialStateOutcomeV1` wraps only `ASAuthorizationAppleIDProvider.getCredentialState`, and callback error, cancellation, or `@unknown default` maps to unresolved. `GoogleCredentialOutcomeV1` is `disconnected|signedOut|identityMismatch|failed`. `GoogleURLHandleOutcomeV1` is `handled|notHandled`. `GoogleFirebaseSignInFailureV1` is exactly `missingClientID|missingPresenter|sdkFailure|missingToken|firebaseFailure|staleGeneration`, and `GoogleFirebaseSignInOutcomeV1` is `signedIn(uid)|cancelled|failed(GoogleFirebaseSignInFailureV1)`. `GoogleIdentityControlling` is exact `currentProviderUID() async -> String?`, `handle(_ url: URL) async -> GoogleURLHandleOutcomeV1`, `signIn() async -> GoogleFirebaseSignInOutcomeV1`, `disconnect(expectedProviderUID:) async -> GoogleCredentialOutcomeV1`, and `signOutAll() async -> GoogleCredentialOutcomeV1`. Restore is not a shipped reachable operation and no restore method or state is invented. Every result is a closed Sendable value; raw SDK/Firebase errors, tokens, credentials, GID/Firebase User objects, and view controllers never escape. One process-wide `GoogleIdentityAuthority` actor is the sole production caller of every `GIDSignIn.sharedInstance` API, including configuration/signIn, auth-callback handle, currentUser, disconnect, and signOut. Its exact slot state is `idle|signingIn(operationGeneration,callbackState:"awaitingCallback"|"callbackHandled")|disconnecting(operationGeneration,expectedProviderUID)|signingOut(operationGeneration)`. `signIn()` obtains the Firebase client ID and current presenter and configures/starts Google Sign-In only inside one authority-owned MainActor operation. The Google callback extracts ID/access tokens, constructs the Firebase credential, completes Firebase Auth sign-in, and destroys all intermediate SDK/token values inside that same authority operation; only the final Firebase UID or fixed outcome crosses back to the actor. AuthViewModel maps the closed outcome to its existing completion/UI and never sees a credential or token. `currentProviderUID()` reads and validates only the stable provider UID inside the actor and returns a copied String or nil. Reentrancy is closed except for the authentication URL that completes the already-active sign-in: The sole eligible callback scheme is byte-equal and case-sensitive to literal `com.googleusercontent.apps.833904565407-m9jhkhdhvih0aeeknk4fpcfbnv3g4oso`. Before build, `Peezy 4.0/Documents/GoogleService-Info.plist` must retain SHA-256 `2f67a70dd2cd4daebb988e7f45be2d64904714dbfa5dd9b4308ff3486e0cfa6c` and exact `REVERSED_CLIENT_ID` equal to that literal, while pre-edit `Peezy-4-0-Info.plist` must retain SHA-256 `603c9c711ccd47d7a2929a2c794b88b5b50d78e722703c501f30aac70e7ffbbf` and exactly one byte-equal URL-scheme entry. The Phase-2 plist edit preserves that URL configuration byte-for-byte. A matching-scheme `handle(url)` observed in exact `signingIn(g,awaitingCallback)` is an event of that same slot and serializes through it. Exact handled CASes to `signingIn(g,callbackHandled)` and no later URL invokes the SDK; exact notHandled returns notHandled but leaves awaitingCallback unchanged, so a spoof/unrelated matching-scheme URL cannot consume or retire the pending sign-in. A stale generation, callbackHandled, idle, disconnecting, or signingOut state makes zero SDK call and returns notHandled; handle never acquires or queues a second slot. The sign-in callback or cancellation retires the slot, so a stale redirect cannot mutate a later identity. The exclusive generation spans each complete callback await and postcondition; another sign-in/disconnect/sign-out cannot begin until retirement. Gate closure blocks new sign-in admission but allows an already-signingIn URL event to settle, after which its stale gate/auth generation prevents publication. Disconnect and all-scope sign-out wait for signingIn settlement. Before disconnect it settles or invalidates any prior sign-in continuation, requires the stable current provider ID byte-equal to the expected immutable ID, invokes `disconnect(completion:)`, then accepts disconnected only in the same slot/generation with nil currentUser. Error, drift, nonnil current user, or stale callback returns identityMismatch/failed and leaves the Google ack absent without clearing another identity. Ordinary all-scope signOut uses the same slot, returns signedOut only after nil currentUser, and never claims remote revocation. Starting deletion/all-scope purge waits for prior Google sign-in/callback settlement. `AccountDeletionCompletionPresenting` exposes only §11's non-sensitive closed result and exact acknowledge/open-link actions. Every listed seam protocol inherits `Sendable`, and every concrete value injected across an actor or `Task` boundary must be `Sendable`; no closure captures a non-Sendable mutable adapter. `RouteIngressReceiving.receive(intentId:source:) async` returns exactly `persisted|duplicate|rejected_blocked|rejected_full|invalid`, with source exactly `notification_tap|url`. `RouteIngressDiagnosticReporting.record(_:)` is synchronous/nonthrowing with closed reason `INTENT_ID_TYPE_INVALID|INTENT_ID_INVALID|TASK_URL_INVALID|RECEIVED_AT_EPOCH_INVALID|ROUTE_INBOX_BLOCKED|ROUTE_INBOX_FULL|ROUTE_INBOX_RECEIVED_EVICTED`. Production uses `os.Logger(subsystem:"peezy.Peezy-4-0",category:"route-ingress")` and logs only that literal reason—never intent, account, or URL; tests inject a recorder. This best-effort diagnostic is not dispatch authority, never changes an envelope, and may be lost on process death. No silent-push source or background-fetch callback is added in Phase 2. Static fixtures require zero production GID type/token/credential return outside the authority, exercise every sign-in/failure/cancel/handle/current-UID outcome, and prove callback/presenter/token lifetimes remain internal.

`AccountDeletionRemoteProviding` is exact `func perform(_ request: AccountDeletionRequestV1) async throws -> AccountDeletionRemoteResultV1`. The request enum has only `discover(uid,operationId,proofNonce)|begin(uid,operationId,proofNonce)|resume(uid,operationId,proofNonce)|finalize(uid,operationId,proofNonce)` and encodes exactly §11's per-action maps. `AccountDeletionAuthorityKindV1` is only `member|authenticatedOverflow`. The result enum is only `discoveryAbsent(operationId)|dataFinal(operationId,authorityKind,startedAt,dataDeletedAt,replayed)|authFinal(operationId,replayed)`. Its typed thrown union exhaustively mirrors §11's four details: REQUEST_INVALID(field), AUTH_REQUIRED, DELETION_CAPABILITY_INVALID, and DELETION_RETRY_REQUIRED; unknown code/detail/member is protocol ambiguity and retains local bytes. `TaskPlanService` is sole request encoder and result/error decoder. Fixtures cover every case and reject unknown cases, codes, details, missing, and surplus members.

`AuthAuthorityProviding` is explicitly asynchronous and nonthrowing at its boundary. `currentSignedAuth() async -> SignedAuthAuthority` returns the closed union `signedOut|signedIn({uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision})`. `forceRefresh(expected:{uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision}) async -> AuthRefreshOutcome` returns only `committed({uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision})|notCommitted`. `committed` is legal only after the same UID/epoch CAS has durably written exactly `expected.credentialRevision + 1` as a safe integer; every API failure, overflow, auth drift, or envelope-CAS loss returns `notCommitted` and writes no auth authority. The caller rereads `currentSignedAuth()` after the await, so the result never invents a competing current tuple. The deletion-only `confirmAccountDeleted(expected:{uid,authEpochUUID}) async -> AccountDeletionAuthObservation` returns only `definitivelyDeleted|notProven`. `definitivelyDeleted` is legal only when the provider still holds the exact cached Firebase user for `expected.uid`, the epoch has not been replaced, and a forced token refresh fails with Firebase's exact user-not-found error; manual sign-out, nil current user, disabled user, revoked/expired token, network failure, cancellation, provider drift, and every other code are `notProven`. It writes no auth or envelope authority and never treats generic `signedOut` as deletion proof. `ResetEpochAuthorityProviding` has B2's exact authenticated epoch-read shape. `InstallationIdentityProviding` exposes only B3's exact load, add-if-absent, empty-container-rekey, and invalid-repair operations/result unions. Readiness vector is exact `{route,handoff,reset,workflow}`, each `loading|ready|blocked(BlockedSnapshot)`.

Required sets are exact: handoff operations `{handoff}`; route claim/refresh/returned-session presentation `{route,handoff}`; reset reserve/bind/dispatch/application `{reset,handoff}`; workflow prepare/dispatch/application `{workflow,handoff}`; direct task callables touching no durable aggregate `{}`. Independently, `StartupBarrier` owns exact `AccountDeletionGate = loading|clear|active(uid)|blocked`; this gate and the local-privacy purge journal are loaded/classified before any store owner may publish dispatch/application readiness. Every state other than `clear` globally forbids every UID-bearing dispatch, server call other than the exact deletion/local-purge reducer, protected callback, presentation, opener, Firestore acquisition/use, recording start, or notification-token registration, including an otherwise empty-required-set direct callable. Authentication callback URLs remain the sole nonrecovery bypass. The `uid` in `active(uid)` identifies the durable deletion target; it does not narrow the global gate.

Signed-out is not deletion proof, but it is a product-level local-retention boundary. Every transition to SIGNED_OUT—including cold launch already nil, manual sign-out, revoked/disabled token, account deletion on another device, and failed credential restoration—synchronously enters `loading`, creates/resumes a crash-durable **all-accounts** local-privacy purge, and may become `clear` only after that purge completes and its journal is fsynced/unlinked. Every direct A→B auth-identity switch also requests the all-accounts purge and does not publish B until that journal finishes, after which B's startup discovery runs; a bare UID-scoped transition purge is forbidden. The all-accounts purge clears every UID candidate/quarantine/pref/media artifact, the global Firestore cache, all app notifications/badge/FCM token, and all local Google sign-in state; it does not claim remote account deletion or Google grant revocation. Failure is `blocked` with Retry, never clear. An offline device can classify and erase every offline-capable local artifact without the old UID or a tombstone secret; if Messaging-token or Firebase-Installation provider deletion cannot complete offline, the notifications ack remains absent and the global gate stays blocked until connectivity and Retry. Every transition to a signed-in Firebase user likewise sets `loading` before publishing that UID and completes any prior purge plus startup discovery before changing to `clear|active|blocked`; this closes both terminal-intent and cross-device gaps.

While a durable deletion target exists or the gate is active/blocked, `startDeletion(uid:)` for a different UID returns exact local `{schemaVersion:1,reason:"ACCOUNT_DELETION_BUSY"}` with zero file/server/auth mutation, and every ordinary nondeletion operation remains globally gated regardless of its UID. A valid unacknowledged completion-presentation target has no UID by design and makes `startDeletion(uid:)` return that same local busy result for every UID until its exact action consumes it. For the same UID, an existing valid `prepared`, `data_confirmed`, `purging`, `auth_finalize_dispatched`, or `local_detaching` target is never replaced and no new capability is generated: `startDeletion(uid:)` joins the exact actor singleflight when present or resumes the sole reducer action for that stored phase and returns that outcome. The separately frozen `prepared/startup_discover` consent transition remains the only same-UID purpose change. Route ingress may persist RECEIVED but may not dispatch, and authentication callback URLs remain the only other bypass. Account-deletion and local-privacy recovery APIs bypass the gate solely to advance their exact durable blocked state.

The public deletion blocked snapshot is exact `{schemaVersion:1,state:"account_deletion_recovery_unavailable",reason:"FILE_IO"|"REMOTE_UNAVAILABLE"|"REMOTE_MALFORMED"|"LOCAL_PRIVACY_PURGE_FAILED",availableActions:["retry"]}`; unknown, missing, or surplus members reject. Retry is actor-singleflight, CASes the complete gate/file/auth observation, reruns local classification, and selects the sole verb from durable state. With matching UID/auth epoch, allowing the exact same-epoch higher-revision rebase, prepared/startup_discover→discover and prepared/confirmed_begin→begin. Each authenticated action handles an existing valid marker atomically: below 64 members it enrolls this exact durable capability before cleanup; at 64 it changes no capability byte, increments `account_deletion_capability_registry_full_count`, performs the same cleanup under matching Firebase auth, and returns data authority tagged `authenticatedOverflow`. Thus no ALREADY→recover gap and no registry-full blocked state exist. A signed-out or UID/epoch-mismatched prepared intent first attempts only member `resume(uid,operationId,proofNonce)`. Accepted member authority advances normally. On capability-invalid, the actor calls `confirmAccountDeleted` only when its exact cached expected user remains available. Exact `definitivelyDeleted` atomically enters `local_detaching(reason:"auth_deleted")`; otherwise `confirmed_begin` enters `local_detaching(reason:"remote_unverified")` under its retained explicit user consent, while `startup_discover` CAS-unlinks/fsyncs only that probe and then runs the mandatory signed-out/transition purge before reclassifying. The fixed result for `remote_unverified` is `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"ACCOUNT_DELETION_REMOTE_UNCONFIRMED"}` with copy `Local data for this account was removed, but remote account deletion could not be verified. Sign in again to retry if the account still exists.` It is never reported as server completion. For member `data_confirmed|purging`, resume is the sole server verb; authenticated-overflow authority performs local purge without resume because the exact data-final response is already durable. At `auth_finalize_dispatched`, member authority calls finalize with or without Firebase auth. Authenticated-overflow calls finalize only while a fresh signed authority still matches the stored UID/epoch, and the server accepts that nonmember only while the registry is exactly full; otherwise exact definitive deletion enters local detaching as completed, while an ambiguous/generic sign-out enters `local_detaching(reason:"remote_unverified")` and never claims finalization. This authenticated overflow path lets a still-authenticated owner complete deletion even if all 64 member secrets are lost. `local_detaching` never calls a server: it completes the intent-linked UID purge and preferences, writes the terminal presentation, conditionally signs out only the matching Firebase user, and then creates/resumes §11's linked all-scope journal before the intent may be unlinked; only the linked journal's exact terminal order may reclassify the gate. Any request/purpose/operation/proof/authority-kind/envelope CAS loss discards the response. On signed-in startup with no completely usable local deletion intent, S4 first persists one fresh prepared capability and invokes only discover. Exact absent response includes and must match the requesting operationId, CAS-unlinks/fsyncs that still-prepared startup probe, then reclassifies; a member or authenticated-overflow data response follows the ordinary reducer even if the first response is lost. Malformed response or unavailable transport becomes the exact blocked reason with durable bytes retained. Signed-out absence runs/resumes all-accounts local purge and becomes clear only after success; every later sign-in synchronously performs loading/discovery. A successfully observed malformed/over-cap deletion-intent target is privacy-unlinked under §11's exact identity CAS and then classified as absent, but never bypasses the transition purge; open/read/protection ambiguity is FILE_IO. Recovery APIs and authentication callback URLs bypass the general four-store vector, but auth-dependent recovery on route/reset/workflow—including receipt mismatch and reset-epoch recovery—has the narrow prerequisite `handoff == ready`. If handoff is not ready, S4 disables that action; invocation makes zero auth-provider/server/callback/file call and returns its own unchanged blocked snapshot. Structural item recovery that only retains route/reset/workflow bytes remains available while handoff is blocked, and handoff's own recovery remains the root action that can clear the dependency. Subject first to deletion/local-privacy gates, an operation proceeds iff every required store is ready. Directed dependencies are unchanged. Fixtures include begin enrollment racing Auth deletion; response loss; startup-probe sign-out; every definitive/ambiguous auth result; local-purge crash at every ack; 64-member/overflow cases; and A/B enrolled before B deletes Auth. A separate offline-device fixture leaves B offline through A's finalization and relaunches B already Firebase-nil with no deletion intent; it proves every offline-capable local postcondition, notifications ack absent, journal retained, and gate blocked, then restores network, retries the exact Messaging-token and Firebase-Installation deletions, and proves journal unlink precedes clear. Generic manual sign-out, revoked token, direct A→B, purge failure/Retry, and lost response prove no generic signed-out state is mistaken for remote deletion authority.

`AppRouteInbox.receive(intentId:source:)` remains sole route-envelope writer and serializes load with ingress. A valid task notification/URL is handed to it immediately even while other stores load/block. Precedence is exact: after store-ready and intent-syntax validation, duplicate lookup precedes clock sampling and returns `duplicate` with zero clock read, write, purge, or eviction; only a fresh valid intent samples the injected clock once. That same sample must be a safe nonnegative epoch-millisecond integer with safe `receivedAtEpochMilliseconds + 86_400_000` and governs receipt time, expiry comparison, purge, and eviction; otherwise it records `RECEIVED_AT_EPOCH_INVALID`, returns `invalid`, and writes no byte. Route records are ordered by `(receivedAtEpochMilliseconds ascending, intentId unsigned-UTF8 ascending)`, and oldest eligible eviction uses that same tuple. For a ninth distinct valid intent it first removes expired records only when their phase is plain RECEIVED and no live `refreshLease/refreshNamespace` pair exists. Every live-lease RECEIVED row and every CLAIM_DISPATCHED, CLAIMED, or PRESENTING row is protected even after TTL. If still full, it selects only the oldest plain RECEIVED record with no live refresh lease; it emits `ROUTE_INBOX_RECEIVED_EVICTED` only after the atomic replacement durably commits, then returns `persisted`. Diagnostic failure does not roll back that durable receipt. If all eight are protected it records `ROUTE_INBOX_FULL`, returns `rejected_full`, and changes no byte. If any attempted fresh-receipt, purge, or eviction envelope replacement fails at write/fsync/rename/directory-fsync, the owner publishes the exact §7 `storage_io_unavailable` snapshot, best-effort records `ROUTE_INBOX_BLOCKED`, and returns `rejected_blocked`; it never returns `persisted|duplicate|rejected_full` for that attempt. The complete old-or-new atomic directory state is classified on Retry, and no eviction diagnostic is emitted before commit. Invalid intent String and an already blocked store respectively record `INTENT_ID_INVALID` or `ROUTE_INBOX_BLOCKED` and return their exact result. Diagnostic loss follows the existing no-zero-loss posture. It never synthesizes empty authority. Only claim/refresh/presentation/callable/opener wait for readiness.

Production contains exactly one process-wide `AppRouteInbox` actor instance, one `GoogleIdentityAuthority`, one `NotificationIdentityAuthority`, one `ClientTelemetryPrivacyAuthority`, one startup barrier/deletion gate, one account-deletion completion presenter, and one account-deletion coordinator. S7 declares `Phase2ProductionRuntime` in this initialization order: process-lifetime `static let routeInbox`, `static let ingressDiagnostic`, `static let googleIdentityAuthority`, `static let notificationIdentityAuthority`, `static let clientTelemetryPrivacyAuthority`, `static let currentFirebaseUIDProvider`, `static let startupBarrier`, `static let accountDeletionCompletionPresentation`, then `static let accountDeletionCoordinator`. S1's already-authorized `TaskPlanService.swift` owns the typed account-deletion DTO/transport conformance to `AccountDeletionRemoteProviding`; S2 owns only the JS callable implementation. The coordinator is constructed once from that transport, all eight exact local-purge owner seams (route, handoff, reset, workflow, room capture, Firestore cache, notifications, and Google), the independent client-telemetry privacy barrier, the immutable provider-context seam, the exact completion presenter, and the exact shared `startupBarrier` through its Sendable deletion-gate-controlling seam. The Google seam, provider-context seam, URL handler, auth model, and Google purge seam all receive that one Google authority; the notification delegate adapters, registration path, startup-discovery hook, and notification purge seam all receive that one notification authority; every deletion/auth-transition coordinator receives the one client-telemetry authority. `AppDelegate.override init()` stores the exact route/checker/Google/notification references before calling `super.init()`; `@UIApplicationDelegateAdaptor(AppDelegate.self)` is never expected to pass constructor arguments. `PeezyV1App`, every purge/store owner, auth/scene observers, presentation/opening consumers, every route ingress adapter, and `PeezySettingsView` obtain the applicable exact static instance; the internal AppDelegate/test runtime initializer is the sole alternative initializer. Constructing a second production inbox, Google authority, notification authority, client-telemetry authority, Firebase-UID provider, startup barrier, deletion gate, completion presenter, or deletion coordinator is forbidden. Identity fixtures capture `ObjectIdentifier` for every production reference, and spy mutations prove Settings/app/AppRootView/all eight purge owners share the same serialized identity and telemetry authorities, barrier, presentation, and deletion owner.

The AppDelegate adapter never writes. It binds immutable production receiver/sink instances synchronously in `AppDelegate.init`; an internal test initializer may inject both, and no callback may observe an unset/default dependency. Define `final class CompletionOnce: @unchecked Sendable` with an `NSLock`-protected optional raw `() -> Void` and `scheduled` Boolean. `finish()` atomically wins once: on the main thread the winner takes and nils the callback while holding the lock, releases the lock, then invokes; otherwise it marks `scheduled` under the lock, releases it, and `DispatchQueue.main.async` captures only the Sendable box. The private main-thread-asserting method likewise takes/nils under lock, releases the lock, then invokes. UIKit's closure is never invoked while the lock is held, and every later or reentrant `finish()` is a no-op. No actor isolation is assumed for the UNUserNotificationCenter delegate callback, no unstructured Task captures the raw UIKit closure, and the callback is invoked exactly once on the main thread.

Notification-tap precedence is exact: if `response.notification.request.content.categoryIdentifier == "PEEZY_SUPPORT_REPLY_V1"`, one main-actor `Task` captures only immutable `CurrentFirebaseUIDProviding`, the process-wide deletion-gate checker, and `CompletionOnce`. It reads only the current Firebase UID snapshot, asks the checker for that value, and calls `SupportChatNavigation.requestOpen()` exactly once only when the gate is `clear`; loading, blocked, active-same-UID, active-other-UID, and active-with-nil-current-UID call no opener. It always calls `completion.finish()` exactly once afterward and captures no route receiver or raw UIKit closure. Otherwise, absence of `peezy_task_intent_id` is non-task and finishes immediately; otherwise, an exact String value is handed unchanged to `receive(...,source:.notification_tap)`, while any other value synchronously records `INTENT_ID_TYPE_INVALID` and finishes. Only the non-support task-route `Task` captures the receiver, intent ID, and `CompletionOnce`; it uses `defer { completion.finish() }` and awaits the nonthrowing receiver exactly once. Success, every rejection, and cooperative cancellation all leave through that defer. No catch or thrown-receiver branch exists. Diagnostic recording adds no await. The support branch never reads `userInfo`; a spoofed legacy `thread:"support"` key without the fixed category follows the ordinary task/non-task branch. Additional APNs/system keys do not invalidate an otherwise valid task payload. No branch calls a completion-handler variant with `.noData`. Support-over-task precedence is unchanged; fixtures cover foreground/background/cold support-category taps, spoofed support userInfo, deletion gate loading, blocked, active-same, active-other, active-with-nil, and clear.

`onOpenURL` parses the task namespace before Google Sign-In. An exact `peezy://task/v1?intentId=...` under the frozen one-query-pair grammar starts one unstructured Swift `Task` that calls the receiver with `.url`. Any URL whose scheme is exactly lowercase `peezy` but fails that grammar synchronously records `TASK_URL_INVALID` and is not offered to Google. Only a non-`peezy` URL whose scheme is byte-equal and case-sensitive to the literal Google callback scheme frozen above starts one task that calls `await Phase2ProductionRuntime.googleIdentityAuthority.handle(url)` and discards its exact handled/not-handled result. Every other non-`peezy` URL is ignored with zero Google SDK call. This authentication callback bypasses readiness but not `GoogleIdentityAuthority`'s slot: only exact `signingIn(g,awaitingCallback)` treats it as the same operation's completion event; notHandled preserves that substate, handled enters callbackHandled, and every other slot state returns notHandled with zero SDK call. It never queues a second operation or interleaves with disconnect/sign-out. The receiver never dispatches the route before durable receipt.

S4 owns the new recovery coordinator/view and the durable account-deletion actor; S1 `TaskPlanService` owns the typed account-deletion DTO/transport conformance, while S2 owns the callable's JS server implementation and S3 performs the exact server/rules integration pass frozen in §12.2. S7 constructs and publishes the barrier, deletion gate, and recovery surface in `PeezyV1App` before `AppRootView` is created. `AppRootView` may render; each operation is gated by its declared required set plus the deletion gate. The recovery surface overlays blocked-store/deletion actions and remains mountable with all stores blocked; it does not turn an unrelated empty-required-set callable into a four-store dependency when the deletion gate is clear. Integration order: S1 seams plus TaskPlanService DTO/transport → S2 workflow/server implementation → S3 scheduler/migration/account-deletion final integration → S4 recovery UI plus durable deletion orchestration → S5 handoff/auth conformance → S7 route/AppDelegate/root wiring.

Fixtures: fixed support-category+task collision proving support precedence; support category in foreground/background/cold tap and spoofed `userInfo.thread` without the category proving zero support open; valid, absent, and non-string task notification payload; task URL and malformed Peezy URL before readiness; Google sign-in held at redirect; unrelated non-Peezy URL ignored; matching-scheme notHandled spoof preserving awaitingCallback; then real handled redirect settling that same signingIn slot; post-handled URL making zero SDK call; gate/disconnect racing immediately after callback settlement; stale URL during disconnect/signOut/idle producing zero SDK call or later-identity mutation; `CompletionOnce` concurrent/double/reentrant finish from main/background threads, cooperative task cancellation, every nonthrowing receive result, and every persist/reject/diagnostic branch proving exactly-once main-thread invocation with no raw-closure Task capture or invocation under lock; immediate non-task completion; receiver/sink bound before callback; `@UIApplicationDelegateAdaptor` default construction bound to the exact `Phase2ProductionRuntime` references; one production inbox and account-deletion-coordinator identity across Settings/app/store owners; safe received-at boundary, maximum value allowing `+86_400_000`, and its +1/unsafe-clock rejection; duplicate under a throwing/invalid clock proving zero clock read; equal-millisecond receipts ingressed in inverse lexical order proving canonical order and eviction tie-break; ninth distinct receipt with an expired purge, expired+live-lease RECEIVED protection, safe oldest-RECEIVED eviction plus exact post-commit diagnostic and durable replacement, and eight protected rows producing only FULL/rejected-full; every file-replacement failure boundary returning rejected-blocked with callback once and correct old/new classification; kill after receipt/before readiness and disk replay; route blocked; every dependency edge mutation; direct WAIT with handoff blocked; storage/installation authority unavailable; async auth refresh success/failure/drift/overflow and exact durable revision; signed-in reinstall with no local stores and DELETING/DATA_DELETED/AUTH_GUARDING/ACCOUNT_DELETED discovery; missing/corrupt intent; four owners racing ready while deletion gate is loading/blocked/active; and all-four-blocked UI.

## 9. D18–D21 — superseded presentation and undo

Fresh writes use only exact `SupersededContractV2`:

```text
{schema_version:2,terminal_kind:"superseded",superseded_by,
 superseded_at,visible_status_copy:"Replaced",
 visible_status_detail:{kind:"DATE",at}}
```

Fresh v2 `superseded_by` is exactly the replacement `task_instance_id` under H54, never a task document ID. `superseded_at` is the confirming transaction's one concrete Firestore Timestamp and detail `at` is the same value. Any hybrid/mismatch/missing/surplus is malformed. Fresh v1/schema-less writes reject.

Decoder-only legacy is the exact committed four-member map `{schema_version:1,terminal_kind:"superseded",superseded_by,visible_status_copy:"Replaced by an updated task"}` with no date/detail. `superseded_by` must satisfy the committed nonblank task-document-ID grammar but is opaque compatibility/display data only and never resolves or validates as a modern instance link. It renders the literal copy and derives no date. Freeze one byte-exact fixture from the committed mapper; every wrong-copy/type, invalid ID, missing, surplus, schema-less, or hybrid shape is `malformedPresent`.

Production formatting: Gregorian calendar, `Locale.autoupdatingCurrent`, `TimeZone.autoupdatingCurrent`, `.medium` date style, no time; user-local day is intentional. Exact fresh-v2 primary copy is `Replaced — {formatter(visible_status_detail.at)}` with one ASCII space on each side of U+2014 and no other suffix or identifier. Exact decoder-only legacy output is its committed `visible_status_copy` verbatim and has no separator/date. Tests inject fixed values and golden-test both strings, including locale/time-zone day rollover.

Every H54 attempt uses the already-frozen retry-scoped concrete `server_write_time` from §4.1 for authored timestamps and deadline comparisons; a transaction retry obtains its new injected-clock value. Undo exists exactly in first CF while unused and `server_write_time <= first_confirmation_undo_until`. It survives transport failure and TRIGGER_INVALID; the latter uses the frozen read-only restoration preflight/selector and retries. It disappears only on success, `server_write_time > first_confirmation_undo_until`, or authoritative instance/cycle/state change. Stale instance without descriptor dismisses. Client/device wall time and `FieldValue.serverTimestamp()` never decide eligibility.

The new history presentation below applies only after the exact policy-bearing `planChangeHistory` union validates. Policy-absent legacy H54 retains its committed bytes and frozen behavior—including legacy actions such as `confirm|reconfirm` and its `at` fields—and is never decoded or rendered as one of these five policy-bearing rows.

The stored array remains in its frozen authoritative append order. Presentation iterates ordinary rows in exact reverse stored order, excludes the optional rollup during that reversal, then renders that sole rollup once as the final row. Ordinary date comes from `occurred_at`; rollup range comes from `first_at/last_at`; all use D19's formatter. Literal titles: supersede “Plan update started”; replacement_outcome “Updated task outcome recorded”; confirm_amendment “Plan update confirmed”; undo_confirmation “Confirmation undone”; reopen “Original task reopened”. Ordinary line is `{title} · {medium date}`. For a rollup compute `firstDate = formatter(first_at)` and `lastDate = formatter(last_at)`; if those resulting strings are byte-equal render `Earlier plan changes ({count}) · {firstDate}`, otherwise render `Earlier plan changes ({count}) · {firstDate}–{lastDate}`. Never render request/operation/submission/snapshot/evidence/task/instance/digest identity. Golden tests inject POSIX/UTC and cover same-local-day/different-time, different-day, all branches, and order.

## 10. D25/D26 — per-instance evidence roots and literal pointer registry

Policy-bearing ordinary plan history becomes exact:

```text
{schema_version:1,revision,
 action:"supersede"|"replacement_outcome"|"confirm_amendment"|
        "undo_confirmation"|"reopen",
 request_identity:{kind:"OPERATION",operation_id}|
                  {kind:"WORKFLOW_SUBMISSION",submission_token},
 original_task_instance_id,replacement_task_instance_id?,snapshot_record_id,
 evidence_refs:[{task_document_id,task_instance_id,evidence_id}],occurred_at}
```

`evidence_refs` is always present, stable creation order, unique complete triples, and names only the row's original or its one replacement; at most one non-original instance per row. Policy-bearing `evidence_ids` is forbidden because no shipped writer exists. Policy-absent legacy H54 stays byte-compatible. Rollup forbids refs and digests the new complete ordinary-row bytes.

Replacement-only exact root:

```text
foreignRoots:{schema_version:1,original_task_document_id,
 original_task_instance_id,plan_change_revision,
 counts:{<evidence_id>:<positive_safe_integer>}}
```

Counts has 1...128 `ev1_` keys present in that replacement's evidence, values 1...17, canonical map order, complete cap 16,384; absent rather than empty. Forbidden on original/unrelated tasks. For replacement evidence `e`, count equals retained confirmation attempts whose `source.evidence_id == e` plus retained non-rollup history refs naming that replacement/e. A confirmation snapshot source is a validation mirror of its one currently retained attempt, the same logical claim, and adds zero count; an unreferenced/old snapshot source is immutable audit bytes and is not a live pointer, resolver input, or root. `confirmation_evidence_id` is original-owned. `replacement_evidence_copy.evidence_id` is verified copied data and never pointer/root/resolver/count.

Transaction order: build prospective history and its fixed rollup; derive original/replacement/cross claims; read all affected peers; recompute complete foreign counts; build `R_original` from normal roots, original-owned refs, and confirmation IDs; build `R_replacement` from normal roots plus foreign-root keys; follow same-instance dependency closure; run retention, D27, and budget; commit all pointer/root/count changes atomically. One appended row/one rolled row touches at most original, current replacement, and one older replacement. A malformed, missing, or mismatched graph authority—peer, link, instance, revision, evidence identity/content, count content, or surplus graph member—is `PLAN_GRAPH_CHANGED`, zero writes. Ordinary component overflow, escrow shortfall, and actual Firestore-write-budget overflow instead use §11's separately frozen capacity errors and precedence. Rollup decrements only in the same transaction that removes the row. Reset may temporarily break equality only while root fence blocks all writers and physical deletion proceeds; finalize requires all tasks/snapshots absent.

`foreignRoots` is excluded from semantic/rollback snapshots, included in whole-task/budget, preserved by writers, and wildcard-index-exempt. Owner task reads necessarily include its raw map; the Swift exact-member decoder validates it, never treats it as product authority, and exposes it through neither the product model nor UI. Rules deny every client create/update/delete that adds, changes, or removes `foreignRoots`, and deny every other user's write; Admin SDK transactions own all mutations.

`EVIDENCE_POINTER_PATHS_V1` is a literal manual registry, not schema discovery:

```text
# same-instance live roots
taskInteractionState.milestone_profile.resolved_from_evidence_ids[]
taskInteractionState.required_milestones[].evidence_id
taskInteractionState.required_milestones[].not_required_source

  snapshot_record_id is referenced by current planChangeCycle.confirmation_attempts[];
  this mirrors that attempt and adds zero foreignRoots count
planChangeHistory[ordinary].evidence_refs[].evidence_id when replacement-owned
```

`EVIDENCE_NON_POINTER_PATHS_V1` is the separate literal exclusion registry:

```text
# notification/wake linkage, never evidence lookup
wakeEvidence.intent_id
notificationIntents[TASK_RESUME].cause.wake_evidence_id

# handoff display/snapshot/operation identity
activeHandoff.prior_dynamic_snapshot_id
activeHandoff.adapter_operation_id

# H54 operation/snapshot/submission identity

users/{uid}/research/{taskId}.brief.sections[].items[].applicability_evidence_ids[] # required []

  planChangeCycle.confirmation_attempts[] references its snapshot_record_id
```

Only paths in `EVIDENCE_POINTER_PATHS_V1` are traversed. Every unlisted runtime path is ignored even when its string is exactly `ev1_`-shaped. `EVIDENCE_NON_POINTER_PATHS_V1` is a required regression-sentinel set, not an alternative traversal. Every path in that exclusion table remains subject to its own frozen grammar/prefix; an `ev1_` spelling is permitted only where that schema permits it, but still never triggers lookup, retention, dependency closure, resolver access, or `foreignRoots` count. Snapshot-embedded evidence is self-contained. No umbrella noun such as “operation ID” or “presentation source” substitutes for either literal table.

`functions/taskInteraction.js` contains both exact tables; independent tests exercise entries but do not claim discovery. The registry test also enumerates every exact persisted task-plan operation request/response and workflow-submission response shape after decoding only fields that the stored schema itself exposes; any evidence-ID-valued member absent from both literal tables fails the test. Opaque `requestCanonicalJSON` remains an immutable string and is never traversed as a hidden object. §A10/§8.6 require any persisted field capable of `ev1_` addition/removal/rename/ownership change to update the applicable registry, ownership/root rule, D27 calculator, and fixture in the same review. Exact-member validators reject unknown runtime fields.

Fixtures cover every listed positive and exclusion path; the exact persisted `users/{uid}/research/{taskId}.brief.sections[].items[].applicability_evidence_ids[]` shape emitted by `functions/researchTask.js`; `interactionHistory[ordinary].operation_id`; `wakeEvidence.cause.event_high_water.source_evidence_id` containing an `ev1_`-shaped value with zero traversal/root/count; an otherwise unrooted record whose own `evidence_records[].id` does not prevent rollup; an unlisted runtime field containing a valid `ev1_` string and proving zero traversal; policy-absent legacy evidence retention/rollup; dependency chains; mixed-owner rows; attempts/current-snapshot one-claim mirror; cycle replacement proving an old unreferenced snapshot no longer roots replacement evidence; original confirmation; copied tuple; 15→16 rollup against older replacement; last-claim removal; wrong/missing/third-instance/old-schema/forgery; reset kill; complete stored operation/response-shape enumeration; and an intentionally added schema field failing the review fixture until all four artifacts change.

## 11. D27 — durable capacity escrow for an admitted H54 cycle

External supersede writes one copy on original and replacement in the first-PA transaction:

```text
evidence_reservation:{
 schema_version:1,reservation_id,task_generation_epoch,plan_change_revision,
 original_task_document_id,original_task_instance_id,
 replacement_task_document_id,replacement_task_instance_id,
 holder_role:"ORIGINAL"|"REPLACEMENT",
 phase:"first_pa"|"first_pc"|"first_cf_undoable"|"second_pa"|"second_pc",
 remaining:{evidence_record_count,evidence_record_bytes,
            foreign_root_entry_count,foreign_root_bytes,
            interaction_history_bytes,task_interaction_state_bytes,
            semantic_snapshot_bytes,task_document_bytes},
 release_after?
}
```

`reservation_id = "pcr1_" + first40(SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({uid,task_generation_epoch,original_task_instance_id,replacement_task_instance_id,plan_change_revision})))`. All numbers are safe nonnegative. `release_after` exists only first-CF-undoable and equals first undo deadline. Complete map ≤16,384, wildcard-exempt, excluded from semantic/rollback snapshots, and included in creation hash and actual whole-task/budget. Owner task reads necessarily include the raw map; Swift validates its exact members, never treats it as product authority, and exposes it through neither the product model nor UI. Rules deny every client create/update/delete that adds, changes, or removes `evidence_reservation`, and deny every other user's write; Admin SDK transactions own all mutations. Copies share identity/phase/release; role and holder-specific remaining differ.

Exact cap vector:

| Component | Limit |
|---|---:|
| evidence records | 128 |
| evidence canonical bytes | 131,072 |
| foreign-root entries | 128 |
| foreign-root canonical bytes | 16,384 |
| interaction-history canonical bytes | 65,536 |
| taskInteractionState canonical bytes | 196,608 |
| semantic snapshot canonical bytes | 262,144 |
| task bytes with the `evidence_reservation` member wholly omitted | 638,952 |

The reservation map itself caps defensively at 16,384 canonical bytes and reinsertion adds the exact 24-byte canonical member prefix `,"evidence_reservation":`. The omitted-member admission ceiling remains the literal 638,952 bytes in the table and is never recomputed from a reachable map. Let `M` be the exhaustive maximum `TaskCanonicalV1` byte length of a schema-valid reservation map over every reachable holder/phase and maximum legal identity; production and independent oracle must prove `M <= 16,384`. Therefore every admitted reserved task is at most `638,952 + 24 + M <= 655,360`; `M` does not alter either component limit. Actual complete task independently caps at 655,360, and both checks are mandatory.

Remaining transition tree is literal:

```text
first_pa: first outcome -> first confirm -> expire
        | first outcome -> first confirm -> undo -> second outcome -> second confirm
        | initial-PA reopen
first_pc: first confirm -> expire
        | first confirm -> undo -> second outcome -> second confirm
first_cf_undoable: expire | undo -> second outcome -> second confirm
second_pa: second outcome -> second confirm
second_pc: second confirm
```

For holder `T`, phase `p`, component `i`, let `U_i` be exact prospective use after mandatory retention, excluding reservation only for the final task-byte component. Mandatory history rollup runs before every sizing step and the post-retention state must contain at most 32 ordinary interaction-history rows and 15 ordinary plan-history rows; row count is therefore not an escrow component. For each mutually exclusive handoff/wait/workflow producer, use exact frozen constructors separately; do not union incompatible maxima. Include history/evidence rollups, D25 roots, restoration branches, both roles, snapshot/operation, terminal children, and copies. Store `remaining_i = max over reachable S in F(p) of max(0,U_i(S)-U_i(T))`. Shared production and independent oracle implement the literal tree/constructors, not a schema registry.

`EVIDENCE_RESERVATION_WRITERS_V1` is the literal fresh-task-mutation registry:

```text
functions/taskDisposition.js =
 [assertAlreadyHandled,markNotApplicable:none,deferTask,markSelfHandling,
  markWaitingOnExternal,beginHandoff,acknowledgeHandoffOpened,
  recordHandoffReturned,continueHandoff,cancelHandoff,
  resolveHandoff:mutating,resolveWaiting:mutating,
  initializeFlowProgress,continueFlowProgress,clearFlowProgress,
  replaceTaskNotes,replaceTaskQuotes,
  mutateLegacyTask:applicable,persistLegacyPackingPlan:applicable]
functions/taskPlan.js =
 [supersede,confirmAmendment,undoConfirmation,reopen,
  resetAllTasks:root-fenced-delete-exception]
functions/getWorkflowQualifying.js =
 [submitWorkflowAnswers:COMPLETE,submitWorkflowAnswers:WAIT,
  submitWorkflowAnswers:WAIT_DISPLAY_FALLBACK]
functions/dispositionTriggers.js =
 [date_snoozed_deferred,event_snoozed_deferred,
  date_inprogress_user_action,event_inprogress_user_action,
  date_matching_waiting,event_matching_waiting,threshold_attention]
```

`markNotApplicable:present|unknown`, `resolveHandoff|resolveWaiting` descriptor-only branches, exact operation replay, and every other zero-task-mutation response bypass without changing either copy. `:applicable` means the branch is in this registry exactly when its target set contains either reservation holder; shared spawn/terminal-child writes are charged by the invoking registered transaction, never by an unregistered child helper. Reset may remove one holder/copy only through the root-fenced one-task deletion exception in the lifecycle table and cannot perform an ordinary admission mutation.

Before the first write on every listed committing branch, one shared admission/recompute seam reads both linked tasks and both reservation copies, constructs the complete two-holder prospective state, and in table order requires `U_i(post) + remaining_i(post) <= limit_i`. Every admitted fresh mutation atomically recomputes and replaces **both** reservation copies from that complete state and the same reachable transition tree, even when ordinary product data changes on only one holder. Both reservation writes are included in the actual and every future `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1` calculation. If present use decreases while a reachable maximum is unchanged, stored remaining increases accordingly. Exact replay changes neither copy. Admission also computes maximum `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1` over every remaining transition including original/replacement, at most one older replacement from rollup, ops/snapshots/children/index deltas, and requires ≤8,388,608; actual transition separately checks its exact charge. Production dispatch coverage and an independent oracle enumerate the same literal registry; any new task writer/action must update the registry, shared-seam coverage, oracle, and fixture in one reviewed change or fails the gate.

Lifecycle:

| Transition | Result |
|---|---|
| external supersede | create both `first_pa` |
| first outcome | both `first_pc` |
| first confirmation | both `first_cf_undoable`, same release time |
| timely undo (`server_write_time <= release_after`) | both `second_pa`, remove release |
| second outcome | both `second_pc` |
| second confirmation | atomically remove both |
| untouched initial-PA reopen | atomically remove both; retain D25 audit roots |
| expired first CF (`server_write_time > release_after`) | next mutation touching either holder validates both copies and atomically removes both before ordinary admission |
| reset | each one-task deletion removes its task/copy; root reset fence blocks writers; finalize requires all tasks absent |
| account deletion | write root deletion marker, recursively delete subcollections while preserving a minimal root tombstone; no task/reservation writer may recreate a copy |

One-sided absence is always `PLAN_GRAPH_CHANGED`, zero writes, except while the root reset fence proves the peer task itself was physically deleted by the fenced reset sequence. Identity/phase mismatch, malformed vector, or reservation on another lifecycle shape is likewise `PLAN_GRAPH_CHANGED`. Expiry cleanup never legalizes a one-sided reservation. No second undo window/time expiry exists.

Every user-linked provider call is fenced before account deletion, including FCM, SMTP, Twilio, and Anthropic. Add server-only `users/{uid}/outboundLeases/{deliveryId}` with exact `{schema_version:1,kind:"USER_OUTBOUND_LEASE",account_uid,delivery_id,channel:"fcm"|"support_email"|"support_sms"|"inventory_email"|"checkin_sms"|"anthropic",state:"sending",created_at,expires_at}`. `deliveryId = "uol1_" + <lowercase RFC-4122 UUID>` and must equal the path ID; `account_uid` must equal the parent UID. Complete canonical bytes are ≤1,024 and no valid collection may contain more than 64 documents. Client access is denied.

Immediately before each awaited provider call, one transaction reads the existing user root and an unsigned-document-ID-ordered `limit(65)` snapshot of its lease collection. `leaseNow` is the root snapshot's server `readTime`, never process time. Every returned document must be the complete exact schema above, with canonical concrete Firestore Timestamps, `created_at <= leaseNow`, and `expires_at == created_at + 600 seconds`; a path/UID/schema/time disagreement, unsafe arithmetic, or 65th document is `OUTBOUND_LEASE_INVARIANT`. That condition emits only the fixed invariant metric/alert, performs no provider/root/lease mutation, and is never treated as expired. The transaction deletes every exact lease with `expires_at <= leaseNow`; if 64 valid live leases remain it returns exact internal `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"not_sent",reason:"OUTBOUND_LEASE_CAPACITY"}`, increments only the capacity-refusal metric, and makes no provider call. Otherwise it creates one lease with concrete `created_at = leaseNow` and `expires_at = leaseNow + 600 seconds`. The root read makes lease creation conflict with marker creation. A transaction error or invariant is retryable and never silently projects as provider success. For best-effort FCM/SMTP/Twilio surfaces, capacity leaves the already-committed system-of-record unchanged and returns their existing public success while recording the fixed no-send result; for Anthropic-dependent surfaces, capacity/invariant/transaction failure takes that surface's existing retryable no-detail failure before the model call and before any model-derived commit. The 65th call never reaches a provider.

Only a positively accepted provider response authorizes a second transaction to reread and delete that exact lease tuple. Any provider throw, timeout, cancellation, response loss, process termination, ambiguous response, or lease-delete failure retains it until an exact later expiry cleanup; there is no `finally` deletion and no error is classified as definitive no-send after invocation. Every participating function and provider-client deadline is pinned at or below 300 seconds, so the 600-second lease outlives local execution; no code renews or extends it. Fresh marker-absent **begin**, and only begin, performs the same bounded lease query/validation/expired deletion in the transaction that creates DELETING; any live lease returns existing `DELETION_RETRY_REQUIRED`, and any invariant does the same after emitting the fixed alert. Marker-absent discover reads only the root and returns exact absent with zero lease query or write. Marker-present discover/begin only classifies authority and runs the existing cleanup reducer. Fixtures cover concrete read-time timestamps and no sentinel; send-before-marker, marker-before-send, query/root serialization, transaction retry, accepted-response/delete loss, every ambiguous outcome, crash after provider acceptance, 64/65, valid expiry, malformed/noncanonical/over-cap fail-closed, exact per-surface capacity projection, absent-discover with expired lease and byte-identical zero-write state, and begin deletion retry.

Future notification payloads are deliberately content-free. The frozen task-notification-intent contract retains no sender or FCM call. The one active FCM surface is support-reply multicast in `functions/supportAdmin.js`: one `fcm` lease surrounds each awaited multicast call, whose exact app-controlled payload is `notification:{title:"Peezy",body:"You have a new support reply."}`, no `data` member, `android:{ttl:0}`, and `apns:{headers:{"apns-expiration":"0"},payload:{aps:{sound:"default",badge:1,category:"PEEZY_SUPPORT_REPLY_V1"}}}`. The fixed category preserves H12d/H45 support-tap routing without a data payload or custom action. It carries no UID, thread ID, message text, or other user-derived value. Destination tokens are routing credentials already removed with `fcmTokens`. Any additional reachable FCM call is a boundary contradiction.

Support-reply fanout preserves the callable's pinned 10-second timeout and is exactly one bounded best-effort page per committed reply. `functions/supportAdmin.js` queries only the direct child `users/{uid}/fcmTokens`, orders by `admin.firestore.FieldPath.documentId()` ascending, applies literal `limit(501)`, and materializes at most that one snapshot. Before any capacity decision or lease creation, every returned row must have the exact token document-ID grammar and complete `{createdAt:<concrete Firestore Timestamp>,platform:"ios"}` map above, with path UID equal to the authenticated reply target; duplicate ID, missing/surplus/wrong-type/nonconcrete content is fixed `FCM_TOKEN_SCHEMA_INVARIANT`, makes zero provider/lease/token mutation, and never logs a token or UID. Zero rows returns with no lease/provider call. One through 500 valid rows create exactly one distinct `fcm` lease and one awaited multicast. Exactly 501 rows returns fixed internal `FCM_DESTINATION_CAPACITY`, increments one fixed count-only `phase2/support_fcm_destination_capacity_count` metric, makes no lease/provider/token mutation, and preserves `adminReplySupport`'s existing public best-effort success because the Firestore reply is complete authority. There is no cursor, second query, or second provider call in Phase 2.

`FCM_ACCEPTED_FAILURE_CODES_V1`, copied from the installed Firebase Admin 13.6.0 Messaging client and thereafter frozen independently of SDK drift, is exactly `messaging/invalid-argument|messaging/invalid-recipient|messaging/invalid-payload|messaging/invalid-data-payload-key|messaging/payload-size-limit-exceeded|messaging/invalid-options|messaging/invalid-registration-token|messaging/registration-token-not-registered|messaging/mismatched-credential|messaging/invalid-package-name|messaging/device-message-rate-exceeded|messaging/topics-message-rate-exceeded|messaging/message-rate-exceeded|messaging/third-party-auth-error|messaging/too-many-topics|messaging/authentication-error|messaging/server-unavailable|messaging/internal-error|messaging/unknown-error`. `BatchResponse` is positively accepted only when `responses` is an array whose count exactly equals the submitted token count, `successCount` and `failureCount` are safe nonnegative integers summing to that count, and every position has exactly one semantic outcome: `success === true` with a nonempty UTF-8 `messageId` of at most 4,096 bytes and absent error, or `success === false` with absent message ID and a FirebaseError code in that exact union. The observed counts must equal the positional outcomes. No message ID or SDK error text is persisted or logged. Throw, function timeout, cancellation, response/count/type mismatch, contradictory success/error, code outside the union, or any malformed semantic outcome is ambiguous: retain the lease and delete zero token documents. After an accepted response, first perform the shared exact-tuple lease-release transaction; failure retains the lease and performs no token cleanup. After successful release, one separate root-fenced transaction rereads only positions whose code is exactly `messaging/invalid-registration-token` or `messaging/registration-token-not-registered` and deletes only a document whose complete map and update time still equal the queried snapshot and whose root still has no deletion marker; every other accepted failure retains its token. Drift or a newly created marker preserves the row for ordinary account cleanup. This deletion-only transaction performs at most 500 writes, is idempotent, and never sends. Its failure records only a fixed cleanup code; a later ordinary support-send encounter may try the same deletion again but never replays this multicast. Fixtures cover 0/1/499/500/501 rows, exact order and one-page ceiling, capacity result/public projection/metric, malformed row and ID, held root/lease races, provider throw/timeout/cancellation, response and aggregate-count mismatch, every frozen code plus unknown code, contradictory/partial shapes, mixed success/failure, kill before/after provider acceptance and lease release, token deletion/update/recreation drift, marker creation before cleanup, and zero second provider call.

Support email subject and text are both exact `New support message. Open Peezy admin.` with HTML absent; support SMS body is the same literal. Inventory email subject and text are both exact `New inventory package. Open Peezy admin.` with HTML absent. Check-in SMS body is exact `New move check-in requires review. Open Peezy admin.` No application-controlled email/SMS member carries a record ID, UID, auth/email, name, task/title/text, address, move date, room/item, vendor, flag, note, or other user-derived value. Routing `from`/`to` values come only from fixed deployment configuration and are never logged. The fenced system-of-record commits before lease creation and the awaited provider call: `supportThreads/{uid}` for user support, the support reply message for FCM, the admin inventory-package row for inventory email, and the review/calibration/accountability transaction for check-in SMS. Provider failure never rolls back those records. This expressly reorders the current detached pre-write `notifySupport` call and the current package email-before-map order. `functions/index.js`, `functions/notifySupport.js`, `functions/supportAdmin.js`, `functions/packageInventory.js`, and `functions/submitCheckIn.js` use only these exact constructors through the shared lease owner. `functions/notifyAdmin.js` remains deployment-inactive; making it reachable is a boundary contradiction until it is added to this registry. Static active-export tests enumerate every FCM/SMTP/Twilio call, reject detached/unawaited calls, and reject any provider argument not built by the exact constructor.

Every reachable `client.messages.create` in `functions/processInventory.js`, `functions/researchTask.js`, `functions/peezyChat.js`, and `functions/resolveProvider.js` is Anthropic and acquires one `anthropic` lease for that exact awaited call; continuations acquire distinct leases. Static tests distinguish those clients from Twilio and reject every unleased model call. Account-deletion release additionally requires one accepted provider-native or contractual evidence artifact binding the exact Anthropic organization/workspace, API credentials, enabled products/tools, and deployed revision to zero retention of prompts, images, outputs, tool/search traces, and error material for all future traffic. If that complete zero-retention predicate cannot be proved, new Anthropic traffic and account-deletion invocation remain revoked; this manifest invents no retention exception.

Legacy provider copies are a pre-ship blocking gate, not an invented retention exception. After content-free notification revisions own 100% traffic, revoke every old outbound revision, prove its final execution time, and wait the full pinned provider/function window. The owner then deletes **all** Peezy-generated Gmail sent messages matching the legacy support/inventory sender+subject templates and all legacy Twilio outbound messages from the configured Peezy numbers to the configured admin destinations, from the first production timestamp through the proved cutoff. Provider-native before/after exports, counts, cutoff, query criteria, account identifiers hashed in the evidence file, and zero remaining matches are signed into one accepted evidence digest. For legacy Anthropic traffic, the accepted evidence must bind the exact old revisions and credentials to either provider-native deletion/zero remaining retained material or a finite proved maximum retention followed by the complete elapsed interval and post-expiry verification. An inaccessible, unknown, or unbounded retention destination blocks account-deletion release until an owner-approved, counsel-supported retention rule explicitly supersedes this gate; this manifest supplies no such rule. No raw provider export enters the repository.

FCM is a separate blocking provider gate. Its accepted evidence freezes Firebase project `peezy-1ecrdl`, iOS app `1:833904565407:ios:d03ca5a2ca59f5f10156aa`, sender `833904565407`, the deployed Admin app/service-account credential fingerprint and revision, configured APNs credential fingerprint and revision, pinned Firebase Admin/package-lock digest, client Firebase Messaging/Installations artifact digest, and the exact supportAdmin revision. The provider inventory enumerates registration-token and Firebase-Installation records, pending message queues, Firebase console delivery reports/Data API, every BigQuery or other delivery-data export, every Cloud Logging/Error Reporting route and copied sink, APNs handoff, and any support/diagnostic export enabled for that project/app/sender. For the legacy support-reply revision that sent message text and thread data, each destination must provide provider-native deletion with zero remaining match or a documented/contractual finite maximum retention followed by the complete elapsed interval and post-expiry zero-match evidence. For future traffic, invocation is restored only if the exact zero-expiry content-free payload, successful client Messaging-data deletion through `Messaging.messaging().deleteData(completion:)` plus Firebase Installations deletion, with accepted evidence for both exact successful callbacks and their postconditions, and a documented/contractual finite maximum residual provider retention or deletion procedure together cover every inventoried destination. Inaccessible, unbounded, unlisted, or identity-unbound provider state blocks account-deletion release; the only alternative is to remove the FCM send surface and client registration path from the accepted artifact before release. No token, installation ID, message ID, payload, destination, credential, or raw export enters the repository evidence.

Future runtime telemetry is likewise content-free: every active function—including `functions/notifySupport.js`—replaces dynamic UID, email, room/path/document/provider ID, title/text/address/vendor/error-message interpolation with a fixed event code plus bounded numeric counts and fixed error class. A static active-export walk covers every explicit log/metric/Error Reporting sink and every thrown/rejected `Error` crossing a deployed handler boundary; it catches and projects provider/SDK errors to fixed codes before a fixed-code throw, and rejects dynamic `Error.message`, stack, cause, request, payload, or identifier leakage.

The pre-ship platform inventory is not limited to one bucket. It enumerates every project/folder/organization logging route reachable from every old revision: `_Default`, `_Required`, every custom/user bucket, every aggregated or external sink destination, Error Reporting, and every derived/copy destination. The accepted evidence schema is an ordered destination array; each entry freezes destination type/ID, governing project/folder/org, inclusion/exclusion route, access result, retention or deletion authority, last-match query, last legacy-PII timestamp, expiry/deletion instant, and post-expiry/deletion zero-match count. After the redacted revision owns traffic and every old revision is proved terminated, account-deletion release waits until every entry satisfies its recorded deletion/expiry and zero-match proof. An inaccessible, unbounded, or unenumerated destination blocks release. Reducing retention or deleting a whole legacy log destination is permitted only as an independently owner-authorized operational action; no per-entry deletion capability is assumed.

Account deletion uses the permanent user-root document as its universal fence. The client generates `operationId = "adel1_" + <lowercase RFC-4122 UUID>` and a 32-byte cryptographically random unpadded base64url `proofNonce`; `proofSHA256 = lowercase SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({uid,operation_id:operationId,proof_nonce:proofNonce}))`, and `AccountDeletionCapabilityV1` is exact `{operationId,proofSHA256}`. The marker's `capabilities` array contains 1...64 exact capabilities, has unique `operationId`, is sorted by operationId under unsigned UTF-8, and rejects duplicate, reordered, same-ID/different-proof, unknown, missing, or surplus content. The complete constructed 64-member maximum and each member-field maximum must be `<= 16,384` `TaskCanonicalV1` bytes; cap +1 or any unreachable padded/surplus shape rejects without write. While application data remains, the root contains exactly one §11.3 DELETING-sweeping or DELETING-guarding branch; retained-copy convergence then advances through exact DATA_DELETED and AUTH_GUARDING to permanent ACCOUNT_DELETED. All times and branch-presence rules are exactly §11.3; every transition preserves `startedAt`, guard authority, and the complete existing capability set byte-for-byte except for one authenticated append below. The callable request is a closed union whose every case carries the durable-intent UID: authenticated conditional participation `{schemaVersion:1,action:"discover",uid,operationId,proofNonce}`; authenticated begin `{schemaVersion:1,action:"begin",uid,operationId,proofNonce}`; member resume `{schemaVersion:1,action:"resume",uid,operationId,proofNonce}`; or member/authenticated-overflow finalization `{schemaVersion:1,action:"finalize",uid,operationId,proofNonce}`. Before any root read or write, discover and begin require authentication and exact `request.auth.uid === request.uid`; mismatch is the nondisclosing capability-invalid result below. Discover requires the fresh startup-discovery capability already durable locally. One transaction reads the request UID root: marker absence returns exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"account_deletion_discovery",state:"absent",operationId}` with the requesting operationId and zero lease query, deletion, or other write. For a valid DELETING, DATA_DELETED, AUTH_GUARDING, or ACCOUNT_DELETED marker, discover or begin classifies the supplied capability atomically: an exact member is `member` with zero marker write; same operationId with different proof is capability-invalid; a distinct nonmember at count below 64 is appended and canonically resorted while preserving every time/state byte, becoming `member`; a distinct nonmember at count 64 performs no marker write and becomes request-scoped `authenticatedOverflow`. Both authority kinds then run the phase-selected §11.3 reducer. Discover never creates a marker. Begin on marker absence uses §11's one root-plus-lease transaction to validate/prune exact expired leases, reject live/invariant state, and create exact DELETING-sweeping plus its Storage work row with a singleton member; no other path prunes leases before marker creation. On marker presence it uses the same member/append/overflow classification and never returns an intermediate already-in-progress result. A lost response after a committed append is resumable by that member; a lost overflow response is retried only through authenticated begin/discover while auth remains, and the exact local fallback in §8 never claims remote completion otherwise. Resume may be unauthenticated but only continues when the recomputed capability is an exact member and never adds or retargets one. Finalize accepts DATA_DELETED, AUTH_GUARDING, or ACCOUNT_DELETED with either exact member proof, with or without auth, or a distinct nonmember while the registry count is exactly 64 and current request auth exactly matches the UID; the latter is authenticated overflow and performs no marker-capability write. DATA_DELETED and AUTH_GUARDING execute the retained-copy reducer and return only retry; only ACCOUNT_DELETED permits final success/replay. Every response is scoped to and echoes the requesting operationId. A malformed marker is protocol failure, never absence or overflow. A freshly confirmed client with missing/corrupt local intent persists its new confirmed-begin capability first and invokes begin; no separate recovery verb or pre-enrollment response gap exists.

The complete error union is literal. Invalid top-level/type/member order uses `invalid-argument/{schemaVersion:1,reason:"REQUEST_INVALID",field}` where field is the first of `request|action|uid|operationId|proofNonce`; `uid` is required in all four cases, and an unknown/surplus member is `request`. Unauthenticated discover/begin is `unauthenticated/{schemaVersion:1,reason:"AUTH_REQUIRED"}`. An authenticated UID mismatch, wrong UID, absent/wrong member resume proof, a nonmember finalize without the exact full-registry matching-auth overflow predicate, same-operation/different-proof collision, marker state/content, or malformed marker is the single nondisclosing `permission-denied/{schemaVersion:1,reason:"DELETION_CAPABILITY_INVALID"}`. Cleanup, transaction exhaustion, or Auth failure that may succeed on retry is `unavailable/{schemaVersion:1,reason:"DELETION_RETRY_REQUIRED"}`. No error contains UID, marker state, operation, proof, path, capability count, or raw server detail. Registry capacity is a measured authority kind, never an error or local blocked state. Swift exhaustively decodes this table: capability-invalid follows §8's purpose/authority-specific total reducer; retry-required and transport ambiguity retain the exact local capability/phase for retry; every unknown code/detail is protocol ambiguity and likewise retains bytes.

Phase 2 removes the UID-less persistent provider cache rather than pretending it is not user-derived.
