In `functions/resolveProvider.js`, `cacheResolved` and its call from `resolveProviderRequest` are deleted; a high-confidence resolution returns the same validated safe response without any Firestore mutation. `loadDirectory()` also admits into its process-global `directoryCache` and lookup output only complete exact `source:"seeded"` rows under the existing seeded schema; absent, malformed, or any other source is ignored and never retained. The cache-free revision initializes that cache empty, and no deployment artifact may prewarm/copy it from an older instance. New one-shot `functions/scripts/purgeLegacyResolvedProviders.js` is import-side-effect-free and exports pure `auditAndPurgeLegacyResolvedProviders({db,apply,emit})`; that core receives an injected Firestore adapter and contains no project, environment, process-exit, or argument authority. The CLI wrapper defaults to audit/read-only. Delete code is reachable from the CLI only when one invocation supplies exact `--apply --project-id peezy-1ecrdl --confirm-project peezy-1ecrdl --drain-evidence-sha256 <64-lowercase-hex>`, no unknown/duplicate argument, resolves public-v1 client project/database exactly `peezy-1ecrdl/(default)`, observes `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` absent, and binds the final digest to the separately owner-accepted platform drain artifact recorded in the run report. The CLI performs every production guard before invoking the pure core. Any guard, read, transaction, or delete failure exits nonzero; no delete precedes all guards. The core pages the entire `providerDirectory` collection sequentially with exact `limit(100)`, a run-local unsigned full-document-path cursor, and at most one resident page. Immediately before each delete, a bounded transaction rereads the row: exact literal `source:"seeded"` is preserved byte-for-byte because it is frozen WAIT display authority; every non-seeded value, including absent/malformed/resolved, is deleted under that reread. It repeats until two fresh complete passes observe zero non-seeded rows. Output contains only counts, fixed guard/class tokens, and lowercase SHA-256 document-path digests—never raw IDs/paths or document bytes. The run report captures exact command, resolved target, drain-artifact digest, per-class counts, and two-pass result. Rollout revokes invocation to **every** resolveProvider revision before shifting the cache-free bytes, proves final old execution termination `TproviderZero`, and waits through `TproviderZero + Commit.timeout_millis(60,000)` before the purge. If termination cannot be proved, undeploy all revisions, prove zero instances/executions, then wait the same full window. Only then may the separately authorized script run. After its accepted two-pass report, force the pre-purge cache-free instances to zero and deploy a fresh instance set from the identical accepted artifact digest; prove old-instance count zero, then restore invocation only to that fresh cache-free revision before account-deletion invocation is restored. No new Phase-2 writer may recreate a non-seeded provider row. One active-export fixture drives high-confidence resolution and asserts zero runtime Firestore writes. Separate core tests call `auditAndPurgeLegacyResolvedProviders` directly with an emulator/fake containing mixed seeded/resolved/malformed rows, proving seeded rows remain and still qualify frozen §122 WAIT authority while every non-seeded row is removed; the CLI arming path is never used against the emulator. A mandatory warm-cache fixture loads a legacy resolved, malformed, and missing-source row before purge, completes purge and fresh-instance restoration, and proves none is retained or returned. Fixtures also cover import/default, page sizes 100/101, continuation, each missing/malformed arming member, wrong project/database, `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` exiting before core invocation or write, unknown/duplicate argument, failure before/after nomination, an old `.set()` begun at timeout-minus-epsilon settling at plus 60 seconds, and static zero reachable `providerDirectory` create/update calls.

`ACCOUNT_DELETION_FENCE_WRITERS_V1` is the literal active user-linked mutation registry: `functions/index.js` requestConcierge, submitTaskFlow, and submitSupportMessage, with `deleteAccount`, `reconcileAccountDeletionStorage`, and `reconcileAccountDeletionAuth` as the exact marker/cleanup coordinator exceptions; every committing branch/shared child in `functions/taskDisposition.js`, `functions/taskPlan.js` (`inspectCommittedOperation` is read-only), `functions/getWorkflowQualifying.js` submitWorkflowAnswers, `functions/dispositionTriggers.js`, `functions/notificationIntents.js`, and `functions/spawnTasks.js`; process success/error plus `onInventoryRoomWritten` in `functions/processInventory.js`; `functions/researchTask.js`; `functions/peezyChat.js`; `functions/packageInventory.js`; `functions/submitCheckIn.js` plus `functions/submitCheckInCore.js`; `functions/entitlement.js`; `functions/validateSubscription.js`; and the writing tails of supportAdmin adminGetThread, adminReplySupport, adminMarkSeen, and adminSetThreadStatus. The `functions/taskPlan.js` coverage expressly includes creation of `users/{uid}/legacyResetMigrations/{migrationId}` in the same root-fenced transaction as every reconciliation mutation; neither initial creation nor exact replay can recreate migration authority after DELETING. Global scheduler lease/heartbeat-only writes, scheduled account-deletion generation-preconditioned Storage deletion, support invalid-token deletion, getWorkflowQualifying's read-only branch, healthCheck, adminListThreads, and requireMovePass are explicit no-user-write or deletion-only exclusions. Scripts, `functions/notifyAdmin.js`, and test-profile utilities are deployment-inactive and are named as such. New import-safe `functions/accountDeletionFence.js` owns marker validation/transitions, the writer-registry export, and the sole `USER_OUTBOUND_LEASE` transaction helper; its exact lease create/delete/prune mutations are coordinator exceptions, not no-user-write helpers.

`USER_OUTBOUND_PROVIDERS_V1` is a second literal active-export registry: `functions/index.js` submitSupportMessage → `functions/notifySupport.js` support email/SMS; `functions/supportAdmin.js` adminReplySupport → support-reply FCM; `functions/packageInventory.js` → inventory email; `functions/submitCheckIn.js` → one check-in SMS per flag; and every Anthropic call in `functions/processInventory.js`, `functions/researchTask.js`, `functions/peezyChat.js`, and `functions/resolveProvider.js`. Every entry is dominated by the shared lease helper and its surface's root-fenced business-record rule; no notification/model helper is a no-user-write exclusion merely because its only Firestore mutation is the lease. Static analysis starts at active `functions/index.js` exports and fails on every reachable Firestore/Storage mutation retaining UID-owned, user-derived, or UID-reference data that is neither dominated by the shared assertion nor in the exact deletion-only/coordinator list, and separately fails on every reachable provider call absent from or not dominated by `USER_OUTBOUND_PROVIDERS_V1`.

Before each registered Firestore create/update/delete, the committing transaction reads every user root named by the current and prospective UID projection, in unsigned-UTF8 UID order, requires `accountDeletion` absent on all of them, and commits every same-operation user-linked document in that transaction; a transaction that read an old root conflicts with marker creation and sees DELETING on retry. A single-owner write therefore reads `users/{uid}` once. Every mutation that adds, removes, or changes a UID reference fences both the old and prospective owners. In particular, validateSubscription transfer rereads the binding, then reads stored owner A and requester B roots in unsigned order; a deletion marker on either rejects, so transfer waits until A cleanup removes the binding and B may then create only under B's clean root. Nontransactional writers are converted to this seam, and long AI/network work rechecks only in its final committing transaction. Producer projection remains surface-specific: authenticated callables use existing no-detail `failed-precondition/COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED`; validateSubscription uses its existing no-detail HTTP rejection; scheduler/triggers use the definitive no-op rule with no wake/intent/refusal; support-admin uses its existing no-detail not-found result; and client writes receive Rules denial. No single invented wire error replaces these contracts. Fixtures serialize select-A→rebind-B in both orders and prove neither A-linked escape nor deletion of B's binding.

Firestore Rules define total one-access `deletionAbsent(uid)`. An owner create of `users/{uid}` requires `accountDeletion` absent from the prospective map. An owner update/delete requires it absent from both stored and prospective maps; no owner request may add, change, or remove it. Thus every owner root write is denied while DELETING, DATA_DELETED, AUTH_GUARDING, or ACCOUNT_DELETED, and every deletion-state root read is denied permanently. Every descendant `/users/{uid}/{document=**}` write and `userKnowledge/{uid}` write uses the same stored-root predicate. Task predicates reuse their already-required root read and retain exactly two access calls per task write and 16/20 for the frozen eight-write batch. The other descendant predicates gain exactly one root access per single-document write and remain within 10; `userKnowledge/{uid}` gains exactly one root access and remains within 10. Batches require the ordinary 20-call aggregate and have equal/over boundary fixtures, including deletion-state root update/delete and equal/+1 limits. Other-user/anonymous denial is unchanged. Storage Rules for both exact prefixes `inventory/{uid}/{allPaths=**}` and reserved `users/{uid}/{allPaths=**}` require owner auth, `firestore.exists(/databases/(default)/documents/users/$(uid))`, and `!("accountDeletion" in firestore.get(/databases/(default)/documents/users/$(uid)).data)` for create/update; a missing root therefore denies instead of dereferencing absence as authority. Reads retain owner auth and deletes are permitted only under the frozen owner/admin paths. Rules cannot add/change/remove the server marker; Admin marker/cleanup paths bypass client Rules.

`ACCOUNT_DELETION_EXTERNAL_FAMILIES_V1` is exact. Direct deletes are `userKnowledge/{uid}` and `supportThreads/{uid}`. Query deletes are `conciergeRequests.userId`, `taskFlowSubmissions.userId`, legacy top-level `inventorySessions.userId`, `workflowSubmissions.userId`, `workflowSubmissions.owner`, `subscriptions.userId`, `vendorReviews.userId`, `estimateCalibration.userId`, `admin/inventoryPackages/packages.userId`, and `adminNotifications.userId`; the two workflow queries union and deduplicate by full path. `giftCodes.redeemedBy` is query-scrubbed by removing only the UID field while retaining consumed status/time. A query page is nomination, never delete authority: immediately before every delete/scrub, its transaction rereads the document and requires the relevant owner field still equal the deleted UID; the workflow union requires at least one of `userId|owner`, and gift code requires `redeemedBy`. Drift skips that row and the next fresh sweep reclassifies it. Before deleting each user review, one transaction reads the marker, review, and referenced vendor, requires `review.userId == uid`, removes every `vendor.accountability.strikes[]` member whose exact `source == reviewId`, recomputes the already-frozen vendor accountability transition, and deletes the review atomically; a missing vendor means no retained vendor document can carry that strike. `ACCOUNT_DELETION_FIRESTORE_PAGE_SIZE = 100`; every family pages sequentially in unsigned full-path UTF-8 order, retains only a run-local cursor containing the full document path plus the filtered order value when the query requires one, materializes at most one page, and admits at most ten in-flight nominated-row transactions/deletes. A page advances in memory only after every admitted row settles; no page cursor/checkpoint is persisted. Timeout or process death retains only the marker and client capability, and the next invocation restarts that family from its beginning, with already-deleted rows idempotently absent. Equal/plus-one page, timeout-mid-page, and restart-from-beginning fixtures are mandatory.

Global scheduler-owned cleanup is exact. UID validation forbids `/`. Set `p = "users/" + uid + "/events/"` and `u = "users/" + uid + "/events0"`; query literal `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/quarantinedEvents` with `sourcePath >= p`, `sourcePath < u`, `orderBy(sourcePath ASC, documentId ASC)`, and `limit(100)`. The run-local cursor stores `lastSourcePath` plus the exact full `lastFullPath`; after validating that path is a direct child of `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/quarantinedEvents`, continuation is literal `startAfter(lastSourcePath, db.doc(lastFullPath))`. Immediately before deletion, transactionally reread and require that the row's present String `sourcePath` parses exactly as `users/{uid}/events/{oneNonblankId}`; any in-range malformed value blocks DATA_DELETED with fixed `ACCOUNT_DELETION_GLOBAL_PATH_MALFORMED`, while an exact row is deleted regardless of every other member. Deletion makes restart progress monotonic without a persisted global cursor; arbitrarily many unrelated quarantine rows are outside the range. The migration/pre-ship gate scans every preexisting quarantine row and requires its complete schema and exact parsable sourcePath before account-deletion invocation is restored; afterward only the frozen exact server writers may create these rows. Production and an independent query/index oracle prove the range bounds, order, and continuation against the final index registry. In one marker-fenced transaction, reread literal `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState`. For each of the seven complete cursor-map members `event_envelope_prepass`, `date_snoozed_deferred`, `date_inprogress_user_action`, `date_matching_waiting`, `event_snoozed_deferred`, `event_inprogress_user_action`, and `event_matching_waiting`, remove that entire cursor-map member iff its exact `afterPath` names the deleted UID; never remove only nested `afterPath`, and preserve every nonmatching cursor map byte-for-byte. Remove the complete `dueObservation` member iff its exact `oldestCandidatePath` names that UID; delete literal alert `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/phase2bAlerts/p2b1_oldest_due_over_900_seconds` iff its exact `candidatePath` names that UID. Preserve every unrelated state/alert byte. A present malformed path-bearing state/alert member whose UID attribution cannot be proved likewise blocks DATA_DELETED, emits the fixed code, and pages S3 rather than being retained silently. Fixtures preserve another UID's complete cursor, observation, alert, and quarantine row; put the deleted UID after more than one invocation's worth of unrelated rows; cover 100/101 matching rows, wrong-parent/depth cursor rejection, and mid-page timeout restart; and prove no orphan `afterAt` survives a matching cursor removal.

One complete cleanup sweep discovers direct subcollections below `users/{uid}` only through pinned public-v1 `FirestoreClient.listCollectionIds`; `DocumentReference.listCollections()` is forbidden. The authenticated Firebase UID is already validated nonblank/without `/`, and exact request is `{parent:<project peezy-1ecrdl database (default) user-root resource name>,pageSize:100}` with `pageToken` omitted. Call only `const tuple = await client.listCollectionIds(request,{autoPaginate:false})` and require an Array of exact length three. Let `[pageIds,nextRequest,rawResponse] = tuple`. `pageIds` and `rawResponse.collectionIds` must both be Arrays of 0...100 strings with identical length, order, and byte-equal values. Every ID must be unique, a nonblank one-segment UTF-8 string of at most 1,500 bytes, not `.` or `..`, without `/`, and is used byte-for-byte. Because pinned google-gax decodes proto3 with defaults, `rawResponse.nextPageToken` is required to be a String: exact `""` is terminal, otherwise it is an opaque nonempty UTF-8 value of at most 4,096 bytes. `nextRequest` must be null iff that token is empty; for a nonempty token it must contain exact byte-equal parent, numeric `pageSize:100`, and `pageToken` equal to that token, with no missing, surplus, or contradictory member. It is validation evidence only and is never invoked. Error, tuple-length/type/default mismatch, pageIds/raw-array mismatch, duplicate/invalid ID, missing/null/nonstring token, or nextRequest mismatch fails the sweep with zero nomination.

Hold at most the validated page and one collection's recursive-delete working set. Process IDs sequentially only after the complete tuple validates. Each collection uses only public `db.recursiveDelete(collectionRef)` from pinned `@google-cloud/firestore` 7.11.6 with a fresh default BulkWriter and must settle successfully before the next ID. The deployed `build/src/recursive-delete.js` must have SHA-256 `2a17d8fb6d975cdf3863c3826783f061a728a8a710fa7a3f81b10d0e7c407270`, `RECURSIVE_DELETE_MAX_PENDING_OPS == 5000`, and `RECURSIVE_DELETE_MIN_PENDING_OPS == 1000`; mismatch blocks deployment, no private API is called, and any aggregate/retry failure fails the sweep. Thus descendant deletion has a pinned 5,000-operation pending bound and never targets the user root. After all nonempty page IDs settle, discard the entire tuple and restart discovery with the original request and pageToken omitted. A nonempty returned token is validated but deliberately never followed, so deletion/insertion/recreation cannot be skipped and no global collection-ID ordering is claimed. Exact `pageIds:[]`, raw `collectionIds:[]`, raw `nextPageToken:""`, and `nextRequest:null` on a fresh absent-token call is the sole direct-subcollection-empty observation; empty IDs with nonempty token is invalid. This expressly reaches `legacyResetMigrations` and orphaned nested descendants whose parent document is absent. Fixtures cover emitted-default empty string, missing/null/nonstring token, tuple length/type, pageIds/raw length/order/value mismatch, terminal/nonterminal nextRequest mismatch, 0/1/100/+1 IDs, nonempty token ignored, and restart-from-absent-token convergence.

The marker-creation transaction must have left `outboundLeases` empty. Any lease document observed after DELETING is an invariant/race failure: cleanup validates it only for the fixed alert, blocks DATA_DELETED, and never treats or deletes it as ordinary descendant data until the registered lease owner resolves its exact expiry path. After direct-subcollection emptiness, the sweep processes every external family in the literal order above and then performs the exact global scheduler cleanup.

Storage deletion is conditional on one accepted bucket configuration, not on an ordinary live-object listing alone. Before account-deletion invocation is restored, owner evidence for project `peezy-1ecrdl` and bucket `peezy-1ecrdl.firebasestorage.app` records the exact accepted `BucketDeletionConfigV1`, metageneration, and lowercase SHA-256 constructor from §11.2 and proves: soft-delete retention is disabled; object versioning is disabled; no locked or unlocked bucket retention policy can delay deletion; default event-based hold is false; no event-based or temporary object hold exists under either application namespace; and no lifecycle/replication rule copies these objects elsewhere. Disabling soft delete governs only future deletes, so the gate separately inventories every legacy soft-deleted object in the bucket, waits through each maximum `hardDeleteTime`, and proves zero remaining; after soft delete is off it enumerates `versions:true`, permanently deletes every legacy noncurrent generation under `inventory/` and `users/`, and proves zero noncurrent generation there. Provider-native before/after exports contain only counts and hashed object-name evidence. If any retention/hold/version/copy policy cannot be disabled and proved, or a soft-deleted legacy object has not reached and passed hard-delete time, account-deletion invocation remains revoked. No manifest text treats a soft delete as erasure.

Every cleanup sweep and scheduled-reconciler visit first calls `const metadataTuple = await bucket.getMetadata()` and requires an Array of exact length two. Let `[metadata,apiResponse] = metadataTuple`: metadata is a nonnull plain object with exact bucket name, one canonical positive decimal metageneration, and the complete frozen relevant configuration projection hashing to the accepted digest; apiResponse is the pinned nonnull successful response with numeric `statusCode === 200` and supplies no bucket/config authority. One-/three-element, wrong-type, name/metageneration/config/hash, or non-200 mismatch is fixed `ACCOUNT_DELETION_BUCKET_CONFIG_DRIFT`, performs no Storage nomination/deletion or DATA_DELETED transition, and keeps deletion/reconciler progress blocked until the bucket gate is re-established. `@google-cloud/storage` is pinned by the deployed lock to 7.18.0. For each literal prefix in order, `inventory/{uid}/` then `users/{uid}/`, let `L = 100` for the direct callable sweep and `L = 20` for the scheduled reconciler, then call only `await bucket.getFiles({prefix,maxResults:L,autoPaginate:false,versions:true})` with pageToken omitted. Require exact three-tuple `[files,nextQuery,rawResponse]`; files is an Array of at most `L` File values. `rawResponse.items` is absent exactly when files is empty, otherwise an Array of the same length/order. Each raw/File pair must have byte-equal exact bucket/name, requested prefix plus nonempty tail, and a canonical positive int64 decimal-string `generationToken` in 1...9223372036854775807. `file.metadata.generation` must be that exact String; the SDK's Number-coerced `file.generation` is neither read nor used as authority. Raw and File metadata must have absent `timeDeleted` and absent/false event-based and temporary holds; a present deletion time or other live/noncurrent disagreement is `ACCOUNT_DELETION_NONCURRENT_GENERATION_PRESENT` and blocks, because the pre-ship gate requires versioning off and zero noncurrent versions. `rawResponse.nextPageToken` is absent for terminal or a nonempty UTF-8 String of at most 4,096 bytes; empty/nonstring is invalid. `nextQuery` is null iff token absent, otherwise exact `{prefix,maxResults:L,autoPaginate:false,versions:true,pageToken}`. Tuple/raw/identity/generation/hold/token/query mismatch fails with zero delete.

After that complete tuple validates, admit at most ten current-generation deletes at once. Construct only `bucket.file(name,{preconditionOpts:{ifGenerationMatch:generationToken}})` and call `delete()` with no option override; a `generation` FileOption and every Number conversion are forbidden. This exact string precondition targets only the current generation. Every delete settles before page completion; 404 is idempotent absence, 412 forces a fresh absent-token restart, and every other error fails. After a nonempty page, discard tuple/token and restart that prefix with pageToken omitted. Once `versions:true` returns its exact terminal zero tuple, call `await bucket.getFiles({prefix,maxResults:1,autoPaginate:false,softDeleted:true})` and require the same exact three-tuple discipline specialized to zero files, raw items absent, raw nextPageToken absent, and nextQuery null. Any soft-deleted item or continuation emits fixed `ACCOUNT_DELETION_SOFT_DELETED_OBJECT_PRESENT`, blocks DATA_DELETED/finalize, and is never treated as absence. Prefix-empty means both the fresh versions:true zero and fresh softDeleted:true zero under the accepted config. Immediately before DELETING-sweeping→DELETING-guarding and before every finalize attempt, rerun both prefixes' zero soft-deleted probes plus the bucket metadata gate. Finish prefixes sequentially; no cursor/checkpoint persists. Timeout/death restarts Firestore and both Storage modes from absent token. A sweep is empty only when fresh reads/listings observe zero root descendant, external delete/scrub, global quarantine/path scrub, outbound lease, live/noncurrent generation, and soft-deleted generation. Two consecutive complete empty sweeps remain mandatory; any hit resets the count.

Fixtures race scheduler/migration/outbound writers against marker creation; preserve another UID's cursor/observation/alert/quarantine; cover Firestore 0/1/100/101 IDs, tuple/token mismatch, delete/recreate, nested orphan-parent, and timeout/restart; cover Storage metadata tuple 1/2/3 and non-200/config mismatch, list 0/1/100/101 tuple/File/raw identity, terminal/nonterminal token/query, int64 max and max+1 generation without Number conversion, live plus legacy noncurrent invariant, 404/412/restart, held object, retention/versioning/soft-delete/config drift, legacy soft-deleted expiry and zero proof, configuration enabled between precheck/delete then reverted before soft-deleted probe, ten/+1 concurrency, scheduled-reconciler terminal-zero probes, and both prefixes. They prove a soft-deleted or hidden generation can never count as empty. The task/reservation guarantee is a strict subset of this universal postcondition, not its limit.

Two consecutive empty application sweeps perform only §11.3's DELETING-sweeping→DELETING-guarding transaction and return the exact queued retry branch; they never create or claim DATA_DELETED. Only the scheduled guard transaction writes DATA_DELETED and deletes its work row. A later discover/begin/resume against an exact DATA_DELETED marker returns or replays the data-authority wire exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"account_deletion_data_final",operationId,authorityKind:"member"|"authenticatedOverflow",startedAt,dataDeletedAt,replayed}` with frozen time encoding and request operation ID. Member/overflow predicates remain exact. Data authority does **not** delete Auth. Only after the client has durably purged/acked all eight local owners, the preference barrier, and the client-telemetry barrier does it enter auth_finalize_dispatched. Finalize freshly re-proves the accepted Storage/Firestore/Auth destination configurations, zero outbound leases, exact complete tombstone, absent work row, and member/full-registry-overflow authority, then calls Auth deletion. Success/replayed user-not-found wires remain exact; every other error retries. Coalescing, marker CAS, lost response, two-device membership, and export/options remain as frozen, with fixtures additionally requiring no local/provider outbound, cache, notification, telemetry, narration/transcript, recording, or credential residue before finalize.

No Storage-finalization function ships in Phase 2. Static export/import/call-graph gates require zero `onObjectFinalized`, `firebase-functions/v2/storage`, `onInventoryObjectFinalized`, or `handleInventoryObjectFinalized` reachability. Pre-ship provider inventory deletes every old/test GCS notification or Eventarc trigger bound to the bucket, proves its last delivery time, waits its complete finite event-retention/retry horizon, and proves topic/subscription/dead-letter/snapshot backlog plus post-cutoff match count zero. Only §11.3's durable work row, one-week guard, generation-preconditioned deletes, and scheduled reconciler establish remote-zero authority. Fixed metric `phase2/account_deletion_late_object_cleanup_count` is emitted by the reconciler for a positively deleted post-marker generation; it carries only a bounded count and never UID, object name, or generation.

Local deletion is crash-durable too. `DurableStoreRecoveryCoordinator.swift` owns one actor and exact target `PeezyAccountDeletion-v1.json`, using file protection `.completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication`. This capability file has **no quarantine sibling**. Its envelope is exact `{schemaVersion:1,fileKind:"ACCOUNT_DELETION_INTENT_V1",generationId,payload,sha256}`, capped at 16,384 canonical bytes; `sha256` covers the complete canonical envelope with only itself omitted, and every replacement uses the standard unique-temp/write/fsync/rename/directory-fsync sequence. Payload is exact `{schemaVersion:1,uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision,operationId,proofNonce,appleRevocation:"not_required"|"manual_required",googleRevocation:"not_required"|"sdk_disconnect_required"|"manual_required",googleProviderUid?,phase:"prepared"|"data_confirmed"|"purging"|"auth_finalize_dispatched"|"local_detaching",purpose?,authorityKind?,detachReason?,startedAt?,dataDeletedAt?,acks:["route"|"handoff"|"reset"|"workflow"|"room_capture"|"firestore_cache"|"notifications"|"google"],createdAt,updatedAt}`. Before the first intent write, the exact provider-context seam computes both dispositions; `googleProviderUid` is required only for sdk_disconnect_required. Ambiguous provider state chooses the appropriate manual-required value and never blocks Peezy deletion. These members are immutable. Prepared requires `purpose:"confirmed_begin"|"startup_discover"`, forbids authorityKind/detachReason/server times, and requires `acks:[]`. Data_confirmed requires `authorityKind:"member"|"authenticatedOverflow"`, both times, `acks:[]`, and forbids purpose/detachReason. Purging preserves that authority kind and both times, permits any exact displayed-order ack prefix, and forbids purpose/detachReason. Auth_finalize_dispatched preserves that authority kind and both times, requires all eight acks, and forbids purpose/detachReason. Local_detaching requires `detachReason:"auth_deleted"|"remote_unverified"`, forbids purpose/authorityKind/server times, and permits any exact displayed-order ack prefix. Every other combination rejects. `createdAt/updatedAt` are canonical local UTC-millisecond instants, creation sets both from one injected clock sample, and each transition preserves created and sets updated to `max(sample,old.updatedAt)`. Clock failure or a noncanonical/nonrepresentable sample or stored time writes nothing; an equal or backward valid sample is accepted and preserves old `updatedAt`. Every transition CASes complete generation/hash, both immutable provider dispositions/optional UID, phase/purpose/authorityKind/detachReason, operation/proof, auth tuple, times, ack prefix, and the target's inode-plus-digest observation. Fixtures cover Apple/Google/nonprovider/mixed/empty/ambiguous provider data, matching/mismatching Google SDK identity, every legal/illegal cross-product, forward/equal/backward valid clock samples, and clock/nonrepresentable failure.

S4's `LocalPrivacyPurgeCoordinator.swift` also owns process-wide `AccountDeletionCompletionPresentation` and exact non-sensitive target `PeezyAccountDeletionCompletion-v1.json`, no quarantine sibling, same protection/atomic replacement protocol, cap 4,096 canonical bytes. Its envelope is exact `{schemaVersion:1,fileKind:"ACCOUNT_DELETION_COMPLETION_V1",generationId,payload,sha256}`; `sha256` is lowercase SHA-256 over `TaskCanonicalV1` of that complete envelope with only `sha256` omitted. Payload is only `{schemaVersion:1,result,createdAt}`, where `result` is either the exact completed UI result `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"ACCOUNT_DELETION_COMPLETED",appleRevocation:"not_required"|"manual_required",googleRevocation:"not_required"|"revoked"|"manual_required"}` or exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"ACCOUNT_DELETION_REMOTE_UNCONFIRMED"}`. It contains no UID, auth tuple, operation/proof, provider UID/token, time from the server, or user content. The terminal reducer derives and durably writes this file from the exact intent result before conditional Firebase sign-out or linked all-scope creation; a write/fsync failure retains local_detaching and performs neither. Exact replay preserves the file byte-for-byte; a different pending presentation blocks rather than overwrite. The presentation actor may publish it only after the deletion/local-purge gate is clear and both intent and purge journal are absent. Relaunch loads it before auth-root rendering, so sign-out cannot remove its UI. `createdAt` is one canonical local UTC-millisecond instant from the injected clock; clock failure or a noncanonical/nonrepresentable sample writes nothing, and replay never resamples it. Unknown/malformed/over-cap/I/O state blocks terminal presentation with Retry and is never silently discarded.

The public immutable value is exact `CompletionSnapshotV1 = {generationId,sha256,result,createdAt}`. `AccountDeletionCompletionPresenting` is exactly `current() async -> CompletionSnapshotV1?`, `acknowledge(expectedGenerationId:expectedSHA256:) async -> acknowledged|stale|failed`, and `open(expectedGenerationId:expectedSHA256:provider:) async -> opened|stale|notOffered|failed`, where provider is only `apple|google`. `current()` returns nil only for an observed absent target; invalid/I/O state uses the blocked path above. The actor privately binds each returned snapshot to its no-follow device/inode/digest observation. Both actions reread complete bytes plus device/inode, require the exact expected generation/hash and private observation, and make zero opener/unlink call on drift. Acknowledge is the sole consuming action: it unlinks and directory-fsyncs only that exact file; observed absence is idempotently acknowledged, while a different present snapshot is stale. Open is offered only when the exact snapshot result carries that provider's manual-required disposition and uses only its frozen HTTPS URL. A matching offered open invokes the opener and returns opened or failed, but in every opener outcome it performs zero file mutation, unlink, acknowledgment, or dismissal; `notOffered` opens nothing. Multi-scene fixtures cover both-manual G1: open Apple leaves G1 byte-identical, open Google leaves G1 byte-identical, then acknowledge consumes it; they also hold a stale G1 action after G2 appears and prove it neither opens nor unlinks G2.

Already-authorized `PeezyV1App.swift` mounts the one presentation actor above the auth-root switch, and already-authorized `AppRootView.swift` renders its exact surface. Completed title is `Account deleted`. With neither manual disposition, body is `Your Peezy account was deleted.` and the sole button is `Done`. Apple manual-required adds exactly §11's Apple paragraph and button `Apple instructions`; Google manual-required adds exactly §11's Google paragraph and button `Google instructions`; both appear Apple then Google and retain `Done`. A link button calls the injected opener only for its frozen HTTPS URL after the user taps it. Remote-unconfirmed title is `Deletion not verified`, body is §8's exact remote-unconfirmed copy, and sole button is `Done`; it exposes no provider claim or link. A link action never dismisses or consumes the presentation; only Done invokes exact-snapshot acknowledge and dismisses after successful unlink/directory-fsync. No automatic URL open exists. Fixtures kill before/after presentation durability, sign-out, auth-root replacement, gate clear, each link no-mutation and Done unlink, and relaunch; test one/two/no manual paragraphs, labels/order/URLs, remote-unconfirmed, opener failure, stale presentation CAS, and zero UID-bearing bytes.

S4 also owns exact target `PeezyLocalPrivacyPurge-v1.json`, no quarantine sibling, protection `.completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication`, cap 4,096 canonical bytes. Its envelope is exact `{schemaVersion:1,fileKind:"LOCAL_PRIVACY_PURGE_V1",generationId,payload,sha256}`; `sha256` covers the complete canonical envelope with only `sha256` omitted, and every replacement uses unique-temp/write/fsync/rename/directory-fsync. Unknown, missing, surplus, wrong-kind/hash, or cap+1 content rejects without mutation. Its exact payload is `{schemaVersion:1,scope:{kind:"all"}|{kind:"uid",uid},providerContext?,terminalDeletionLink?,acks:["route"|"handoff"|"reset"|"workflow"|"room_capture"|"firestore_cache"|"notifications"|"google"],createdAt,updatedAt}`. `providerContext` is forbidden for all-scope and required for every UID scope; it is exact `{deletionOperationId,deletionProofSHA256,googleRevocation:"not_required"|"sdk_disconnect_required"|"manual_required",googleProviderUid?}`. Its operation/hash must match the deletion intent's exact operation and proof hash; provider UID is present exactly for SDK disconnect. UID scope is legal only when created under exact deletion-intent generation/hash/inode CAS and linked by these immutable members, and forbids `terminalDeletionLink`. `terminalDeletionLink` is forbidden for UID scope and ordinary auth-transition all-scope; it is exact `{deletionOperationId,deletionProofSHA256}` and required only for the terminal all-scope handoff below. Every auth-identity transition, including direct A→B, uses all-scope. Acks are only exact displayed-order prefixes. `createdAt/updatedAt` are canonical local UTC-millisecond instants: creation sets both from one injected clock sample, every update preserves created and sets updated to `max(sample,old.updatedAt)`, and a failed/noncanonical/nonrepresentable sample writes nothing while an equal/backward valid sample preserves old updated. Every transition CASes the complete envelope/hash, immutable scope/link/provider context, timestamp pair, ack prefix, and no-follow inode/digest observation. Fixtures round-trip the exact envelope and every legal payload branch, reject each missing/surplus/hash/kind/cap boundary, and kill every atomic-replacement boundary.

Auth transitions and account deletion join one process-wide singleflight, but a valid intent-linked UID purge has absolute priority and is never subsumed. An ordinary all-scope request arriving during that purge waits and reclassifies only after the UID journal reaches all eight owners—including the exact provider-context Google action—and both independent barriers before its deletion caller advances the intent. A different UID deletion request likewise waits and reclassifies. An all-scope ack is never mirrored into a UID deletion intent. Before creating any ordinary all-scope journal, the actor atomically classifies the deletion intent and purge target; a nonterminal valid intent creates/resumes its exact UID journal first. On launch, a valid deletion intent plus matching UID journal resumes before any signed-out/all-scope creation.

There is one narrow two-file terminal handoff. After an Auth-final success, or after an honest remote-unverified branch finishes its UID purge, the actor durably CASes the intent to exact `local_detaching` with all eight acks and the applicable immutable provider dispositions. Under a fresh exact intent/journal observation it then creates an all-scope journal with matching `terminalDeletionLink` **before** unlinking the intent. This exact linked all-scope journal may coexist only with that byte-matching `local_detaching` intent; any other all-scope-plus-intent pair is structural `LOCAL_PRIVACY_PURGE_FAILED` with zero owner/ack/unlink action. The linked all-scope reducer repeats all eight owners, preferences, and client telemetry. After it proves completion, it unlinks/fsyncs the matching deletion intent first, then unlinks/fsyncs the purge journal, then clears/reclassifies the gate. If a crash leaves the linked journal with the intent absent, the journal itself is sufficient durable handoff authority to re-prove its postconditions, unlink, and clear; if the intent remains, operation/proof and complete state must still match. This order closes unlink-before-gate-update without treating signed-out as deletion proof.

Each ack is appended only after the corresponding owner proves its postcondition and all required fsync/callbacks complete. Crash reloads the exact next owner. Ordinary all-scope completion runs the preference barrier, unlinks/fsyncs the journal, then permits gate reclassification. Neither a malformed journal nor I/O ambiguity is discarded; it blocks with Retry. Only the exact matching UID journal may mirror successfully committed acks into its deletion intent under a two-file digest CAS; on crash, either file's longer valid prefix is replayed only after re-proving every claimed owner, never by ordinal trust. Fixtures require `sdk_disconnect_required` with mid-purge Firebase sign-out, direct A→B, and a crash at every owner and terminal-handoff boundary: disconnect occurs exactly once, no all-scope Google ack substitutes for it, the linked all-scope purge follows, and no result falsely projects `revoked`. Direct A→B fixtures cover the current GID user being A, B, or nil; all three complete ordinary all-scope sign-out before B publication.

A completely valid target keeps `AccountDeletionGate.active(uid)`, except that a retained target may coexist with the exact §8 remote/file blocked snapshot until Retry. A successfully opened malformed, wrong-kind, wrong-hash, or illegal-phase target whose complete bytes fit the cap is privacy-unlinked only under its complete digest+inode observation and directory fsync; an over-cap target is unlinked only under its stable inode/device/size/mtime identity without reading or logging its contents. It is then classified as absent, never as successful deletion. Open/read/protection or identity drift yields the exact FILE_IO blocked state and removes nothing. `PeezySettingsView.deleteAccount()` calls the sole production coordinator. Before the first server await it atomically persists prepared purpose `confirmed_begin`, so crash/relaunch with matching auth retries begin and never discover. Startup/reinstall under a synchronously loaded signed-in gate persists purpose `startup_discover`, CASes `loading→active(uid)`, and retries discover, never begin; remote failure retains bytes and CASes active→blocked, while Retry reclassifies to active before its attempt. Before dispatching authenticated discover/begin, the actor requires the current signed authority's UID and auth epoch to match the intent; a same-epoch higher credential revision may atomically rebase the stored revision, while any other mismatch follows §8's member-resume/definitive-or-honest local branch and never calls an authenticated verb. Every request sends the durable-intent UID. After dispatch, a successful UID-bound response may advance only the exact request UID/purpose/operation/proof/envelope CAS even if local auth later changed—the server-side UID equality has already bound its target; the actor never retargets the response to the new account. A same-UID fresh user confirmation while `prepared/startup_discover` exists atomically changes only purpose to `confirmed_begin`, preserving capability/auth/times, before any confirmed-path await. An in-flight discover response then loses its purpose/envelope CAS and is discarded; confirmed path invokes idempotent begin. An exact absent discovery response must echo the request operationId and unlinks/fsyncs only that still-prepared startup-purpose target under complete CAS before auth/gate reclassification. Any data authority atomically advances the exact prepared target to data_confirmed, stores its exact authorityKind/times, removes purpose, and preserves operation/proof. No Discard action exists. Kills before/after unlink, discover/begin enrollment-or-overflow classification, prepared durability, data-authority durability, every local-detach transition, and response loss resume only from durable phase bytes. Fixtures hold discover while confirmation arrives for absent, DELETING, and response-loss branches. Pre-dispatch A→B drift proves zero authenticated call and zero B mutation; a response that loses request/purpose/envelope CAS proves zero advance; an already-dispatched server-UID-bound successful A response after local auth drift may advance only A's exact intent. Every branch proves consent is neither consumed nor duplicated.

Exact data success/replay advances data_confirmed, then purging with the same authorityKind. A local-only terminal branch enters local_detaching directly. Both phases drive the same eight-owner `PeezyLocalPrivacyPurge` reducer. For route/handoff/reset/workflow, each owner snapshots ordinary target and fixed quarantine as two independent CAS-protected sources. An absent source contributes nothing. Selective rewrite is permitted only when a bounded duplicate-rejecting parse proves one unambiguous envelope/payload whose aggregate and every candidate/singleton are fully valid under that store schema and either the complete envelope is valid or its sole defect is the outer-envelope hash mismatch. UID scope removes every named UID row/gesture/migration/diagnostic and replaces deleted-UID handoff auth as below; all scope writes each canonical empty target and unlinks every quarantine without decoding user identity. It re-encodes remaining UID-scope payload in the same physical role under complete digest/inode observation, preserving unrelated candidates canonically. Any other outer defect, duplicate, unknown, wrong type, invalid candidate/singleton, unsafe count, over-cap residue, or inability to prove the UID field universe makes that source privacy-wipe-only: replace target with canonical empty or unlink quarantine under observation/fsync, then emit fixed `LOCAL_ACCOUNT_PURGE_WHOLE_STORE` with only store/source tokens. Open/read/protection failure appends no ack. Each ack requires fresh bounded postcondition rereads; Retry resumes the journal prefix and local_detaching never calls account-deletion server APIs.

`RoomCaptureArtifactOwner` is one process-wide actor and sole authority for recording artifacts, extracted/downloaded frame buffers, and every Firebase Storage upload/download. Fresh captures require a current signed UID plus clear gate, use exact directory `<temporary>/PeezyRoomCapture/<lowercase SHA-256 UTF8(uid)>/` and lowercase UUID `.mp4`, reject symlink/nonregular/path substitution, and durably register the open recording with the actor before `AVCaptureMovieFileOutput.startRecording`. `InventorySessionManager`, `InventoryStorageService`, and `InventoryItemConfirmView` may hold frame bytes only through actor-issued UID/generation-scoped mutable buffer leases; release or purge zeroes the owned bytes before dropping storage, and no naked `Data` survives in a view/model property. The upload loop transfers ownership of every source JPEG buffer before its first await. `putDataAsync` is replaced by a registered callback continuation around the concrete `StorageUploadTask`; download uses the analogous registered `StorageDownloadTask` and returns only a buffer lease. Every task/buffer record carries the current media generation and UID. A callback whose generation is stale may only settle/release its record and can never advance the next frame, publish progress, write Firestore, or return bytes.

`RoomCaptureArtifactOwner` also owns every active `NarrationService` session and transcript buffer. `NarrationService.start` requires an actor-issued `{uid,mediaGeneration,narrationGeneration}` lease before installing the audio tap or starting `SFSpeechRecognitionTask`; `requiresOnDeviceRecognition` remains literal `true`, and absence of on-device support is a no-op—no server-recognition fallback exists. Partial/final callbacks CAS all three generations before appending text. Actor-owned transcript storage preserves the existing cap of 4,000 Swift `Character` elements (extended grapheme clusters), with no normalization change, and is held as an owner-issued lease carrying UID and both generations. `InventoryCameraView.pendingNarrationTranscript`, `InventorySessionManager.pendingNarration`, and retained Move-Pass retry state hold only that lease handle. Immediately before the already-registered `InventoryAPIClient.processInventory` task, the actor CASes gate/UID/generations and materializes one bounded immutable String solely for the exact `InventoryProcessingRequest`; that unavoidable SDK/request copy may live only until the registered callable settles. Gate activation prevents materialization, cancels when supported, and otherwise waits for that task to settle before room/media ack; its response is discarded on generation drift. After settlement the caller releases the request and lease and retains no app-owned String copy. No view/model property, retry record, callback, or successor task retains a naked transcript String across an await.

Gate activation invalidates the narration generation, sets `activeSegmentID = nil`, calls `request.endAudio()`, `task.cancel()`, removes the input tap, stops and nils the engine, removes the interruption observer, deactivates the audio session, overwrites mutable actor-owned transcript buffers, clears `accumulated`, `currentSegment`, and every transferred/pending transcript lease, waits for every already-materialized SDK request copy to settle and release, and awaits main-actor settlement. A stale callback may only release its lease and cannot append, snapshot, transfer, upload, publish, or start another segment. The room/media ack additionally requires zero narration task/request/tap/engine/observer/text/lease for the purged scope. Fixtures hold partial/final/error/interruption callbacks across UID and all-scope purge, delete between stop and each downstream transcript transfer, and prove zero stale transcript publication or provider call.

Gate activation closes admissions before inspecting the registry. UID purge cancels every matching-UID upload/download, stops/cancels matching recording/extraction, awaits terminal callback/cancellation, zeroes/releases every matching buffer, and prevents the next sequential-frame iteration. A foreign-UID provider task already in flight is not cancelled, but no next iteration may start and the room/media ack waits for that current task to settle; all-scope cancels every UID. Cancellation/settlement failure appends no ack. Only after registry settlement does UID purge boundedly no-follow enumerate and unlink its digest directory and fsync the parent; all-scope removes every valid digest directory. The scheduled Storage/Firestore guard is the sole remote-convergence authority and is independent of local task settlement. On first upgraded launch with no active capture/transfer, the owner boundedly deletes only root-temp regular no-symlink names matching `peezy_room_` plus one canonical hyphenated RFC-4122 UUIDv4 plus `.mp4`, where all hexadecimal letters are either ASCII uppercase throughout or ASCII lowercase throughout, the version nibble is `4`, and the variant nibble is `8|9|A|B` in the selected case. Mixed-case, non-v4, symlink, nonregular, and every other name remain and block neither purge nor deletion. Fixtures include byte-exact uppercase names from the committed `UUID().uuidString` producer, lowercase compatibility, and every rejection. Ack requires zero matching registered work/buffer and zero matching file, with no foreign task able to start a successor. Races cover active recording/extraction, upload held across A deletion, deletion between sequential frames, active download/returned buffer, cancellation failure, late stale callback, scene loss, crash/relaunch, A purge with a B transfer settling without cancellation, symlink, inode swap, and legacy-name boundaries.

`FirestoreLocalCachePurging` globally gates all Firestore acquisition before its ack. Privacy-first invalidation is exact: stop admissions; cancel every registered app listener/task; atomically invalidate the old `FirestoreRuntimeGeneration` so every late callback/result is discarded; call `terminate()` on that exact instance **without** `waitForPendingWrites()`; then call `clearPersistence()` on the terminated instance. Locally queued writes are intentionally discarded and may never replay under another identity. Only after clear succeeds may the owner obtain a fresh `Firestore.firestore()` instance, reapply the frozen production settings, and publish a new generation after probes prove the old instance rejects use and the new instance starts with no cached document or queued mutation. No consumer can reacquire between invalidation and publication. Terminate/clear/recreate/probe failure leaves the purge journal and global gate blocked with the old generation permanently invalid; no deletion completion or new Firestore use is reported. Persistence remains enabled after the empty restart; a later deletion repeats this barrier. Tests cover cached A/B documents without listeners, active listener teardown, offline pending A and B writes discarded with zero replay after network return, late completion/callback rejection, terminate/clear/recreate/probe failure, every crash boundary, and A→B reload.

`Peezy-4-0-Info.plist` freezes all three exact Boolean collection flags in §§11/11.1 as `false`, and the pre-configuration fixture proves every bundled value is visible before `FirebaseApp.configure()`; production never sets `Messaging.messaging().isAutoInitEnabled = true`. One process-wide `NotificationIdentityAuthority` actor conforms to `NotificationIdentityPurging` and is the sole production owner/caller of Messaging delegate assignment, APNs register/unregister, APNs-token forwarding, `Messaging.token(completion:)`, `Messaging.messaging().deleteData(completion:)` (the Objective-C `deleteDataWithCompletion:` surface), `Installations.installations().delete(completion:)`, and `users/{uid}/fcmTokens` writes. The broader Messaging delete is mandatory because default-token deletion alone does not delete nondefault-sender tokens or Messaging check-in data. Remove `pendingFCMToken` and every separately persisted registration-allowed Boolean. Registration authority is derived only from the live tuple `{gateGeneration,gateState,signedAuthIdentity,startupDiscoveryGeneration}` and is true only for `gateState == clear`, one signed-in auth tuple, and a successful startup-discovery generation bound to that same gate generation and auth tuple. `NotificationRegistrationGeneration` is a process-lifetime safe monotonic counter owned by the actor; overflow blocks with the existing local-purge failure and performs no registration or ack.

The actor's ephemeral state is the closed union `closed(registrationGeneration)|awaitingAPNs(context)|apnsFailed(context)|fetchingFCM(context,fetchOrdinal)|uploading(context,fetchOrdinal)|ready(context,fetchOrdinal)`, where exact context is `{registrationGeneration,gateGeneration,uid,authEpochUUID,credentialRevision,startupDiscoveryGeneration}` and every ordinal is safe monotonic. Gate activation first atomically increments the registration generation and enters closed, closing delegate/token/upload admission; it then on the main actor unregisters remote notifications, removes all delivered and pending requests, and sets badge zero, next awaits `Messaging.messaging().deleteData(completion:)`, and finally awaits Firebase Installations deletion. Any main-actor, Messaging, Installations, reread, counter, or settlement failure leaves the notifications ack absent. Before ack, the actor proves zero delivered/pending requests, badge zero, and zero actor-retained APNs/FCM task, token buffer, or upload for the retired generation; an OS callback arriving later can only enter the closed-state check and is discarded. Notification-tap callbacks during the gate still take §8's no-op opener/dispatch branches and invoke their exact completion once without recording payload.

A later clean signed-in startup discovery CASes `closed|apnsFailed→awaitingAPNs(context)` and explicitly requests APNs registration; it does not request an FCM token yet. Only a current matching `didRegisterForRemoteNotificationsWithDeviceToken` forwards that APNs token inside the actor, CASes `awaitingAPNs→fetchingFCM` with one fresh ordinal, and starts exactly one explicit `Messaging.token(completion:)` request carrying the complete context/ordinal. A matching `didFailToRegisterForRemoteNotificationsWithError` CASes only `awaitingAPNs→apnsFailed`, emits fixed `NOTIFICATION_APNS_REGISTRATION_FAILED`, allocates no ordinal, and uploads nothing; only a later explicit clean-discovery/Retry trigger may CAS that same context back to awaitingAPNs. The `didReceiveRegistrationToken` argument is never buffered, persisted, hashed, logged, or assigned to a UID because the callback contains no origin generation. In `ready` it may only trigger one fresh explicit token fetch under the current context; in `fetchingFCM|uploading` it coalesces with the existing attempt, and in `closed|awaitingAPNs|apnsFailed` it is discarded. Sole token authority is the result of the actor-issued explicit fetch, accepted only after complete context/ordinal CAS before and after the callback and before/after the Firestore await. A valid token is an opaque UTF-8 document-ID segment of 1...1,500 bytes, not `.` or `..`, with no `/` or NUL; the sole replace-not-merge write is exact `users/{uid}/fcmTokens/{token} = {createdAt:<server Timestamp>,platform:"ios"}` under the same auth tuple, gate generation, notification generation, and Firestore runtime generation plus the root-deletion fence. Success enters ready; fetch or upload failure emits only its fixed diagnostic, uploads no stale bytes, and returns to ready under the same context/ordinal. APNs failure remains apnsFailed and cannot reach a token fetch. Missing, surplus, wrong-type, malformed-ID, or drifted content is never sent or retained.

S7 imports Firebase Installations from the pinned transitive Firebase Messaging dependency; a clean compile/import gate is mandatory and `Peezy 4.0.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` remains byte-identical. Failure is a boundary contradiction, never authority to omit FID deletion, enable Messaging auto-init, or expand project scope.
