The shared seam returns only this internal closed value before any producer-specific projection:

```text
ReservationAdmissionFailureV1 =
 {schemaVersion:1,
  kind:"ORDINARY_CAP"|"ESCROW_CAP"|"ACTUAL_WRITE_BUDGET",
  holderRole:"ORIGINAL"|"REPLACEMENT",
  taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,
  component:"EVIDENCE_RECORD_COUNT"|"EVIDENCE_RECORD_BYTES"|
   "FOREIGN_ROOT_ENTRY_COUNT"|"FOREIGN_ROOT_BYTES"|
   "INTERACTION_HISTORY_BYTES"|"TASK_INTERACTION_STATE_BYTES"|
   "SEMANTIC_SNAPSHOT_BYTES"|"TASK_DOCUMENT_BYTES"|
   "FIRESTORE_TRANSACTION_BYTES",
  required,available}
```

Component/holder selection uses the exact ordering below. A callable branch projects `ORDINARY_CAP` to retained `EVIDENCE_UNKNOWN` or `TASK_CAP_EXCEEDED`, `ESCROW_CAP` to `EVIDENCE_CAP_RESERVED`, and `ACTUAL_WRITE_BUDGET` to the unchanged `TERMINAL_WRITE_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` only when that request supplies its required operation ID or submission token. No internal value is serialized directly or decoded by Swift.

A scheduler candidate has no callable error channel. Scheduler `ORDINARY_CAP|ESCROW_CAP` performs zero task/wake/intent/reservation mutation and atomically creates/updates the candidate's D4 refusal with the same fingerprint and `reasonCode:"AUTHORITY_REFUSAL"`; only successful refusal bookkeeping permits the ordinary cursor to pass, and threshold remains run-local. The actual candidate mutation first computes exact `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1` over the candidate, both reservation holders, the at-most-one older rollup replacement, any operation/snapshot/terminal child, wake/intent, current/prospective refusal mutation or deletion, and every current/prospective index-entry delta. Production and an independent literal-constructor oracle must prove every admitted scheduler branch strictly below 8,388,608 bytes. A computed over-bound result is systemic `SCHEDULER_WRITE_BUDGET_INVARIANT`: write no task, reservation, wake, intent, refusal, cursor, or completion; keep heartbeat state `running`; mark the evaluation unsuccessful; emit structured error fields only `{code:"SCHEDULER_WRITE_BUDGET_INVARIANT",lane,runOrdinal}`; increment exact log-based metric `phase2/scheduler_write_budget_invariant_count`; and page named owner S3 on any positive value. It is never a refusal and never projected as a callable error. The pre-ship checklist records metric and alert-policy resource IDs plus a successful test notification. Producer-specific precedence is request/auth/graph validation, ordinary cap, escrow cap, then actual budget/invariant.

Add these exact §4.6 `resource-exhausted` detail members; every unshown member is forbidden:

```text
{schemaVersion:1,reason:"TASK_CAP_EXCEEDED",taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,
 component:"FOREIGN_ROOT_ENTRY_COUNT"|"FOREIGN_ROOT_BYTES"|
 "INTERACTION_HISTORY_BYTES"|"TASK_INTERACTION_STATE_BYTES"|
 "SEMANTIC_SNAPSHOT_BYTES"|"TASK_DOCUMENT_BYTES",
 required,available}
```

For this member `required = U_i(post)` and `available = limit_i`, both safe nonnegative integers, for the first ordinary stored-component overflow in the displayed cap-vector order after evidence. The frozen prospective evidence-set algorithm retains `failed-precondition/EVIDENCE_UNKNOWN` for ordinary `EVIDENCE_RECORD_COUNT|EVIDENCE_RECORD_BYTES` overflow and adds no details; this compatibility mapping is the only ordinary-cap exception. Swift validates every listed `TASK_CAP_EXCEEDED` component and maps it to capacity recovery.

```text
{schemaVersion:1,reason:"EVIDENCE_CAP_RESERVED",taskDocumentId,taskInstanceId,
 component:"EVIDENCE_RECORD_COUNT"|"EVIDENCE_RECORD_BYTES"|
 "FOREIGN_ROOT_ENTRY_COUNT"|"FOREIGN_ROOT_BYTES"|
 "INTERACTION_HISTORY_BYTES"|"TASK_INTERACTION_STATE_BYTES"|
 "SEMANTIC_SNAPSHOT_BYTES"|"TASK_DOCUMENT_BYTES"|
 "FIRESTORE_TRANSACTION_BYTES",required,available}
```

Callable failure precedence is exact: validate request/auth/immutable graph authority first; apply ordinary component caps (`EVIDENCE_UNKNOWN` for the two evidence components, otherwise `TASK_CAP_EXCEEDED`); then apply capacity escrow as `EVIDENCE_CAP_RESERVED`; then apply the actual transition's `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1` as the unchanged `failed-precondition/TERMINAL_WRITE_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`. For stored escrow components, `EVIDENCE_CAP_RESERVED.required = remaining_i(post)` and `available = limit_i - U_i(post)`. For `FIRESTORE_TRANSACTION_BYTES`, required is the maximum exact future `FirestoreWriteBudgetV1` charge over the reachable tree and available is literal 8,388,608. The first escrow failure in displayed order wins. Swift validates the exact callable shapes and maps them to capacity recovery. Every rejection writes no task/history/evidence/op/snapshot/child/reservation byte. §7.4 adds empty-index overrides for both `tasks.foreignRoots` and `tasks.evidence_reservation`.

Failure selection is component-major in the displayed error-union order and, within each stored component, holder-role ORIGINAL before REPLACEMENT; the detail names that first failing holder's document/instance. `FIRESTORE_TRANSACTION_BYTES` is evaluated last and always names ORIGINAL while `required` remains the complete two-holder transaction maximum. Fixtures make both holders fail the same/different components and cover a transaction-only failure.

Fixtures use exact/+1 only where a schema-valid reachable state can construct that boundary; otherwise they generate the exhaustive reachable maximum, prove algebraic headroom, and exercise every underlying field/count cap +1 without padding or surplus members. They compute `M` exhaustively, prove `M <= 16,384`, and construct a schema-valid task whose reservation-omitted bytes are exactly 638,952 plus a maximum reachable map `M` when those maxima are constructible together, proving the fixed headroom and rejecting one additional omitted byte by the 638,952 component cap; if either exact maximum is not constructible, they use the exhaustive reachable maximum plus algebraic headroom and every underlying adjustable-field cap +1, never padding or surplus. The ordinary no-reservation task retains separate exact 655,360/+1 coverage. Every `TASK_CAP_EXCEEDED` member has exact/equal/+1 producer and Swift-decoder coverage; the two evidence components separately prove the retained `EVIDENCE_UNKNOWN` mapping. The oracle asserts mandatory rollup precedes sizing, with 32→33 interaction rows and 15→16 plan rows rolling rather than returning a capacity error. Fixtures additionally cover maximum first-confirm/undo/second-outcome/second-confirm; every literal registry branch; a spy that fails any reserved-holder write not preceded by the shared two-copy admission seam; exact replay and zero-mutation bypass; an unregistered/new action failing coverage; unrelated writer consuming each cap; an original-only mutation increasing a replacement-side future maximum and atomically updating both vectors; either-side-triggered atomic two-copy expiry cleanup; undo exact deadline and one tick late; every one-sided absence rejection except a root-reset-fenced deleted peer; atomic second-CF/reopen removal; retained roots; reset killed after either task; replay each phase; malformed maps; constructible exact 8MiB/+1 future transition including both reservation writes or, when exact construction is impossible, exhaustive reachable maximum plus underlying bounds; owner read allowed plus owner add/change/remove denial for both embedded fields; and no admitted cycle later failing solely because reserved capacity was consumed.

Scheduler capacity fixtures cover every lane and retry/fresh source; ordinary versus escrow refusal with exact fingerprint/backoff/cursor behavior; refusal-bookkeeping failure; threshold no-cursor behavior; the literal budget constructor/oracle over candidate, both holders, older replacement, intent, refusal, children, and index deltas; a constructible maximum strictly below 8 MiB; zero-value no-alert behavior; and a synthetic over-bound invariant proving zero task/refusal/cursor/completion mutation, heartbeat still running, no callable error, exact log fields/metric increment, S3 page, recorded resource IDs, and successful test notification. Callable fixtures separately prove every internal-kind projection and that `TERMINAL_WRITE_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` always has its required operation/submission identity.

### 11.1 Mandatory client-telemetry and Release-log closure

`ClientTelemetryPrivacyPurging` is declared in `DurableStoreReadiness.swift`. One process-wide `ClientTelemetryPrivacyAuthority`, owned by `LocalPrivacyPurgeCoordinator.swift`, is initialized in `Phase2ProductionRuntime` before the startup barrier and injected into the deletion coordinator as an independent mandatory barrier parallel to preferences—not as a ninth `acks[]` member. Gate activation closes telemetry admission before the first deletion or auth-transition await. Every UID purge, all-scope purge, and `local_detaching` pass must complete this barrier before `auth_finalize_dispatched`, presentation publication, any intent/journal unlink, or gate clear; each of those boundaries reruns its complete postcondition.

`Peezy-4-0-Info.plist` adds exact Boolean `FIREBASE_ANALYTICS_COLLECTION_ENABLED = false` and `FirebaseCrashlyticsCollectionEnabled = false` beside `FirebaseMessagingAutoInitEnabled = false`; all three values must be observable before `FirebaseApp.configure()`. The accepted artifact never calls either telemetry collection-enable API with `true`, never calls `sendUnsentReports`, and contains zero production `Analytics.logEvent`, nonnil Analytics user-ID/property write, raw `Crashlytics.record(error:)`, Crashlytics log/custom-key/user-ID write, or direct Crashlytics initialization outside `ClientTelemetryPrivacyAuthority`. `AnalyticsEvents.swift` remains API-compatible but performs no provider call. Remove the raw Crashlytics import/call at `AssessmentCoordinator.swift:18,651`, `IdentityService.swift:14,95`, and `WorkflowService.swift:10,75`, and remove `PeezyV1App.swift:12,39` direct initialization.

The barrier calls in exact order `Analytics.setAnalyticsCollectionEnabled(false)`, `Analytics.setUserID(nil)`, `Analytics.setUserProperty(nil,forName:"has_subscription")`, and `Analytics.resetAnalyticsData()`. It then owns the sole process-lifetime `Crashlytics.crashlytics().checkForUnsentReports(completion:)`, permitted only because bundled collection is false. Exact `false` is the Crashlytics-local-absence observation for that process. Exact `true` calls `deleteUnsentReports()` once and deliberately does not complete the barrier: the pinned public API has no deletion completion, so the journal and gate remain blocked as existing `LOCAL_PRIVACY_PURGE_FAILED` with fixed diagnostic/UI copy `Close and reopen Peezy to finish clearing local diagnostics.` A same-process Retry remains blocked; only a fresh cold process whose sole check returns false completes the barrier. Callback loss or duplication, an impossible callback before the request, or SDK/API drift likewise stays blocked. Once a process has observed false, that observation is reusable for later purges in the same process because every nonfatal record/log entry point is statically absent; a process crash necessarily creates a new process and a fresh check. Fixtures cover initial false, true→delete→same-process no-ack→cold-launch false, callback loss/duplicate, crash/relaunch, Analytics call order, preconfigure flags, and zero telemetry admission after gate closure.

Firebase Analytics and Crashlytics form one additional blocking provider-evidence family. Accepted evidence binds project `peezy-1ecrdl`, iOS app `1:833904565407:ios:d03ca5a2ca59f5f10156aa`, Firebase iOS SDK `12.7.0`/`Package.resolved`, and the accepted app revision, then inventories Analytics app-instance/event/user-property/audience/Ads/attribution/BigQuery/export destinations and Crashlytics crash, nonfatal, log, custom-key, Firebase Sessions/App Quality Sessions, MetricKit/ANR, BigQuery/Data API, and every export/linked-project destination. Every legacy destination requires provider-native deletion with zero remaining match or a documented/contractual finite maximum retention followed by the complete elapsed interval and zero-match proof. Inaccessible, unbounded, unenumerated, or identity-unbound state blocks `deleteAccount` restoration. Future traffic is closed by the bundled false flags and static zero-send/zero-record gate; re-enabling either product is later scope.

The Release product has zero active `print`, `debugPrint`, `dump`, or `NSLog` argument and zero raw `os_log`/`Logger` argument derived from UID/auth, URL/path/document/workflow/task/status, request/response/payload, user-entered value, or `Error`/`localizedDescription`. These exact already-authorized paths are broadened solely to delete their Release-reachable rejected sinks: `FlowDefinition.swift`, `FlowEngineView.swift`, `InAppTaskFlows.swift`, `MoveAnswersStore.swift`, `BookYourMoversView.swift`, `SuppliesKitView.swift`, `SupportChatService.swift`, `PeezyV1App.swift`, `DailyDoseEngine.swift`, `TaskActionService.swift`, `PeezyHomeViewModel.swift`, and `PeezyStackViewModel.swift`. Debug calls are allowed only when lexically enclosed by `#if DEBUG`; a `#Preview` sink is allowed only when the accepted Release SIL and link-map proof show that its enclosing preview expansion emits no Release-reachable symbol. A whole-target Release-call-graph fixture rejects every other dynamic sink and permits only fixed literal event codes plus bounded numeric counts. Adversarial `NSError` values carry UID/path/URL/content in domain, description, userInfo, underlying error, and stack and prove no Release sink receives any member.

### 11.2 Exact provider configuration and retained-copy closure

This subsection is the sole Storage/Firestore retained-copy and remote-convergence authority; no earlier finalizer or projection shorthand enters the amended specification. No Storage finalizer is authorized. Any legacy/test event delivery has finite retention and is removed/drained by the provider-copy gate; it is never deletion authority. Durable scheduler work plus the provider-copy gates below are the only server convergence authority.

`BucketDeletionConfigV1` is the exact unknown-rejecting map `{schemaVersion:1,name,metageneration,softDeletePolicy,versioning,retentionPolicy,defaultEventBasedHold,objectRetention,lifecycle,logging}` projected from pinned `@google-cloud/storage` 7.18.0 `bucket.getMetadata()` only after its exact returned tuple is validated. `name` is literal `peezy-1ecrdl.firebasestorage.app`; `metageneration` is a canonical positive int64 decimal string. For each of the seven selected SDK-returned own-property values (`softDeletePolicy`, `versioning`, `retentionPolicy`, `defaultEventBasedHold`, `objectRetention`, `lifecycle`, `logging`), absence is projected to literal null; otherwise the complete already-parsed value is recursively copied through `CanonicalProviderJSONV1`: only null, Boolean, valid UTF-8 String, finite safe Number normalized to its canonical decimal value with no negative zero, provider-order Array, or own-property plain Object whose keys are sorted under unsigned UTF-8. Present `defaultEventBasedHold` must be Boolean; each of the other six present top-level values must be a nonnull plain Object. Undefined, inherited property, prototype other than `Object.prototype|null`, accessor, symbol, BigInt, function, nonfinite/unsafe Number, invalid Unicode, repeated own-key traversal, or aggregate canonical bytes above 65,536 rejects. No claim is made about unrecoverable raw JSON duplicate spelling or pre-parse number lexemes. No nested SDK-returned member is ignored: any added/changed member is copied and therefore changes the accepted digest. Int64/duration members must arrive as canonical decimal Strings and timestamps as canonical RFC-3339 Strings. `TaskCanonicalV1` of that exact map is the sole projection and its lowercase SHA-256 is `bucketDeletionConfigSHA256`.

The accepted pre-ship evidence records one byte-exact map and digest; runtime accepts only that map's exact null-versus-false/map representation, name, metageneration, and digest. Its semantics must prove soft-delete duration zero, versioning disabled, retention policy null, default event-based hold false, object-retention mode absent, no lifecycle copy/delete transition, and logging null. A nonnull logging map is not silently accepted: its destination bucket and prefix first enter the destination inventory below, are deleted/expired to zero, and the production bucket is then changed to the single accepted logging-null map under separately authorized operations. Every object listing independently rejects a temporary/event hold. Fixtures cover raw absent versus false/null, surplus selected/raw member, wrong prototypes/types, input-key ordering normalization, provider array ordering, unrelated bucket metadata ignored, digest drift, and synthetic metadata that has the right semantics but was not the accepted byte representation.

`StorageDeletionDestinationV1` is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"gcs_notification"|"eventarc_topic"|"pubsub_subscription"|"pubsub_dead_letter"|"pubsub_snapshot"|"usage_log"|"storage_insights"|"inventory_report"|"storage_transfer"|"backup"|"export",resourceName,governingProject,identity,filter,payloadProjection,retentionSeconds,deletionAuthority,cutoff,backlogCount,postCutoffMatchCount,evidenceSHA256}`. Every ordinary String is nonblank valid UTF-8 of at most 4,096 bytes; `filter`, `payloadProjection`, and `deletionAuthority` are at most 8,192 bytes; every SHA is exactly 64 lowercase hex; counts/seconds are safe nonnegative integers; cutoff is a canonical UTC instant; and the complete map is at most 16,384 `TaskCanonicalV1` bytes. The digest covers the exact provider-native evidence with user/object bytes excluded. The accepted array has at most 4,096 entries and 67,108,864 canonical bytes, is sorted by `(kind,resourceName)` under unsigned UTF-8, and has no duplicate. Discovery inventories bucket notification configurations; every Eventarc transport topic, subscription, retry window, dead-letter topic/subscription, and snapshot; legacy access/usage-log bucket and prefix; Storage Insights/inventory reports and their destination buckets; Storage Transfer jobs; backup/export jobs; and every destination named by lifecycle/replication or an old revision. Each listed destination must have finite proved retention/deletion, cutoff-bound backlog zero, and post-cutoff zero match. An unlisted, inaccessible, synthetic, identity-unbound, over-cap, or unbounded destination blocks `deleteAccount` restoration. Every old/test finalizer transport appears in this array through deletion/expiry and is absent from the accepted future configuration.

`FirestoreDeletionConfigV1` is exact `{schemaVersion:1,name,etag,pointInTimeRecoveryEnablement,versionRetentionPeriod}` for literal `projects/peezy-1ecrdl/databases/(default)`. It is derived only from `const tuple = await new Firestore.v1.FirestoreAdminClient().getDatabase({name})` under pinned Firestore 7.11.6/google-gax 4.6.1. Require `tuple` Array length exactly three, destructure `[database,next,raw]`, require `next === null && raw === null`, and take authority only from the fully validated exact `IDatabase` response; a two/four-member tuple or nonnull second/third member rejects. The public fields above are type-normalized without inventing defaults. Pre-ship acceptance requires `POINT_IN_TIME_RECOVERY_DISABLED`, exact version-retention duration `{seconds:"3600",nanos:0}`, and a frozen digest of the complete exact projection. Runtime separately accepts a canonical concrete `earliestVersionTime` from every fresh `getDatabase` observation; it is not part of the stable digest.

`FirestoreDeletionDestinationV1` is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"backup_schedule"|"backup"|"restore"|"clone"|"export"|"change_stream"|"trigger"|"bigquery_copy",resourceName,governingProject,location,identity,filter,retentionSeconds,deletionAuthority,cutoff,postCutoffMatchCount,evidenceSHA256}` with the same exact per-string, per-entry, 4,096-entry, 67,108,864-byte, ordering, uniqueness, and evidence rules as Storage. Public Admin list operations plus Cloud Asset/IAM/Logging evidence enumerate every schedule, backup, restore/clone/export operation and destination, change-stream/trigger transport, extension, and BigQuery copy. Pre-ship requires the accepted array empty after deletion/expiry, no backup schedule, and the copy-producer deny below; its fixed audit alert detects attempted use or policy drift but is not prevention authority. An inaccessible, unbounded, unlisted, over-cap, or synthetic result blocks release. No owner/counsel disaster-recovery exception is invented. The only retained provider version window is the observed one-hour built-in window, and `DATA_DELETED` is forbidden until a fresh accepted observation has `earliestVersionTime` strictly later than the UID's frozen `firestoreCleanupAt`.

Cloud Audit Logs are a separate exact destination inventory, not covered by application log redaction. The accepted org/folder/project policy enumerates Firestore/Datastore, Cloud Storage, Identity Toolkit/Firebase Auth, Cloud Run/Functions, Eventarc, Pub/Sub, Firebase Messaging/Installations/Analytics/Crashlytics, and every other invoked service. `DATA_READ` and `DATA_WRITE` are disabled wherever provider logs can contain UID, object/document path, token, payload, or user value, with exact inherited/local policy, etag, principal set, and drift alert. `_Required` Admin Activity/System Event logs are inventoried separately. Any unavoidable UID-bearing provider log requires owner-approved, counsel-supported finite-retention exception and corresponding narrowing of the deletion promise; because this manifest adopts no such exception, its presence blocks release. A post-cutoff synthetic deletion restores the full destination graph, queries every bucket/sink/copy through its complete retention horizon, and requires zero unexpected match.

`AuthDeletionDestinationV1` is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"auth_user"|"tenant_export"|"backup"|"abuse_risk"|"support_copy"|"audit_log",resourceName,projectId,tenantId,identity,retentionSeconds,deletionAuthority,cutoff,postCutoffMatchCount,evidenceSHA256}` with the same exact per-string, per-entry, 4,096-entry, 67,108,864-byte, ordering, uniqueness, and evidence rules as Storage. Project is exactly `peezy-1ecrdl`; absent tenant is represented by literal empty `tenantId`, never by omitted/null. The accepted sorted unique array binds the deployed service-account and callable revision to the same project/tenant and inventories live Authentication users, imports/exports/backups, abuse/risk and support copies, and the deletion API's documented residual-retention behavior. Each legacy or historical-deleted UID requires provider-native deletion/absence plus zero match after the complete finite retention horizon. Inaccessible, identity-unbound, unlisted, over-cap, or unbounded state blocks release; this manifest invents no Auth retention exception.

`CloudAuditDeletionDestinationV1` is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"log_bucket"|"sink"|"copy"|"error_reporting",resourceName,governingResource,identity,inclusionFilter,exclusionFilter,accessResult:"accessible",retentionSeconds,deletionAuthority,cutoff,lastLegacyPIIAt,expiryOrDeletionAt,postCutoffMatchCount,evidenceSHA256}`. `ProviderCopyDestinationV1` is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"gmail"|"twilio"|"fcm"|"anthropic"|"analytics"|"crashlytics"|"firebase_installations"|"apns"|"bigquery"|"support_export",resourceName,governingAccount,identity,filter,retentionSeconds,deletionAuthority,cutoff,expiryOrDeletionAt,postCutoffMatchCount,evidenceSHA256}`. They use the same exact scalar, entry, array, ordering, uniqueness, and over-cap rules above; optionality is not implied—every displayed member is required, and a known-not-applicable time is the literal empty String rather than omitted/null. Their arrays are sorted by `(kind,resourceName)` under unsigned UTF-8.

`DeletionCopyProducerDenyV1` is the complete unknown-rejecting map `{schemaVersion:1,generationId,projectId,databaseId,bucketName,producerEntries,policyChecks,evidenceSHA256}`. `generationId` is a lowercase RFC-4122 UUID; identities are the three literals used throughout this section. Each `producerEntries[]` member is exact `{ordinal,domain:"storage"|"firestore"|"auth"|"audit"|"gmail"|"twilio"|"fcm"|"anthropic"|"analytics"|"crashlytics",producerId,resourcePattern,permission,reachablePrincipals,controlKind:"iam_deny"|"org_policy"|"provider_disable"|"credential_revocation"|"application_fence",controlResource,controlEtag,controlSHA256}`. Ordinals are contiguous from zero; strings use the bounds above; `reachablePrincipals` is a sorted unique array of at most 128 nonblank Strings; each entry is at most 32,768 canonical bytes; the array has at most 512 entries and 4,194,304 canonical bytes. It enumerates every principal×permission route that can create, enable, update, run, export, clone, restore, replicate, transfer, log, notify, back up, or support-copy a Storage, Firestore, Auth, audit/log, or external-provider destination. Every entry must prove prevention—not later detection—by a live control whose complete current policy bytes and etag are hashed. There is no reachable unlisted creator permission.

`policyChecks[]` is the runtime subset of that deny and has exactly 1...12 contiguous-ordinal exact members `{ordinal,domain,resourceName,adapterId,resourceURL,etagSource,expectedEtag,expectedPolicySHA256}`. `domain` is the producer-entry union; `adapterId` is exactly `google_json_get_v1|google_iam_get_policy_v1`; `resourceURL` is one nonblank canonical HTTPS URL of at most 4,096 bytes whose host is exactly `storage.googleapis.com|firestore.googleapis.com|cloudresourcemanager.googleapis.com|orgpolicy.googleapis.com|identitytoolkit.googleapis.com|firebaserules.googleapis.com`, with no userinfo, fragment, redirect, or nondefault port; `etagSource` is exactly `header|body.etag|body.policy.etag|none`; `expectedEtag` is empty iff source is none and otherwise a nonblank String of at most 1,024 bytes. The subset is accepted only if the signed evidence bundle proves that observing these exact policy/config roots detects every change to any `producerEntries` prevention control. A control without such a bounded readable root cannot enter the accepted deny and blocks release. A fixed alert is supplemental and detects an attempted denied operation or drift; it never substitutes for the deny. Break-glass policy change first revokes `deleteAccount`, disables both deletion reconcilers, proves zero running execution and drains work, changes policy, drains every newly reachable destination to zero, accepts a new evidence generation in a reviewed build, then re-enables in that order.

`PROVIDER_POLICY_CHECK_ADAPTERS_V1` is a literal two-row registry and the production dispatch is a closed switch over exactly these IDs. Both rows use Node 22 `https` plus pinned transitive `google-auth-library` 9.15.1 `GoogleAuth({scopes:["https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform.read-only"]})`, obtain headers for the exact URL, send only those authorization headers plus `accept: application/json`, follow zero redirects, retry zero times, impose a 3,000-ms socket/request deadline, cap the raw response at 1,048,576 bytes, require status 200 and JSON media type, run a streaming duplicate-key/Unicode validator before parsing, require a complete own-property plain-object graph, and set `expectedPolicySHA256 = SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1(completeResponseObject))`. `google_json_get_v1` sends exact GET with no body. `google_iam_get_policy_v1` sends exact POST with content type JSON and byte-exact body `{"options":{"requestedPolicyVersion":3}}`. Etag extraction follows only the selected closed `etagSource`; header lookup is lowercase `etag`, and either body path requires exact own String members. Header/body surplus remains inside the selected complete response-object hash where applicable; SDK/provider error text is never logged. Static evidence requires registry IDs == switch cases == independent-oracle cases and fails any dynamic method name, host, body, retry, redirect, or third adapter. Fixtures cover both rows, all four etag sources, malformed/duplicate/oversize JSON, status/media/redirect/auth/timeout, response-field drift, 1/12/13 checks, and registry/switch/oracle disagreement.

The sole active runtime authority is immutable build input `functions/accountDeletionProviderEvidenceV1.json`, not mutable Firestore prose. `ProviderEvidenceAuthorityV1` is exact `{schemaVersion:1,kind:"ACCOUNT_DELETION_PROVIDER_EVIDENCE",generationId,projectId,databaseId,bucketName,region,implementationSHA256,packageLockSHA256,bucketConfig,bucketConfigSHA256,firestoreConfig,firestoreConfigSHA256,storageDestinations,firestoreDestinations,authDestinations,cloudAuditDestinations,providerCopyDestinations,copyProducerDenySHA256,policyChecks,signatureAlgorithm:"ed25519",externalEvidenceBundleSHA256,externalEvidencePublicKeyBase64URL,externalEvidenceSigningKeySHA256,signedAuthorityPayloadSHA256,externalEvidenceSignatureBase64URL,activatedAt,authoritySHA256}`. The five destination members are exact `AcceptedArrayDigestV1 {count,canonicalBytes,sha256}` with safe count `0...4096`, safe bytes `0...67108864`, and exact digest; `policyChecks` is byte-equal to the deny subset; `bucketConfig` and `firestoreConfig` are the complete accepted maps above. Ordinary strings are at most 4,096 UTF-8 bytes, the public key is exact unpadded base64url for 32 bytes, the signature is exact unpadded base64url for 64 bytes, `activatedAt` is a canonical UTC instant, all digests are 64 lowercase hex, and the complete artifact is at most 131,072 `TaskCanonicalV1` bytes. `externalEvidenceSigningKeySHA256` is SHA-256 of the decoded 32 key bytes and must equal the literal trust-anchor digest compiled into both the sealer and `accountDeletionFence.js`. `signedAuthorityPayloadSHA256` is lowercase SHA-256 of `TaskCanonicalV1` over the complete authority map omitting exactly `signedAuthorityPayloadSHA256`, `externalEvidenceSignatureBase64URL`, and `authoritySHA256`. The signed message is byte-exact `ASCII("peezy.account_deletion_provider_evidence.v1\\0") || hexDecode(signedAuthorityPayloadSHA256) || hexDecode(externalEvidenceBundleSHA256) || hexDecode(implementationSHA256)`; no other serialization enters it. Runtime recomputes that payload digest before signature verification. `authoritySHA256` hashes the complete map with only that member omitted. Missing, unknown, surplus, duplicate-key, wrong-order, over-cap, digest/count/identity/key/algorithm/payload mismatch, invalid Ed25519 signature, or mismatch to the signed external bundle blocks deletion/reconciler activation; changing any authority member while retaining the old signature is rejected.

The immediately preceding exact-map display is superseded only in this one mechanical respect: insert required members `firestoreRulesSHA256,storageRulesSHA256,firestoreIndexesSHA256,firestoreRulesetId,firestoreReleaseId,storageRulesetId,storageReleaseId` immediately after `packageLockSHA256`. The three hashes cover raw accepted repository bytes; the four nonblank IDs name the exact deployed rulesets/releases and are at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes each. They are inside `signedAuthorityPayloadSHA256`. Fresh bounded activation/finalize checks require those deployed release IDs and content hashes; rules/index release mutation permissions are producer entries in `DeletionCopyProducerDenyV1`. No authority lacking any of the seven members is V1.

`implementationSHA256` is nonrecursive: it is lowercase SHA-256 of `TaskCanonicalV1({domain:"account_deletion_provider_implementation.v1",files})`, where `files[]` is sorted by repo-relative path under unsigned UTF-8 and each exact member is `{path,sha256}` over raw repository bytes with no line-ending or Unicode normalization. The literal path set is `functions/accountDeletionFence.js`, `functions/dispositionTriggers.js`, `functions/entitlement.js`, `functions/getWorkflowQualifying.js`, `functions/index.js`, `functions/notificationIntents.js`, `functions/notifySupport.js`, `functions/packageInventory.js`, `functions/package-lock.json`, `functions/package.json`, `functions/peezyChat.js`, `functions/processInventory.js`, `functions/researchTask.js`, `functions/resolveProvider.js`, `functions/scripts/sealAccountDeletionProviderEvidence.js`, `functions/spawnTasks.js`, `functions/submitCheckIn.js`, `functions/submitCheckInCore.js`, `functions/supportAdmin.js`, `functions/taskDisposition.js`, `functions/taskPlan.js`, and `functions/validateSubscription.js`. The authority JSON is deliberately excluded, so the digest is constructible before it exists. External deployment evidence—not a self-referential artifact member—binds the accepted `implementationSHA256` plus `authoritySHA256` to the actual Build-B revision and image digest.

The literal implementation path set in the preceding paragraph additionally and mandatorily includes `functions/scripts/purgeLegacyDeletedAccounts.js`, `functions/scripts/purgeLegacyResolvedProviders.js`, `firestore.indexes.json`, `firestore.rules`, and `storage.rules`, in ordinary unsigned-path sort order. This addition supersedes the shorter set; no other path is implied and `functions/accountDeletionProviderEvidenceV1.json` remains the sole exclusion. Raw-byte mutation of any of the five added paths changes `implementationSHA256`; the separately displayed rules/index hashes provide the deployed-release binding rather than removing them from the implementation digest.

New import-safe `functions/scripts/sealAccountDeletionProviderEvidence.js` is the only artifact producer. Before Build A, the owner creates one offline Ed25519 key, keeps the private key outside the repository, and supplies the exact 32-byte public key; its base64url bytes and SHA-256 are inserted as literal reviewed constants in the sealer and `accountDeletionFence.js`, with no placeholder. Build A contains the complete code and those constants but no authority JSON: unrelated exports load normally, while only `deleteAccount`, both deletion reconcilers, and the historical apply path return fixed `PROVIDER_EVIDENCE_NOT_ACTIVATED` before a provider or Firestore mutation; none is enabled. Under separate operational authorization, evidence is gathered against Build A's nonrecursive implementation digest and signed. The sealer's exact once-only mode validates the complete signed evidence bundle (at most 67,108,864 bytes), the five complete destination arrays, `DeletionCopyProducerDenyV1`, current bucket/Firestore projections, project/database/bucket/region, implementation and package-lock digests, then creates the absent JSON path without overwrite and read-verifies canonical bytes/hash/signature. A reviewed Build B adds only that immutable artifact relative to the frozen implementation path set; no placeholder build may initialize/enable a reconciler or restore deletion. A replacement generation requires a new signed bundle and ordinary source-control replacement under the break-glass order; no deployed function, runtime identity, migration, or repair path can create/update/delete the artifact. Static module-graph tests prove the artifact is imported only by `accountDeletionFence.js`, is packaged byte-for-byte in Build B, and has no writer in the deployed graph.

At every reconciler acquisition and every finalize, Build B validates the loaded artifact and performs only this bounded fresh fence: the one bucket-config RPC, the one Firestore-config RPC, and all `policyChecks` RPCs. There are at most 14 provider observations, admitted in ordinal order at concurrency four with a 3,000-ms deadline each; exact current maps/etags/digests must match the artifact. Thus provider observation has four waves and a 12-second hard local deadline. Finalize separately permits at most one 10-second root/work/lease transaction and one 10-second Auth deletion call, for 32 seconds of admitted awaits inside the pinned 60-second callable; no new await begins after elapsed 42 seconds. A reconciler uses the same observation bound inside 270 seconds. The complete destination arrays are not re-enumerated on that hot path: their signed digests remain authoritative precisely while the copy-producer deny roots match. Build A absence returns only `PROVIDER_EVIDENCE_NOT_ACTIVATED`; Build B missing/malformed artifact, observation timeout, +1 check, config/policy/key/signature drift, or generation disagreement is fixed `ACCOUNT_DELETION_PROVIDER_EVIDENCE_INVARIANT`, writes no guard/final state, never calls Auth delete, and returns/retains `DELETION_RETRY_REQUIRED`. Reconciler work/root mutations and finalize fence the same `generationId` and `authoritySHA256`; migration checkpoints bind them. Fixtures cover exact and every field/count/byte cap +1, Build-A absent behavior with unrelated exports live and zero mutation, Build-B absent failure, seal overwrite refusal, algorithm/key/domain/message/bundle/signature/hash mismatch, package/implementation drift, 1/12/13 policy checks, four-wave deadline, policy/config creation immediately before and after the final read, lease takeover during observations, generation replacement, break-glass ordering, Build-A→Build-B binding, and zero runtime authority writer.

### 11.3 Durable Storage/Firestore guard and scheduled reconciler

The following branch grammar replaces every earlier exact `accountDeletion` root-map sentence in §11. All branches retain `schemaVersion:1`, the exact canonical `capabilities` array, and concrete server Timestamp `startedAt`. Marker creation also requires `storageGuardAfter == startedAt + 604800 seconds`. Exact DELETING-sweeping is `{schemaVersion:1,state:"DELETING",capabilities,startedAt,storageGuardAfter}`. After two complete consecutive empty application sweeps, one transaction replaces it with exact DELETING-guarding `{schemaVersion:1,state:"DELETING",capabilities,startedAt,storageGuardAfter,firestoreCleanupAt}` where `firestoreCleanupAt` is that transaction's server read time. Guarding may add `storageGuardCompletedAt`, `firestoreVersionGuardCompletedAt`, or both; no other optional field is legal, each completion is a concrete server Timestamp, `storageGuardCompletedAt` must be strictly later than `storageGuardAfter`, and `firestoreVersionGuardCompletedAt` must be at or after `firestoreCleanupAt`. Exact DATA_DELETED is `{schemaVersion:1,state:"DATA_DELETED",capabilities,startedAt,storageGuardAfter,firestoreCleanupAt,storageGuardCompletedAt,firestoreVersionGuardCompletedAt,dataDeletedAt}` with `dataDeletedAt` sampled by the final guard transaction and not earlier than either completion. Unknown, missing, surplus, wrong-phase, or time-order content is malformed. Capability enrollment preserves every guard field byte-for-byte.

Marker creation atomically creates server-only `accountDeletionStorageWork/{workId}`, where `workId = "adsw1_" + first40(lowercase SHA-256(TaskCanonicalV1({account_uid:uid})))`. The exact row is `{schema_version:1,kind:"ACCOUNT_DELETION_STORAGE_WORK",work_id,account_uid,marker_started_at,storage_guard_after,failure_count,next_eligible_run,created_at,updated_at}`; IDs/UID/times must agree with the root, `failure_count` is a safe integer 0...8, `next_eligible_run` is a safe nonnegative integer, complete canonical bytes are at most 2,048, and client access is denied. It exists from marker creation until the transaction that enters DATA_DELETED; absence, duplication, malformed identity, or disagreement while DELETING is an invariant that blocks progress. The marker/work creation transaction sets `marker_started_at = startedAt`, `storage_guard_after = storageGuardAfter`, `failure_count = 0`, `next_eligible_run = 0`, and `created_at = updated_at = startedAt`; replay requires complete byte equality. Every later work mutation preserves kind/ID/UID/marker/guard/created fields and sets `updated_at` to that fenced transaction's server read time with `updated_at >= created_at`. A DATA_DELETED tombstone requires it absent.

New export `reconcileAccountDeletionStorage` is exact `onSchedule({schedule:"*/5 * * * *",timeZone:"UTC",region:"us-central1",timeoutSeconds:270,memory:"512MiB",maxInstances:1,retryCount:0}, handler)`. Its sole state document is `phase2System/accountDeletionStorageReconcilerV1`, exact `{schema_version:1,kind:"ACCOUNT_DELETION_STORAGE_RECONCILER",last_started_ordinal,last_completed_ordinal,cursor_id,lease,heartbeat}`, where cursor is literal empty String or a valid work document ID; lease is null or exact `{owner_token,schedule_ordinal,scheduled_at,started_at,expires_at}`; heartbeat is null or exact `{schedule_ordinal,status:"running"|"completed",started_at,completed_at?}`. UUID/token, concrete Timestamp, exact-key, safe-ordinal, order, and time relations are exhaustively validated. The handler parses exact `event.scheduleTime`, requires a canonical UTC instant on a 300-second boundary, and derives safe `scheduleOrdinal = floor(epochSeconds / 300)`. Each acquired run queries only `accountDeletionStorageWork`, ordered by document ID ascending, `startAfter(cursor_id)` when nonempty, `limit(50)`, with no field filter and therefore no composite index. When an eligible row is selected, cursor advances only to that row and the next accepted run continues after it even if the fetched page was shorter than 50; rows later in that page are never skipped. Only a page with no eligible row advances to its last row. A subsequent empty page with nonempty cursor writes empty cursor and completes, while an empty page with empty cursor completes. No completion-time guarantee is promised; H57 measures backlog, oldest age, guard overdue, failure saturation, wraps, and invariant count.

The selected-row reducer rereads exact work and root before and after every provider await. Missing/drifted/root mismatch performs no mutation. A valid DELETING marker first reuses §11.2's accepted bucket map/digest and destination evidence, then in fixed order examines `inventory/{uid}/` versions, its soft-deleted probe, `users/{uid}/` versions, and its soft-deleted probe. On provider/tuple/delete/transient failure, a transaction that still matches the selected work/root sets `failure_count = min(old + 1,8)` and `next_eligible_run = scheduleOrdinal + min(2^failure_count,16)`, using the new stored count in the exponent; a saturated row remains at eight and moves by 16 on each failure. #### Auth retained-copy guard

`DATA_DELETED` is data-final but not account-final; this paragraph supersedes every earlier sentence that lets a finalize response, canonical Auth user-not-found, or client presentation treat it as completed. Percent mode substitutes RFC-3986 percent-encoded UID; hash mode substitutes lowercase SHA-256 of `TaskCanonicalV1({account_uid:uid})`. POST content type is JSON; GET requires empty body. The response uses the same 3-second/no-retry/1,048,576-byte raw JSON discipline as `PROVIDER_POLICY_CHECK_ADAPTERS_V1`, and requires the selected sole top-level safe-integer count be zero. Candidate upsert is deterministic and idempotent; each page is checkpointed only after all extracted UID upserts settle. Raw UID, object name, document path, token, or provider bytes never enter stdout/log evidence; output is only fixed codes, bounded counts, source ordinals, and candidate/path digests.

Reduction and waiting-guards are starvation-safe cursor sweeps. Query `accountDeletionLegacyCandidates` only by document ID ascending, `startAfter(reduction_cursor_id)` iff nonempty, and `limit(1)`. Before any Auth, provider, cleanup, or guard await, one checkpoint transaction rereads the candidate and advances `reduction_cursor_id` to that exact ID; a crash therefore skips it only until the next wrap. Empty-after-nonempty atomically clears the cursor and increments `reduction_round`; empty-at-empty completes that sweep. A malformed candidate or checkpoint disagreement is the migration invariant and never advances past unknown authority. No candidate can monopolize a sweep.

For pending or excluded-live, first require one exact Firebase Auth observation and one exact `users/{uid}` root read. A present nondeletion root writes/refreshes exact `excluded_live/ROOT_PRESENT`; otherwise Auth present writes/refreshes exact `excluded_live/AUTH_PRESENT`. Ambiguous/transport Auth stays exact pending/ambiguous and increments the sweep's deferred count. Canonical `auth/user-not-found` plus root absence is adoptable. Under the proved Auth-mutation freeze, a second user-not-found check immediately precedes one transaction that rechecks root absence and creates the exact DELETING-sweeping marker, one server-generated migration-only `adel1_` RFC-4122 lowercase UUID capability with a fresh 32-byte random proof hash, the exact Storage work row, and atomically replaces the candidate with its adopted branch carrying that operation/hash/marker time. Relaunch drives a marker only when all three adopted identity members match byte-for-byte; pending-plus-marker, adopted-plus-absent/mismatching marker, or foreign capability is an invariant and performs no cleanup. A third Auth check follows marker creation; anything other than canonical user-not-found is `LEGACY_ACCOUNT_AUTH_RACE`, stops all apply work, leaves the marker/candidate for investigation, and never calls Auth deletion.

The reducing sweep drives each adopted candidate through the bounded Firestore/external/global/two-empty-sweep core until `firestoreCleanupAt` is durable; the scheduled reconcilers alone close Storage, Firestore-version, and Auth retained-copy guards. When a complete reducing sweep has zero pending/ambiguous candidate and every adopted candidate has `firestoreCleanupAt`, transition to `waiting_guards` with empty reduction cursor. Waiting-guards uses the identical advance-before-await cursor/wrap rule. Exact ACCOUNT_DELETED triggers a fresh run of all 23 UID-specific adapters plus the already-required global destination-zero proofs and deletes that adopted candidate only after zero residue; any earlier root state retains it. A candidate whose exact ACCOUNT_DELETED tombstone already exists is replayed through the same residual proof. The migration never calls Firebase Auth delete and creates no local intent, completion presentation, provider disconnect, or client purge file.

When a complete waiting-guards sweep has no adopted or pending candidate, enter confirming with `confirmation_zero_passes = 0`. Each confirmation pass starts row 0 at `{kind:"start"}`, revalidates every retained excluded-live candidate against fresh Auth/root authority, and requires zero new candidate, zero pending/ambiguous candidate, zero adopted candidate, and every excluded-live reason still true. Any failure returns to reducing with both cursors reset and confirmation count zero. One qualifying pass sets count one; a second fresh qualifying pass sets count two. Insertion between passes is caught by the second pass. Only then may the signed evidence digest record both pass digests; the permanent ACCOUNT_DELETED tombstones remain, and one final transaction deletes only the still-matching excluded-live candidates plus the checkpoint. Malformed/inaccessible source or provider evidence blocks completion.

CLI apply is authorized only by the exact once-only set `--apply --project-id peezy-1ecrdl --confirm-project peezy-1ecrdl --database '(default)' --bucket peezy-1ecrdl.firebasestorage.app --drain-evidence-sha256 <64 lowercase hex> --bucket-config-sha256 <accepted digest> --firestore-config-sha256 <accepted digest> --auth-freeze-evidence-sha256 <64 lowercase hex>`. Audit mode accepts only the four project/database/bucket identity flags and optional exact `--page-size 1...100`; unknown, duplicate, missing, malformed, emulator, credential-project, database, bucket, config/evidence, or package-lock drift exits nonzero before a write. Pure core is import-safe and CLI runs only under `require.main === module`.

Fixtures recreate the old callable's inventory-only orphan, swallowed `users/` Storage error, each omitted external/global family, each accepted provider-copy pointer, Auth-present/root-absent preservation, Auth-not-found/root-absent adoption, Auth ambiguity, all three Auth checks, root race, random failure, marker/work transaction loss, every sweep/guard/tombstone crash, provider/config drift, candidate collision, bounded paging/apply-cursor replay, audit interruption restarting at source zero with byte-exact zero server writes, insertion between zero passes, restart with each identity/evidence/implementation/source-registry/package/lock digest changed, strict audit/apply argv, emulator rejection, redacted output, and proof that Auth delete/local presentation/provider sends have zero reachable call sites.

Start from the frozen §7.4 parsed object whose normalized serialization is exactly 4,432 UTF-8 bytes/SHA-256 `a7a432ec8e0511176b890432c5e4cb4a07e59a6dba0c9d920948cc446f3a7bbb`. Preserve every existing entry and its order. Append only these exact objects:

```text
indexes[3] = {

fieldOverrides[28] = {"collectionGroup":"eventArchiveChunks","fieldPath":"payload","indexes":[]}
```

The displayed compact member spelling is explanatory; the file is serialized only as `JSON.stringify(value,null,2) + "\n"`. The exact result has 4 `indexes`, 35 `fieldOverrides`, 5,872 UTF-8 bytes, one trailing LF, and SHA-256 `a6de8daf701a75ff3db025ca244dbf2218432c5c1a06b0992cd88358250d88e8`. Firestore document-name order remains the composite's implicit final tie break, and no scheduler-refusal wildcard exemption is added. The two workflow overrides are the sole re-enabled fields from that collection group's existing wildcard exemption and exist only for §11 deletion queries; the migration and outbound-lease wildcard exemptions prevent accidental indexing of server-only authority. Production and oracle first require the frozen base tuple, apply these eight appends in memory, and require the exact result tuple; every write-budget index delta is refrozen against this final registry, and any different base or output stops rather than rebasing silently.

Rules explicitly deny owner/other/anonymous get/list/create/update/delete for every document under `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/quarantinedEvents/{quarantineId}` (including exact `qev1_`, `qevu1_`, and `qev2_` shapes), literal `phase1System/dispositionTriggerState/migrationLeases/legacyOversizeMigrationV1`, every Phase-2b condition slot, and recursively `/users/{uid}/eventArchive/{document=**}`. They likewise deny every `schedulerRefusals` document; recursively deny every owner/other/anonymous get/list/create/update/delete on `/users/{uid}/legacyResetMigrations/{document=**}` and `/users/{uid}/outboundLeases/{document=**}`; and continue denying canonical reset records/tombstones, attempted alias paths, and any fake old-location `LEGACY_RESET_MIGRATION` in `taskPlanOperations`. `taskPlanOperations.kind` remains its sole child re-enable and no longer includes migration authority. Owner task reads remain allowed and necessarily return raw `foreignRoots` and `evidence_reservation`; Rules deny owner add/change/remove of either member and deny every other user's task access.

Rules also deny owner/other/anonymous get/list/create/update/delete for literal `phase2System/accountDeletionStorageReconcilerV1`, literal `phase2System/accountDeletionAuthReconcilerV1`, literal `phase2System/accountDeletionLegacyMigrationV1`, every `accountDeletionStorageWork/{workId}`, every `accountDeletionAuthWork/{workId}`, and every `accountDeletionLegacyCandidates/{candidateId}`. These collections are queried only by document ID, so the exact §7.4 index tuple remains byte-identical. Missing/surplus/wrong-type/server-sentinel/path/cross-UID maps deny within the existing one-root-access budget.

For assessment, knowledge, and root daily dose, Rules implement §5's generation invariant as disjoint branches. Every fresh assessment/knowledge/dose create carries `task_generation_epoch` equal the safe effective root epoch while accountDeletion and taskReset are absent. A stamped update requires that same current epoch and preserves the stamp byte-for-byte. One existing-and-prospective unstamped assessment update is allowed only at effective root epoch 0 with reset absent and must preserve stamp absence. One unstamped `userKnowledge` document may add exactly stamp 0 only in its root-epoch-zero merge transaction. One unstamped root `dailyDose` may be replaced by the complete stamped epoch-zero schema only at root epoch 0 with reset absent. Every other missing, malformed, stale, add, change, or removal case denies.

Cleanup assessment/knowledge delete separately requires §5's complete current `awaiting_local_reset` marker and stored epoch below marker result epoch, or exact unstamped 0→1. Root dose cleanup requires dailyDose be the sole changed key, the same complete marker be preserved byte-for-byte, and the same older/bridge predicate. Rules do not claim caller operation identity; the client transaction supplies that stronger comparison. Account-deletion owner root mutation remains forbidden; every descendant plus userKnowledge write requires marker absence; task access-call budgets remain 2/write and 16/20 for eight, and every other single write remains within 10 after its one root access. Storage creates/updates retain owner/existing-root/no-marker requirements. Tests cover every client operation on `legacyResetMigrations`, `outboundLeases`, all three literal phase2System state documents, `accountDeletionStorageWork`, `accountDeletionAuthWork`, and `accountDeletionLegacyCandidates`; fresh stamped creation; stamped current/stale/changed/removed updates; the exact unstamped assessment preserve, knowledge add-zero, and dose replace-zero branches; every forbidden cross-branch; cleanup generation/marker/time/bridge/current/newer/malformed cases; DELETING/DATA_DELETED/AUTH_GUARDING/ACCOUNT_DELETED; equal/+1 access budgets; late Storage; and other/anonymous denial. Any registry/order/scope change forces new derivation; update production/oracle together.

### 12.5 D32 — exact no-index normalizer

Every new-file `git diff --no-index` first requires the literal manifest path to be an existing regular readable non-symlink. The caller sets `umask 077` before `mktemp -d`, requires that directory to remain owner-controlled mode 0700, and pre-creates two owner-controlled mode-0600, link-count-one capture files. Input/stdout/stderr must have pairwise-distinct device+inode identities before either capture is opened. The opened descriptors remain pinned through all post-run checks; a redirection failure is never normalized as diff status 1.

```sh
phase2_no_index_diff() {
  phase2_diff_mode=$1
  phase2_diff_path=$2
  phase2_diff_stdout=$3
  phase2_diff_stderr=$4
  phase2_diff_capture_dir=$5

  test -f "$phase2_diff_path" &&
  test ! -L "$phase2_diff_path" &&
  test -r "$phase2_diff_path" || return 72

  { test "$phase2_diff_mode" = check || test "$phase2_diff_mode" = patch; } || return 64
  test -d "$phase2_diff_capture_dir" &&
  test ! -L "$phase2_diff_capture_dir" &&
  test -O "$phase2_diff_capture_dir" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%Lp' "$phase2_diff_capture_dir")" = 700 &&
  test -r "$phase2_diff_capture_dir" &&
  test -w "$phase2_diff_capture_dir" &&
  test -x "$phase2_diff_capture_dir" || return 75
  test "$phase2_diff_stdout" = "$phase2_diff_capture_dir/stdout" &&
  test "$phase2_diff_stderr" = "$phase2_diff_capture_dir/stderr" &&
  test "$phase2_diff_stdout" != "$phase2_diff_stderr" &&
  test "$phase2_diff_path" != "$phase2_diff_stdout" &&
  test "$phase2_diff_path" != "$phase2_diff_stderr" || return 75
  test -f "$phase2_diff_stdout" && test ! -L "$phase2_diff_stdout" &&
  test -O "$phase2_diff_stdout" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%l:%Lp' "$phase2_diff_stdout")" = 1:600 &&
  test -r "$phase2_diff_stdout" && test -w "$phase2_diff_stdout" &&
  test ! -s "$phase2_diff_stdout" || return 75
  test -f "$phase2_diff_stderr" && test ! -L "$phase2_diff_stderr" &&
  test -O "$phase2_diff_stderr" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%l:%Lp' "$phase2_diff_stderr")" = 1:600 &&
  test -r "$phase2_diff_stderr" && test -w "$phase2_diff_stderr" &&
  test ! -s "$phase2_diff_stderr" || return 75

  phase2_diff_input_id=$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%d:%i' "$phase2_diff_path") || return 75
  phase2_diff_stdout_id=$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%d:%i' "$phase2_diff_stdout") || return 75
  phase2_diff_stderr_id=$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%d:%i' "$phase2_diff_stderr") || return 75
  phase2_diff_dir_id=$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%d:%i' "$phase2_diff_capture_dir") || return 75
  test "$phase2_diff_input_id" != "$phase2_diff_stdout_id" &&
  test "$phase2_diff_input_id" != "$phase2_diff_stderr_id" &&
  test "$phase2_diff_stdout_id" != "$phase2_diff_stderr_id" || return 75

  exec 3>"$phase2_diff_stdout" || return 75
  exec 4>"$phase2_diff_stderr" || { exec 3>&-; return 75; }
  test "$phase2_diff_stdout" -ef /dev/fd/3 &&
  test "$phase2_diff_stderr" -ef /dev/fd/4 || {
    exec 3>&-
    exec 4>&-
    return 75
  }

  if test "$phase2_diff_mode" = check; then
    if git diff --no-index --check /dev/null "$phase2_diff_path" \
      >&3 2>&4; then
      phase2_diff_rc=0
    else
      phase2_diff_rc=$?
    fi
  else
    if git diff --binary --no-index /dev/null "$phase2_diff_path" \
      >&3 2>&4; then
      phase2_diff_rc=0
    else
      phase2_diff_rc=$?
    fi
  fi

  test -f "$phase2_diff_path" && test ! -L "$phase2_diff_path" &&
  test -r "$phase2_diff_path" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%d:%i' "$phase2_diff_path")" = "$phase2_diff_input_id" || {
    exec 3>&-
    exec 4>&-
    return 75
  }
  test -f "$phase2_diff_stdout" && test ! -L "$phase2_diff_stdout" &&
  test -O "$phase2_diff_stdout" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%l:%Lp' "$phase2_diff_stdout")" = 1:600 &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%d:%i' "$phase2_diff_stdout")" = "$phase2_diff_stdout_id" &&
  test "$phase2_diff_stdout" -ef /dev/fd/3 &&
  test -r "$phase2_diff_stdout" || {
    exec 3>&-
    exec 4>&-
    return 75
  }
  test -f "$phase2_diff_stderr" && test ! -L "$phase2_diff_stderr" &&
  test -O "$phase2_diff_stderr" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%l:%Lp' "$phase2_diff_stderr")" = 1:600 &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%d:%i' "$phase2_diff_stderr")" = "$phase2_diff_stderr_id" &&
  test "$phase2_diff_stderr" -ef /dev/fd/4 &&
  test -r "$phase2_diff_stderr" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%d:%i' "$phase2_diff_capture_dir")" = "$phase2_diff_dir_id" &&
  test "$(/usr/bin/stat -f '%Lp' "$phase2_diff_capture_dir")" = 700 || {
    exec 3>&-
    exec 4>&-
    return 75
  }
  exec 3>&-
  exec 4>&-
  { test "$phase2_diff_rc" -eq 0 || test "$phase2_diff_rc" -eq 1; } || return "$phase2_diff_rc"
  test ! -s "$phase2_diff_stderr" || return 73
  if test "$phase2_diff_mode" = check; then
    test ! -s "$phase2_diff_stdout" || return 74
  fi
}
```

The caller uses `umask 077`, creates the private capture directory with `mktemp -d`, verifies owner/mode 0700, pre-creates its literal empty mode-0600 `stdout` and `stderr` regular files, and passes that directory as argument five; cleanup remains narrow to that validated directory. The 0700 directory is the boundary against path substitution by other principals; same-UID hostile-process replacement is outside this build-script threat model, but every observable replacement before/after the run still fails closed. Patch mode success is status 0/1 + empty stderr; stdout is appended only after every post-run identity/mode check. Check mode additionally requires empty stdout. Missing/unreadable/directory/symlink input, hardlink/identity collision, capture replacement, mode-0755/0777 directory, non-0600 or multi-link capture, missing/nonempty/unwritable capture, FD-open failure, status ≥2, or any stderr is fatal. Tracked `git diff --check -- <literal paths>` is never normalized and requires raw zero plus empty outputs. `git apply --check`, `git cat-file`, and File.txt guards retain raw semantics. Fixtures cover all input path classes, clean/bad new file, patch status1/output, stderr at 0/1, status2, missing/replaced/colliding captures, hardlink-to-input, stdout↔stderr hardlink, 0700/0755/0777 directories, 0600/non-0600 captures, stdout/stderr/input alias attempts, unwritable file/directory, FD-open failure, post-run input/capture symlink/mode/inode replacement, caller errexit initially on and off with its state unchanged, and tracked whitespace error.
